## TRIAGE — 2026-08-13. Read this before the queue.

**The queue holds 134 rows and they all looked equally live. They are not.** Every
row below is classified against ONE question: **does this block an office worker
receiving inventory and sending it out?** Rows not named here are **DORMANT** —
not deleted, not wrong, and not competing for a slot until the inventory path
ships. **A dormant row may be promoted the moment something makes it block; it
must not be worked because it surfaced during an unrelated packet, which is how
the queue reached 134.**

**Why this was needed, measured:** on 2026-08-13, 73 commits landed on `main` and
**13 touched product code**. Two Critical *trunk* defects were found that day — a
write-path control missing from `main`, and a gate that cannot scope containment —
and **neither was on the queue**; both were found by a lane refusing to trust a
stated fact. Meanwhile the rows that would have predicted them sat among 134 that
all read as equally urgent.

#### TIER 1 SPLITS IN TWO, and the reason was measured at `3509ce8` on 2026-08-13

**The triage above, and the 2026-08-13 handoff, both said `relation-scoped-enumeration`
was `PUR-1`'s only remaining blocker. That is WRONG, and the correction is a
prerequisite nobody had priced.**

**`ADOPTED_COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_VERSION` is `COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_V1_VERSION`**
(`packages/compiler/src/compiler.ts:100`), and `targetEntityId` is emitted **only**
under v2 — `relationTargets` at `packages/compiler/src/projections.ts:1100-1103` is
`effectKind === 'createRecordEffect' && relations.length > 0 &&
compilerSemanticProfileVersion === COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_V2_VERSION`. So **no
relation input in the shipped `app.compiled.json` carries a target**, and ADR-0052 §1
makes the target the thing a picker resolves its list from. **A record picker cannot
render against the shipped app until profile v2 is adopted, no matter how well it is
built.** ADR-0052 §5 already says this in passing — *"under the adopted profile no
relation carries a target, so that is the normal case until v2 adoption"* — but it was
read as a note rather than as a scheduling fact.

**So the relation half has TWO blockers, in order:** compiler-semantic profile v2
adoption (a lineage-minting event under ADR-0047 §4, which also carries `U5b`'s
deferred `disclosureTier` obligation), and then `relation-scoped-enumeration`.

**The other half is now accepted.** `form-write-untyped-wire` and
`form-empty-means-nothing` were pure write-path typing, depended on no profile,
and landed together as `form-wire-semantics`. The remaining route starts with
compiler-semantic profile v2 adoption and then `relation-scoped-enumeration`.

**Consequence for the "charter all five as one investigation" recommendation:** the
investigation is still one question — *can the web form write anything that is not a
text box* — but it cannot be one packet, because two of its three answers sit behind
a profile-adoption event and one does not. Chartering them together would have parked
the unblocked half behind the blocked half.

#### TIER 1 GAINS A ROW — `inventory-form-anatomy`, filed 2026-08-15

**`relation-scoped-enumeration` stopped on it, correctly, and it is now `PUR-1`'s
blocker.** Decoded from the shipped release: `component-registry.ts` registers
exactly ONE form content component, `record:sections`. `packages/domain/src/inventory/definition.ts`
authors inventory form anatomy as `['breadcrumb','titleStatus','activity']` while
`packages/domain/src/party/definition.ts` authors the working shape as
`['breadcrumb','titleStatus','commandBar','keyFacts','sections']`. **So every
inventory form renders `UNSUPPORTED_COMPONENT`, has no command bar and no save
control, and refuses at HTTP 422 before any relation logic runs.**

**Measured impact on the inventory path:** of the four form surfaces carrying a
required relation, **three cannot render** — `inventory_transaction_line_form`,
`stock_count_form`, `stock_count_line_form`, all three of which are the
legal-entity-SCOPED specimens. The only one that renders is `party_role_form`,
the single unscoped case. **A picker built today could only ever demonstrate the
easy specimen, which is exactly how both predecessors failed.**

**This is the G2-composition program review's A2 finding, recorded 2026-07-27 and
routed nowhere since.** That review's own standing lesson reads: *"a recorded
finding with no owning row is a disposition with no executing gate — the
composition gap was recorded four times and routed nowhere."* **It has now been
recorded a fifth time, by a lane that could not proceed past it.** This row is
the executing gate.

**Owed:** rule what anatomy an inventory form gets, and whether `activity` stays
declared-but-unregistered or is removed. **Do not assume the answer is "copy
party's five slots"** — the language cannot currently express *declared,
intentionally unregistered*, which the same program review flagged as an
expressiveness gap. Changing authored definitions regenerates release output and
mints a lineage entry, so `check:app-release` and `test:postgres` are required
gates.

**Orchestrator error recorded with it:** the `relation-scoped-enumeration` prompt
named all four required-relation forms as its specimen set. That table was built
by measuring relations, targets and scope exhaustively and never checking whether
the surfaces could render — one half measured, the whole asserted.

#### TWO MORE ROWS, filed 2026-08-17 by the orchestrator from the inventory pilot-readiness audit

**`dev-seed-has-no-inventory` — TIER 1.** Verified by reading, not relayed:
`apps/api/src/main.ts:66` passes `seedProfile: 'distributor'`;
`packages/domain/src/app/seed.ts` emits creates for exactly three entities —
`APPLICATION_IDS.{catalog,location,party}.createOperationId` — and
`grep -c inventory` over that file returns **0**. The only thing in the repository
that produces an inventory draft is `seedInventoryDraft`
(`apps/web/test/browser/composed-application.spec.ts:1813`), which inserts via raw
SQL from the browser suite. **So on `pnpm dev` every inventory list is empty and
the posting route is unreachable; the shipped posting capability cannot be
observed by a human at all.**

**Triage argument, with the case against.** *Against Tier 1:* a dev seed is not
production, and if forms rendered and relations were fillable an operator would
create transactions themselves — making this an observability gap, not a
capability gap. **That argument fails on a measured fact.** `5g3-mount` records
that inventory entities are **structurally non-constructible through the generic
press**: `legal_entity_id` is compiler-derived storage metadata living outside the
entity's canonical `columns`, so a generic create has **no authored field through
which to supply it**, and the database refuses with `23502 null value in column
"legal_entity_id"`. **The operator route does not exist, so the seed is currently
the only way inventory data can exist at all.** Tier 1 while that holds; it drops
to Tier 3 the moment non-constructibility is fixed.

**Collision risk, stated:** `5g3-mount` forbids specific workarounds. A seed row
must say how it avoids them — supplying `legal_entity_id` out of band is the same
defect wearing a different hat. **This row does not yet know how, and says so
rather than inventing a route.**

**`activity-rail-unowned` — DORMANT, promoted out of a parenthetical.** It existed
only inside row 1 ("the activity rail to a trust read model") and as the
2026-07-24 review's NC3. Measured: no surface in `apps/web` reads audit events,
business change documents, or semantic operation receipts; `renderKeyFacts`
(`apps/web/src/component-registry.ts`) emits record id, state and revision only;
no entity carries a created-by or created-at field. **Named firing trigger — it
fires when the first entity carries an authored created-by/created-at pair, NOT
"when the trust read model exists."** `ui-ux-remaining.md` §3d records that second
shape (`U6c`, ladder-trigger) as a deferral waiting on something it had itself
made impossible, which is why this row refuses to use it.

**ROUTING CORRECTION.** The audit routed *"UNSUPPORTED_COMPONENT on inventory and
legal_entity forms"* to `5g3-term`. **That is wrong and would have duplicated a
row filed the same week.** `5g3-term` gates the inventory **mount**;
`inventory-form-anatomy` owns the **slot anatomy** and is the live packet.

**BLOCKED 2026-08-20 — structurally, in the canonical language.** `stock-on-hand-browsable`
stopped before writing any candidate and was right to. Verified by the orchestrator:
`QUERY_AGGREGATE_CONTRACT_V1` (`packages/canonical-model/src/query-aggregate-kernel.ts`)
declares `cardinality: 'exactly-one-scalar-result'` and `grouping: 'unsupported'`, and
`normalizedAggregateQueryDefinition` (`schemas.ts`) declares
**`maximumResultCount: z.literal(1)`** — a literal, not a bound. Three independent
one-property probes (add grouping, raise the count, relabel the aggregate as a list)
each produced `CANON_SCHEMA_INVALID`. **List rendering consumes record DTOs while
aggregate rendering receives one scalar envelope**, so the shapes do not meet either.

**So a browsable balance is a canonical-language event, not a surface packet** —
language semantics, compiler projection, runtime result envelope, PostgreSQL lowering
and cache integration, and a renderer change. **That is the most expensive shape this
program builds.**

**The lane also ruled two product questions worth keeping:** a balance row is **one item
at one location within the selected legal entity** — summing across locations answers a
different question and loses the stock identity ADR-0015/0016 establish; and the default
must be an **explicit captured snapshot instant** bound to both `at_time` and
`recorded_at_horizon`, displayed and preserved through paging — **omitting the filters
would include future-effective movements, and a silent "now" will later be cited as of a
date it never meant.**

**A named alternative, not yet priced:** a materialized balance entity avoids the
renderer change but needs a posting-maintained projection and **would not naturally
preserve the audited bitemporal semantics.** That trade is the fork whoever charters
this must settle.

**RE-CHARTERED 2026-08-20 by program-review finding R6.** The grouped-aggregate
stop above remains correct; its pricing does not. ADR-0007 already sanctions a
rebuildable read-model projection, `inventory_movement` already proves an
operationless platform-written entity can ship, and the provider already owns
posting-linked generation and reconciliation. `stock-balance-read-model` now
implements the bounded alternative as **Posted stock**, explicitly the sum of
all posted movements and never an as-of claim. One row remains selected legal
entity × item × location; the five-parameter bitemporal lookup remains intact;
maintenance, rebuild, and reconciliation reuse the existing generation lock.
The existing List renderer is sufficient, so `apps/web/src` remains unchanged.
Critical round 1 returned REVISE on `c781aaf`: the refusing twin ignored `o1`
and transition-owned operations, and the reserved family earned CRUD/Form
omission without proving the PostgreSQL maintainer's four-field ABI. Critical
round 2 on `ab34546` closed operation attribution but found that canonical
storage metadata could still contradict the provider ABI, malformed fields
could retain the exemption, and an invalid movement companion still qualified
internally. Both rounds' findings are accepted and corrected on
`packet/stock-balance-read-model`. Critical round 3 on `fc788cb` accepted those
repairs but found that the movement companion still proved logical shape rather
than its physical storage ABI: a `source_type.businessKey` edit compiled and
produced an invalid unique constraint. The round-3 replacement executable tree was green
at `83a8b55` (compiler 152/152, architecture 141/141, PostgreSQL 204/204,
browser 91/91). It pins every current lowerer-consumed property on all 16
movement fields, returns false on any mismatch, subject-pins the full dependent
requirement set, and proves operation refusal remains candidate-based even when
qualification fails. Fresh Codex returned PASS on `97eaa36`, but independent
Fable confirmation found declared relations are a
second authored input to the movement physical ABI: an extra movement relation
compiled, retained the exemption, changed the physical table, and made posting
reject the ambiguous transaction binding. Replacement executable `0021bcf` pins
exactly the two provider-maintained movement relations and every property that
changes their material participation. The exact missing, extra and one-property
controls return both their relation diagnostic and all five posted-stock CRUD/
Form requirements. **ACCEPTED 2026-08-21.** Fresh-naive Codex xhigh and
independent Fable max both returned PASS on identical frozen SHA `07c1b44`; the
Fable arm independently ran the focused suite 61/61 and four discriminating
mutants at 60/61. The complete staged-tree matrix is green at `75e6ee8`: unit
120/120, compiler 152/152, performance 5/5, integration 149/149, agent 3/3,
architecture 141/141, contracts 16/16, PostgreSQL 204/204, locale 1/1, browser
91/91, observability 11/11, language coverage PASS, and reachability 104/104.
The first matrix attempt's sole Docker endpoint-publication failure reproduced
green 3/3 in isolation; the full unchanged-tree rerun passed. Lineage remains
12→13 with all predecessors reproduced under their own ADR-0047 §5 profiles.

#### `stock-on-hand-is-not-browsable` — filed 2026-08-19, TIER 2

**Measured from the shipped release, not predicted.** `inventory_movement_on_hand`
is an **aggregate** query taking five parameters — `on_hand_legal_entity_id`,
`on_hand_item_id`, `on_hand_location_id`, `on_hand_at_time`,
`on_hand_recorded_at_horizon` — surfaced as the `task`-archetype
`inventory_on_hand_lookup`. **It answers "how much of item X at location Y", and
nothing else.**

**There is no browsable balance view.** The 31 shipped surfaces include
`inventory_movement_list` (the movement ledger, an audit trail rather than a
balance), but **no stock-on-hand list** — no "all stock at this location", no "all
locations holding this item", no low-stock view. An operator who does not already
know an item id cannot ask what they have.

**Why this was not caught earlier:** every packet in this area asked whether the
*posting* path is correct, and it is. Nobody asked whether an operator can *see the
result* without knowing the answer first. `G3-P6b-2` put the on-hand balance "on
screen" and that row is accurate — it mounted the lookup. A lookup is not a stock
list, and the gap between those two was never written down.

**Tier 2, not Tier 1:** it does not block receiving or shipping, which is the
triage question. **But it is the difference between a system that works and a
system that feels like an inventory system**, and it is the first thing a pilot
user will ask for. **Do not fold it into `ux-list-usability`** — that row is about
search and paging on lists that exist; this is a surface that does not exist.

### TIER 3 — taxes every packet, and one of them makes our evidence unsound.

| row | why it is here and not dormant |
|---|---|
| `container-pressure-forges-outcomes` | **The highest-value infrastructure row.** Docker volume pressure produces a **WRONG release-activation outcome, not merely a slow one** — so a matrix result is not trustworthy without knowing the container state it ran under. Everything else in this tier costs time; this one costs correctness. | **SECOND INDEPENDENT REPRODUCTION 2026-08-14, by `profile-v2-adoption`, with a clean discriminator.** Same test, same string: `composed product activates through the kernel` failing `composed release activation did not verify: NO_SWAP_TERMINAL`. **The discriminator is decisive rather than suggestive:** the ONLY executable change between the SHA where `test:postgres` ran **197/197 green** (`c843c0f`) and the SHA where it failed (`f6ca221`) is `apps/web/test/browser/location-runtime.spec.ts` -- a Playwright spec the PostgreSQL suite neither loads nor executes -- so the packet delta cannot be causal. Re-running that file in isolation gave **16/16**, the same number this row already records. **Container state at failure:** 44 volumes (35 at session start, 38 after one matrix), load 2.20; remediation was this row's own -- 36 dangling ANONYMOUS volumes reclaimed, 44 -> 8, every named volume intact, no orphaned `release-activation` container present. **Why this reproduction is worth more than the first:** `ux-picker` touched the write path, so its red was at least arguable; this packet touches neither the release kernel nor the provider, and its red arrived on a tree whose only delta was a browser test. **The row's cost estimate should rise accordingly** -- two packets have now each spent a review-cycle's worth of time distinguishing a forged outcome from a real one, and the second only resolved quickly because the first had written the signature down.
| `merge-direction-hides-packet-work` | Put a Critical write-path control off trunk while the gate reported OK. Two lanes produced the shape independently. |
| `gate-reads-a-different-thing-than-its-name` | Three instruments in one day read a different tree, format or unit than their name claims. |
| `unrun-quality-gates` | `main` was **format-red on 2026-08-13** and nothing gated it; fixed at the time, still ungated. |
| `leak-guard-orphan` + `matrix-machine-decay` | The container/volume tax: 12.96GB reclaimed once and the suite still ~1.29x above baseline. |

### R2 — control verification: the mechanism is BUILT; three pieces are routed

**`expected-red-gate` closes R2's first half** (frozen 2026-08-21, awaiting review).
`scripts/check-expected-red.sh` plus `test/helpers/expected-red.mjs` generalize the
scoped-create runner into a gate: `check:expected-red` refuses a manifest entry whose
production text has drifted, in CI and the matrix; `evidence:expected-red` reproduces the
declared reds under the exclusive lock. The ruling is
[ADR-0058](../decisions/ADR-0058-an-expected-red-is-identified-by-what-it-kills.md).
**A packet owing §6 negative controls now writes `test/evidence/<packet>.expected-red.json`
instead of inventing a harness.**

Three rows come out of it. None is dormant; each has a named trigger.

| row | why it is here |
|---|---|
| `fenced-claim-needs-a-marker` | **The precondition for R2's second half.** The architecture check that every fenced claim names ≥1 manifest entry was **deferred, not declined**: fences are created in review prompts, which `AGENTS.md` §4 puts in `~/2rain-missions`, outside this repository, and where they appear here they are unmarked English in paragraph-length `review-log.md` cells. A heuristic scanner over that corpus fails on prose and trains everyone to phrase around it — the `gate-reads-a-different-thing-than-its-name` class again. **The fix is a marker, not a cleverer scanner:** record a fence in a machine-findable form (a `Fenced:` trailer on a review-log row, or a fences block in the packet record naming the claim and the control that holds it). Then the check is a join on `test/evidence/**` entry names, which are already stable identifiers. **Trigger: the next packet that fences a claim in a review prompt.** |
| `expected-red-needs-an-initial-state-contract` | **The honest home for what cost `expected-red-gate` five review rounds.** The gate can show that applying a named one-property mutation reds exactly the declared tests and removing it greens them. It **cannot** show the mutation would produce that red from a clean machine, because a suite's own leavings — files, database rows, containers, caches — survive between invocations. Measured: the interaction fixture, whose test fails only when its marker is present AND the source is mutated, is **certified** when the marker was left by an earlier run. Mutation-first ordering stops an invocation manufacturing its own co-cause; nothing in the gate stops a previous one. **The sole-cause claim was withdrawn by orchestrator ruling on 2026-08-21** rather than chased for a sixth round, and control K1 pins the limit executably. **What closing it needs:** a per-entry isolation or reset contract that a manifest declares and the runner enforces, covering at least the filesystem outside the tracked tree, PostgreSQL state, and ephemeral containers — and it may not be expressible for arbitrary suites at all, in which case the answer is to restrict manifests to suites that can declare one. **Trigger: the first packet whose expected-red entry names a suite with external state.** |
| `expected-red-covers-node-tests-only` | The runner builds a `node --test` argv, so a Playwright red cannot be a manifest entry. That is why `inventory-surface-legibility`'s bare-text mutation — the cheapest remaining prose red, and fully reconstructible — was **not** backfilled. **Trigger: the first browser-suite packet that owes a §6 control.** Extending the runner to `test:browser` is honest separate work; smuggling it into the gate packet was not. |
| `postgres-mutation-entries-need-an-isolable-subject` | **Found by running the new gate against the evidence `scoped-create-operand-impl` shipped.** Four PostgreSQL manifest entries could not be made stable. Whole-file, `unscoped-create-closed-key-fence-removed` stopped two tests in one run and four in another — the extra two are 300s-timeout-bounded release-lineage tests that run after the create the claim is about. Bounded to the test holding the claim, the **baseline** fails on unmutated production with an ephemeral-container readiness timeout at `test/helpers/postgres.ts:273`, reproduced twice with no orphaned containers — that test passes as the seventh container of a whole-file run and not as the first. **This is `container-pressure-forges-outcomes` blocking a second instrument.** The entries' manifest form is preserved in `e94441b`; restoring them is a one-file revert once a container-bearing test can start cold within its readiness deadline. |
| `review-log-prose-reds-are-not-executable` | Reds across seven packets exist only as sentences — *"1/1 pass → 0/1 → restored"*. They were not backfilled because prose names the behaviour changed in English at a SHA that has since moved, so `original` would be a guess, and **a guessed entry that happens to red is worse than no entry: it looks like evidence.** Their load-bearing rows also sit in `apps/web/src` and `packages/**`, held by three frozen lanes. **Disposition: backfill belongs to each owning packet's next round**, where the author knows the seam and the source has not moved. |

#### R8 LEAVES A NAMED RESIDUE — `posting-error-provenance`, filed 2026-08-22

**Found by the Fable arm on `posting-error-shape`, verified by the orchestrator.** The
SQLSTATE shape guard closes most of R8, and the residue is a slice of the very class R8
names.

**A SQLSTATE is five characters from `[0-9A-Z]`. So are eleven Node errnos** — `EPIPE`,
`EBUSY`, `EBADF`, `EINTR`, `ELOOP`, `ENXIO`, `EPERM`, `EROFS`, `ESRCH`, `ETIME`, `EXDEV`.
**`EPIPE` is the realistic member:** when the backend dies and node-postgres writes to the
dead socket, the surfaced error carries `code: 'EPIPE'`, and the classifier reports
`INVENTORY_POSTING_STORAGE_REJECTED` with `details.sqlstate: 'EPIPE'`. **A connection drop
mid-posting still reads as a storage rejection** — narrowed from "any string `code`" to
eleven specific tokens, but not closed.

**This is NOT a defect in `posting-error-shape` and that packet must not be widened to
chase it.** `EPIPE` is inside the legal SQLSTATE value space; tightening the shape (say,
requiring a digit) would refuse legal custom trigger and extension `ERRCODE`s, which are
real PostgreSQL emissions — the repo's own `P0001` among them. **Shape cannot separate
these; only provenance can.** The discriminator is the server-error `severity` property or
the driver's `DatabaseError` identity, and that is a different and larger change.

**Does it block `PUR-2`? No — ruled 2026-08-22.** R8 was made a `PUR-2` prerequisite
because `PUR-2` posts through this engine, and the shape guard delivers the bulk of that
protection. Holding `PUR-2` for a provenance refactor is disproportionate to one residual
errno. **Trigger instead: before the first production deployment, or before any incident
runbook cites `details.sqlstate` as authoritative** — whichever comes first, because the
failure mode is a stored false diagnostic and its whole cost is paid during an incident.

**Tier: Behavioral.** Band A — a misclassified posting error is silent until an incident is
misdiagnosed.

## Operating model

**THREE PARALLEL LANES ARE ACTIVE (2026-07-28).** See [lanes.md](lanes.md) for the binding path partition, the shared-file protocol, the serial-integration rule, and the mandatory report header. Writers cannot see each other, so that file is the only shared state — read it before starting or resuming any packet.

- The user drives Codex `gpt-5.6-sol` sessions and pastes their reports back.
- The assistant is **orchestrator + adjudicator**: it hands the user self-contained
  packet prompts, adjudicates review findings and lease-bridge requests by reading the
  code, and runs multi-model **debates** directly (it does not write product code).
- One packet at a time, user-selected, per `mission-cadence`. New Codex session per
  packet — every prompt reconstructs state from disk.
- Reviews follow `review-tiers` (fresh naive spawns, mandatory charter, two-REVISE cap).
- Acceptance requires the **full CI matrix green at the integrated SHA** (not a
  packet-chosen subset) — the rule PR-1 put in force.

### Standing prioritization rule — user directive 2026-07-28 (binding)

**A working inventory module is the goal, as soon as possible.** Every packet
selection passes this filter, in order:

  1. **Does it help get inventory running?** If yes, it is a candidate.
  2. **If no — does it COST inventory?** This is the real test, and it is
     narrower than "is it inventory work". A packet costs inventory if it does
     any of:
       - **holds or contends for a lease** on a file an inventory-path packet
         needs;
       - **takes the full-matrix slot** ahead of an inventory lane;
       - **adds weight to the shared gates** every lane's matrix runs — a slow
         or flaky new test taxes the inventory lanes on every run, which is the
         subtle one; or
       - **consumes adjudication attention** while an inventory lane is stopped
         waiting on a ruling.
  3. **If it costs nothing on all four, run it concurrently.** An idle lane is
     waste, not safety.

**Two standing priorities make concurrency safe**, and they are what replace
idling: inventory lanes get the **matrix slot** first — a non-inventory lane
waits — and inventory-lane reports get **adjudicated** first, always.

**Corrected 2026-07-28, same day it was written.** The first draft said
non-inventory work should be *deferred* unless it faced a closing window. That
was over-corrected, as the user pointed out: the constraint is opportunity cost,
not subject matter, and where opportunity cost is genuinely zero, an idle lane
buys nothing. Authoring is fully parallel and only the matrix serializes, so a
fourth lane on disjoint paths is net positive. What the original draft got right
and is retained: an idle lane is **not itself a reason** to start something, so
the answer to "what can we run" is still a real cost check and not a scramble
for filler.

Applied the same day it was issued, retracting three orchestrator
recommendations: **row 9** (publish-path breadth envelope) and **row 8**
(capability cycle-time baseline) both fail step 1, and row 9's
baseline-cannot-be-reconstructed argument does not reach step 3 — the curve can
begin at any N. **Row 1c-a** (rule the platform tier) also fails: 1c is in
neither inventory chain, its own row records that no product path is affected,
and deciding it later is *better*, because a real posting-service case would
ground the Tier-B question that a hypothetical cannot.

The inventory chains are `4c → Q1-P3b → G3-P5` and
`1d → G3-P1b → G3-P2b → G3-P3`. When every downstream link is gated on a packet
in flight, the highest-value orchestrator action is **landing that packet and
pre-scoping its successor**, not opening a fourth lane.

point is honestly the weaker one: the typed-unsupported hierarchy seam and its only
natural relation were descoped after the factory test found a real press limit, so

**`G2-EK1` Expression kernel is accepted** — ADR-0012 ratifies the ceiling (total, terminating, no loops, no operation invocation, depth ≤ 24), position profiles across five execution targets, SQL admissibility as cost classes, and one owned evaluator per target. The triplicated trivial-predicate fence is converged onto one strict version-dispatched canonical-model entry point. Two rulings land downstream: a **formula/rule projection family IS required**, so queue row 3 now carries two families on one v3 bump; and search input is **literal text**, not a wildcard pattern, which unblocks and specifies row 2.

**`PR-6c` Materializer DDL is accepted** — `deferredOnlineFamily` elements, previously classified then processed in no path, now execute as transactional locking DDL; index verification requires both `indisready` and `indisvalid`; DDL and step receipts are atomic; migration `0011` adds a superuser-owned event-trigger witness with snapshot capture so dropping it is detected. Full matrix green at integrated SHA `4042842`.

**Sequencing question resolved 2026-07-26:** Q1 does **not** depend on the v3 packet. ADR-0012 states that existing query and operation catalogs continue to own their embedded v0 predicates and are not copied into the new formula/rule family as a second authority — that family is for first-class `FormulaRuleDefinition` objects, which are G6-era. **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-28 — v3 IS G3-blocking.** The Q1-P3a writer found what this ruling missed: admitting an aggregate node shape requires a new *serialized spelling*, which is a language-version event. [ADR-0021](../decisions/ADR-0021-total-absent-value-semantics.md) already said so in the same words about `>=`/`<=` — adding spellings under `v2` *"would retroactively widen an immutable language version"* — and `CANON_VERSION_MIXED` (`normalize.ts:355`) enforces one language version for a package's envelope and every nested node, so a per-node version escape is not available. **Row 4's v3 bump therefore sits between Q1-P3a's ruling and Q1-P3b's lowering, and is now on the inventory critical path.** It must carry, on one bump: the capability-disclosure family, the formula/rule family, **D2**, the aggregate node shapes Q1-P3a defines, and `greaterThanOrEqual`/`lessThanOrEqual` parked there by ADR-0021. Order is fixed: **Q1-P3a rules the shapes → row 4 serializes them → Q1-P3b lowers them**; v3 cannot be cut before the shapes are designed. The original ruling below was correct on its own terms — ADR-0012's point about query and operation catalogs keeping their embedded v0 predicates still stands — but it did not consider aggregates. Original text: **v3 is not G3-blocking, and the G3 critical path is Q1 alone**, which can be pulled forward whenever accelerating toward inventory is worth more than completing G2 in order.

**`PR-6d` Prefix search is accepted** — substring search escapes `%`, `_` and `!` so input is literal text per ADR-0012, and prefix search lowers to C-collated range predicates for forced-RLS index access. One REVISE round: emission was widened to search-mapped non-unique fields without execution evidence for that class, closed by a probe with a dropped-index red of 9,999 rows removed by filter.

**`G2-P4` Surface-grammar conformance is accepted** — §8.6's suite now gates every stage from G2 onward, with the bespoke-screen antibody proven by an executed red rather than a green assertion. **G2 has four packets left: P5a, P5b, P8, P9.**

**`G2-P5a` Shared List behaviour is accepted** — bounded paging with coverage, stable sort, authorized pre-page search over relation labels and formatted values, archive visibility, bound cursors, and one shared result consumed identically by UI, Semantic Query and agent. **Freeze I.** Three risks recorded and routed on its ledger row: numeric sort is silently lexicographic (routed to P5b), the count/page coverage race under read-committed, and search that cannot use folded indexes for enum or relation-label arms.

**`G2-P5b` Saved filters is accepted** — canonical predicate envelopes, release-pinned validation failing closed, Semantic Operation writes, and the **first first-party platform package**, which compiled through the unchanged press: the third zero-press-change result and the first for a *platform* module.

## UX conformance — found 2026-07-27, and it re-sequenced the queue

An independent review compiled the three real modules and ran G2-P4's own conformance checker against them. **Party fails 64, Catalog 33, Location 33 — 130 violations — while the architecture suite reports 68/68**, because the suite's only input is a synthetic fixture. `checkSurfaceGrammarConformance` has two call sites: its definition and that fixture test. **No real module has ever reached it.**

The trigger was a human operating the app and noticing the list and record pages were disconnected. That symptom is one of fifteen missing slots on Party alone. The systemic cause is that **every layer is gated against inputs shaped to its own scope and nothing gates the composition** — and the module template replicates it, so Catalog and Location inherited the defect verbatim from the "zero press changes" result we twice celebrated.

Rows 1-3 are the response, in that order: ratchet so nothing new can be added, burn down the anatomy, then write the skill that codifies the template — because writing the skill first would make the defect doctrine.

**SCOPE RULING 2026-07-27 — G2 must be demonstrable.** The user has ruled that G2 ships something a person can open and use, not a set of per-module harnesses. That makes the **composed application G2 scope**, not deferred: one shell carrying the real modules, records reachable, the anatomy present enough that create/save/navigate work, and no error page or `UNSUPPORTED_COMPONENT` panel as the first thing a visitor sees. G2-P9 cannot certify the stage until that is true. This is a constraint on the remaining packets, not an open question.

**Superseded — the earlier framing of this decision:** there is no composed application. `pnpm dev` serves a shell with four `home` surfaces and no domain module, so every "test it yourself" checkpoint so far has exercised a per-module harness rather than the product. A stage gate run today certifies the harnesses. Either G2 ships a composed shell, or G2-P9 states plainly what it does and does not certify — a scope call, not a packet call.

**Not yet started:** G2-P8 import, and everything after.

**G3 ONE-WAY DOORS SETTLED 2026-07-28** — see the [converged record](debates/g3-one-way-doors-verdict.md). Both arms ran independently and reconciled. **Legal-entity classification is ruled with no default** (an undeclared family is a compile-time failure); Party, Party role, Item and Location are `tenantShared`, movements and their kin `entityOwned` — Location decided by the ratified stock tuple, which carries entity *independently of* location and therefore already encodes that location does not determine ownership. **The stock-dimension set was never open** (ratified verbatim at ADR-0016:36); the charter asking it was an orchestrator error. **`negativeStock` defaults to `reject`, evaluated inside the posting transaction**, not as a preflight. **And the deadline list changed shape:** the anchor row as a balance *cache* is addable later, but the **per-stock-identity serialization protocol is required at first posting** — without it a posted fact can violate the negative-stock invariant, and posted facts are immutable. Two items travel to G3-P0 still open: the dial scoping (per tenant or per legal entity), and the concrete concurrency mechanism.

| # | Packet | Tier | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| gate-instrument | **Re-derive the isolated compile budget with an independent cold-regression witness** | Behavioral | **Created 2026-08-01 by gate-perf's failed CPU calibration; recorded, not fixed in Packet A.** Ten samples admitted by the old one-minute-load proxy measured 2,373–3,068 ms CPU, while the frozen matrix measured the same compile at 1,851.8 ms. Direct observation proved the host could be 96.4% idle while load average still read 1.43: the proxy was measuring its own decaying echo. Packet A replaces it with a 300 ms `/proc/stat` delta requiring 90% idle and restores the shipped 5,000 ms wall verdict with CPU telemetry only. Ten direct-idle runs tightened to CPU 2,263.0–2,535.5 ms and wall 1,829.2–2,065.9 ms; even including a 2,859.4/2,238.1 pre-batch validation, maximum-plus-range is below 2× the fastest observation in both units, so the numerical constraints are compatible again. **The bound remains known-loose because the regression witness is still invalid:** two compiles in one process are not both cold, and that arm varied from 4,892 ms focused to 3,196 ms in the matrix. This packet must build an independently repeatable cold-workload witness, choose the measurement unit, and derive rather than guess a replacement bound while preserving direct-idle INDETERMINATE admission. |

| 1b | **Release-admission verification integrity** | Critical | **Violates ADR-0020 as ratified.** `assertReleaseIdentity` validates `verificationEvidenceId` with `assertUuid` and nothing else (`release-repository.ts`); executed results live in a process-local `WeakSet` (`verification.ts:68`); no production service writes the canonical preparation that `createApproval` loads; module harnesses supply `randomUUID()`. ADR-0020's enforcement clause requires that *no code path admits a candidate with an unexecuted verification set*. An immutable release can therefore make a false statement about its own verification, and human approval can bind evidence digests with no connection to any execution. Persist durable content-bound verification results, require exact plan/result conformance at admission, provide the canonical preparation writer, and prove that missing, stale, partial, fabricated and differently-rooted result sets each fail closed. **Ban random evidence IDs and direct pointer mutation from any product-conformance journey** — which is also what row 0's real activation path needs. |

Proved empirically: adding a required numeric `unit_cost`, or removing `quantityDelta` and `stockDimensionSetVersion`, still returns `{"status":"compiled","diagnostics":[]}`. G3-P0 item 6 is **deadline-bound** and assigns G3-P1 the duty of making money fields a *compile-time failure*; G3-P1a made the contract self-consistent instead. That is the *declared rule with no executing gate* pattern this program names as the cause of every escaped defect, sitting on a ratified one-way door. **Ruled: fixed inside `G3-P2b-1`, not routed** — see that packet. **Escalated to a SWEEP 2026-07-29** after a third review round produced the same class again (movement entity omittable entirely; three external-reference fields not lifecycle/type-bound). `review-tiers` says a review that keeps finding more of the same class signals a mis-scoped charter, and the standing rule says three instances is systemic — so the packet was directed to enumerate **every clause the frozen contract asserts** and prove the module is bound to each with an executed red per clause, rather than close instances one round at a time. The two-REVISE cap was deliberately extended for this packet on the grounds that every finding sits on a deadline-bound one-way door and a sweep is the convergence move the skill prescribes, not another chase round. The pattern to watch across all three instances is that the pinned-contract mechanism has no automatic binding to the modules it governs; each binding must be written, and the gap is invisible until a real module exists. **The general observation is the row:** the pinned-contract mechanism the orchestrator endorsed for `G3-P1b`'s family map, and the debate endorsed again for partitioning, systematically deposits module knowledge in the press. | 1f-2 | **One full-matrix command** | Mechanical | **Created 2026-07-29 when G3-P1b's matrix silently omitted `check:boundaries`, `check:schema` and the security scan** — caught by the orchestrator reading the log, not by any gate. There is no single "full matrix" command, so each lane assembles its own chain and they have now measurably drifted: 1d, 4c, packet 9 and G3-P2a all ran those three; G3-P1b did not. This is 1f's gate-inventory friction producing a real evidence gap rather than a theoretical one, and PR-1's rule that acceptance needs the full matrix "never from a packet-selected subset" has no executing gate behind it. Give the repository one command that IS the matrix, so a lane cannot assemble a subset by accident. **ALSO OWNS, found 2026-07-30 by `G3-P3`'s round-3 matrix: an unbounded lock wait hangs the suite instead of failing it.** The matrix ran **92 minutes** with `pnpm test` accumulating only **2 seconds of CPU** — blocked, not slow. Captured before teardown: blocking chain `97 → 95 → 94`, where PID 94 sat **`idle in transaction`** holding advisory `objid 1` while waiting on `Client/ClientRead`, PID 95 waited on that advisory lock, and 97 waited on 95. Because no cycle exists, PostgreSQL's deadlock detector never fires and the wait is infinite. **Two separate obligations:** (a) concurrency controls must set a bounded `lock_timeout` so a blocked acquisition fails observably instead of hanging — a control that can hang forever cannot distinguish *blocked* from *broken*; and (b) **the posting path itself needs a bounded lock wait**, because in production a stalled holder would hang every subsequent poster for that stock identity indefinitely. That is the same availability class PR-6c established when one tenant's held advisory key failed every other tenant's materialization. The runner should also cap total wall-clock so a hung suite surfaces as a red rather than a silent stall. Two more leaked containers were reaped during cleanup, one up **five hours** — the orphan-cleanup gap already recorded above, recurring. **ALSO OWNS, found 2026-07-29: test containers orphaned by a dead owner degrade the whole machine.** A `north-star-party-walking-slice-<pid>-<uuid>` PostgreSQL container ran for **11 hours** under a dead PID, alongside an OOM-killed sibling. Fresh-container readiness measured **167 s** against the repository's 30 s envelope; after removing the two orphans it measured **25 s**. This produced two independent red matrices that looked like unrelated defects — Q1-P3b's `check:schema` readiness failure and 1g2's compile-budget miss at 6012.8 ms against 5000 ms. **Neither bound may be raised**: per the standing rule, a gate whose verdict depends on machine load observes the scheduler rather than the fact, and widening it widens what starvation is allowed to look like. 1f made readiness *deterministic*; the missing half is **cleanup on abnormal exit**, so a killed run cannot leave a container holding resources for the next one. |

| 5 | **Q1 compositional query tier** | Critical | **Also owns plan §5.12 floor item F1 — absent-value semantics.** Optional is the default field presence, so the moment Q1 lowers a `notEquals` or a negation over an optional field, IR two-valued logic and PostgreSQL three-valued logic can disagree and silently drop rows from a saved view. ADR-0012 defers *cross-target* semantic pinning to G6; the filter-position ruling cannot wait that long, because Q1 is where the divergence becomes reachable. **G3-blocking**, per verdict S2: G3's correctness contract is `SUM(posted movement.quantity_delta)`, so without Q1 the inventory stage hand-rolls aggregation against ADR-0011's grain. The plan already specifies the tier (§5.1, §5.9, §9.2.2) — filters, traversal, joins, grouping, aggregates, reports, exports over semantic entities. Today the gateway rejects every tier above Q0 and every filter other than literal `true`. Placed after the fan-out so it is designed against three real modules. Also the answer to "can the agent analyse data" — see verdict R8/R9. **Depends on the accepted G2-EK1 expression kernel:** Q1 is the first tier to compile and SQL-lower non-trivial filters, so the expression ceiling, position profiles and cost classes must be ratified before it is designed. **Owns the precondition field-locality defect** surfaced by the expression-kernel debate: query filters enforce source-entity locality (`normalize.ts:1050-1064`) but operation preconditions validate scalar type only and can reference a field from any entity in the package (`:1074-1083`). Latent solely because the runtime fence rejects non-trivial preconditions — this row opens that door, so it closes the hole or explicitly re-routes it. **Also the trigger for L2-L4** (see "LLM-assisted verification" below): the first non-trivial SQL lowering makes the parity corpus, the model-assisted dispatch tripwire, and the scenarios-as-specification prototype all due here — one body of work, not three. Scope them at admission; if they do not fit, cut them as a paired follow-on rather than deferring past G3. **MUST CARRY A POLICY-PREDICATE SEAM (competitive review D3).** Q1 sits at row 5 and the policy kernel at row 7, so the tier that owns SQL lowering is designed **before** the kernel that will need to narrow it. Policy narrowing cannot be an application-layer post-filter over fetched rows: prior art rejected exactly that design and named the decisive reason — **post-filtering is incompatible with pagination.** Ask for 50, remove 20 by policy, return 30, and the cursor no longer means anything. Our own G2-P5a coverage race is the same failure one level down, since count and page are already two statements whose consistency the gateway asserts. So Q1's lowering must expose a seam where a policy-contributed predicate joins the compiled `WHERE` clause, with the seam proven by an executed test that supplies a narrowing predicate and observes it in the plan — even though row 7 supplies the only real narrowing later. Retrofitting this after the lowering ships means reopening the most expensive layer in the tier. |

| q1-p4 | **Legal-entity scope addressable through the typed query contract** | Critical | **RULING REVERSED 2026-07-30 BY THE PACKET'S OWN REVIEW. The orchestrator declined canonical v4 and was WRONG.** The refusal rested on `LEGAL_ENTITY_FAMILY_RULES` living in `conformance.ts` with zero occurrences in `packages/canonical-model/`. **That precedent does not transfer**, and the review said so precisely: the family map derives **compile-time storage metadata**, whereas the read scope selects a **runtime business value that changes the answer** — scope `{A}` returns `5`, scope `{A,B}` returns `12`. Tenant and environment are ambient identity; *which legal entities to consolidate* is a query operand, and query operands belong in the query shape. **The decisive consequence, verified by the orchestrator at both call sites:** neither production caller can supply a scope — `semantic-gateway-api-adapters.ts:24` invokes `(view, semanticEnvelope)` and `release-verification-service.ts:1269` invokes `(this.view, {...})`, both two-argument. So no API caller can request a balance, and release verification — which probes **every** active entity — fails closed with `MODULE_LEGAL_ENTITY_READ_SCOPE_REQUIRED` the moment inventory mounts, blocking `G3-P5` and `G3-P6a`, the packets this row exists to unblock. The second arm framed the same fact as a security virtue: the scope is unforgeable *because* the wire cannot carry it. Both readings are true; unusable is still unusable. **The enforcement layer stands and is well reviewed** — typed refusal before SQL with no false-zero path, all six read builders and every joined alias scoped, a WeakMap identity seal unforgeable by copy, JSON round-trip or cross-view reuse, genericity keyed only on `StorageEntityTarget.legalEntity`, and a real cross-entity disclosure found and fixed during gating (`module-runtime-interpreter.ts:1255` projected a relation's `recordId` from the SOURCE row's FK while its label came from the scope-filtered join, so a foreign record id leaked while its label was correctly redacted; it now projects from the joined target, nulling by the same mechanism, and both arms independently confirmed no sibling). **Two further defects are owed by this row:** `verifyLegalEntityReadScope` (`request-runtime-view.ts:360-385`) never rechecks authorization nor compares `scope.policyVersion` against current policy, so a scope issued under policy v1 survives a v2 revocation — contradicting `G1-P5.md:54`, which holds that policy changes deny execution immediately and entry policy evidence is not frozen authority; and the per-member authorization controls are vacuous, since adding `break` after the first authorization would leave the matrix green while `[allowedA, deniedB]` issues a scope containing B. **The canonical query-shape event is now authorized and belongs to its own packet**, which must express the legal-entity operand and its cardinality — `onHand` requires exactly one entity while ADR-0015's consolidated reporting requires a set. **Lesson recorded against the orchestrator, not the lane:** a precedent about where a *contract* lives does not settle where a *runtime operand* lives, and the cheap path was refused for one round on that confusion. | The lane's first design was a new canonical predicate/reference/projection spelling plus canonical language **v4** with digest movement across every package. **Declined**, because it did not weigh this program's own precedent: the legal-entity column it needs to address was itself added with **zero canonical spelling** — `LEGAL_ENTITY_FAMILY_RULES` lives in `conformance.ts:433-445`, is resolved by `storage.ts:595`, and appears **zero times** anywhere in `packages/canonical-model/src/`. That is the pinned-contract + conformance-rule pattern, and ADR-0021's ruling pattern is to rule the semantics and decline the spelling wherever that creates no second authority. On re-analysis the lane agreed: *"The family-map precedent does transfer to reads. The missing piece is trusted scope issuance, not canonical representability."* **The lane also corrected the orchestrator's proposal, and the correction is adopted:** it must be an explicit policy-approved **issued capability**, not a third RLS/session identity axis and not a PostgreSQL GUC, because `ADR-0015:38` binds the distinction between tenancy and business scope. Tenant and environment stay ambient; legal entity is a business dimension carried as an authorized scope, keyed only on `StorageEntityTarget.legalEntity` (`storage.ts:938`) so inventory is never named. **The decisive property is failing closed, and the lane found the exact failure mode:** a missing scope must never become `legal_entity_id = NULL`, because that returns no rows and `COALESCE(SUM(...), 0)` then silently returns **zero** — the identical wrong-answer defect this packet exists to prevent. Refusal must be typed and must precede SQL construction, for every entity-owned source and every joined entity-owned alias. **Two further rulings:** the scope contract must admit an authorized **nonempty set**, not merely a scalar, or ADR-0015's cross-entity reporting requirement is designed out — and it must never mean "omitted, therefore all"; and per-entity authorization being **allow-all under today's policy posture** must be recorded plainly rather than implied away, since real restriction stays blocked on the policy kernel at row 7. **Owned behaviour change:** inventory's Q0 get/list/search/resolve queries over transaction, line, period lock and movement move from tenant-wide to scope-required. Not user-visible yet because inventory is unmounted, but real, and this packet owns it. **Deferred to a future language event, explicitly not shipped here:** per-query opt-in/opt-out, a returned legal-entity system field, grouping by entity, and any predicate reference to the compiler-owned column. | **HARD PREREQUISITE OF `G3-P5`, created 2026-07-30. `G3-P5` is blocked until this lands, and the blocker is real rather than a lease problem.** The lane stopped reporting that `onHand` cannot be expressed correctly, and the orchestrator verified the whole chain: the frozen stock tuple is `(legalEntityId, itemId, locationId)`; `inventory_movement` authors item, location, effective time, recorded time and quantity but **no legal-entity field**; the compiler adds `legal_entity_id` as separate storage metadata **outside** the entity's canonical `columns` (`storage.ts:938`); and `predicate-lowering.ts:190-196` resolves a `fieldComparisonPredicate` only when it finds **both** an authored `FieldDefinition` **and** a matching `entity.columns` entry by `canonicalFieldId`, throwing `'query predicate field has no storage column'` otherwise. Decisively, `packages/postgres-provider/src/module-runtime-interpreter.ts` contains **zero** occurrences of legal entity, so reads carry no implicit legal-entity scope either — they are scoped only by hardcoded `trusted_tenant_id()`/`trusted_environment_id()` (`:523-524`). **A registered aggregate would therefore sum identical item/location identities across legal entities and return a balance that is simply wrong.** **Three escapes are closed, which is why this is a new packet and not a bridge grant:** a read capability with hand-authored SQL is forbidden — `G3-P0.md:160` says `onHand` "returns ADR-0022's scalar result through the registered query path" and `G3-P5` "may not hand-author SQL in an inventory service", and `:45` says no G3 packet may replace the `4a -> 4b -> 4c -> Q1-P3b` sequence with inventory-specific SQL; special-casing `legal_entity_id` in the interpreter is module-specific SQL in a generic component, banned by ADR-0011; and authoring an ordinary movement field would create a second physical column that `G3-P3`'s posting service does not populate — a duplicate authority for one fact. **It belongs to the query sequence, not to G3**, since the charter assigns the query-level `sum` contract to that sequence, hence the `q1-` id. Expect a query-contract extension admitting compiler-derived scope dimensions as predicate targets, its own ADR, and careful language versioning — per ADR-0021 adding a spelling to a released version retroactively widens it. |
| 5g3-prog | **Major-correctness-domain program review for the inventory ledger** | Program review | **Created 2026-07-30. `G3-P3`'s re-review flagged this as now due; the orchestrator read the deferral text and rules it due after `G3-P5`, not now.** `G3-P2b-2.md:332-335` defers the major-new-correctness-domain trigger with a precise condition: it fires once *"the serializer, negative-stock evaluation, immutable fact, **and read reconstruction** compose."* Three of those four exist on main after `G3-P3` — the per-stock-identity serializer, negative-stock evaluated inside the posting transaction against serialized state, and the append-only movement fact. **The fourth does not**: read reconstruction is `onHand`, which is `G3-P5`. Firing the review now would examine a correctness domain with a write path and no read path, and the composition question — whether a balance reconstructed from posted movements agrees with what the ledger serialized — is the most valuable thing such a review can ask. **Scheduled after `G3-P5` is accepted and before `G3-P6b`'s agent journeys**, so the review's findings can still shape the write surfaces rather than arriving after them. Two-arm, both arms reading the composed path. **Seed questions, from what this program already learned the hard way:** does a balance computed as of an effective instant agree with the frozen same-instant tuple, given that `5g3-ord` records the tuple's final `movementId` branch as unobserved; can any cache serve a balance that never existed at any instant; and does the ledger's one-way-door set still hold once a read model can be rebuilt, since a rebuildable projection is the classic place a supposedly immutable fact gets quietly corrected. |
| 5g3-berr | **Typed base-unit rejection at the item-update boundary** | Behavioral | **Created 2026-07-30 by `G3-P3`'s Critical review; verified by the orchestrator before ruling.** `G3-P3` added `translateInventoryPostingError`, which turns the trigger's raw `P0001` into a typed `INVENTORY_BASE_UNIT_IMMUTABLE` carrying `itemId`, `bindingMovementId`, `bindingUnitId` and `requestedUnitId`. **No production path reaches that branch.** Verified: the rule is a **CHECK constraint on the item table** (`module-storage-materializer.ts:3324`), so it fires only on item INSERT/UPDATE; posting never updates the item, so the branch is unreachable from `inventory-posting-service.ts:537,542` where the translator is actually wired. The only production caller that can trip it is the Catalog Item update operation, and the generic interpreter's `translateModuleProviderError` has **no `P0001` or base-unit branch at all** — every unrecognised SQLSTATE becomes `MODULE_PROVIDER_FAILURE` (`module-runtime-interpreter.ts:2863,2869,2904`), discarding all four fields. **Why `G3-P3` was not made to fix it:** wiring it means adding inventory-specific error translation to a generic component, which ADR-0011 bans and ADR-0023 narrows only for admitted capabilities; the architecture suite already gates module-specific glue in generic identifiers and filenames. That needs its own bridge, so forcing it into `G3-P3` would have smuggled the exact violation the review chain exists to catch. **What `G3-P3` records instead:** the translator is correct and its reachable branches (`55P03`, `STORAGE_REJECTED`) are proven; the base-unit branch is honestly recorded as **mechanism-proven, product-unreachable**. The test control invokes the translator directly and must be read as a translator control, not an end-to-end product observation. **This row owes the bridge**: either a generic mechanism by which a module registers SQLSTATE-to-typed-error mappings, or an accepted narrowing that admits an inventory branch. Prefer the generic mechanism — a second module will need it. **COST DEMONSTRATED 2026-07-31, and it is higher than "a diagnostic is less specific".** `G3-P6a` mounted Inventory into the composed application for the first time and two `composed-application` controls failed with `MODULE_PROVIDER_FAILURE: module provider rejected the operation`. **No SQLSTATE, no relation name, no constraint, nothing** — grepping the entire suite output for a SQLSTATE, a missing relation or a permission error returns zero lines. The real cause is discarded at the point of translation, so the first genuine integration failure of the inventory mount is **undiagnosable from test output alone** and requires temporary instrumentation to investigate at all. That converts this row from a quality improvement into a debugging tax paid by every packet that touches the generic press from here on. |
| 5g3-ord | **Order-decisive controls for the frozen same-instant tuple** | Behavioral | **Created 2026-07-30. Both review arms independently found the same observation gap, and it corrects an orchestrator claim.** The frozen rule (`contracts.ts:275-288`) is ascending order over `(effectiveAt, recordedAt, sourceType, sourceId, sourceLine, postingRole, movementId)` — `sourceId` ranks **fourth**, `movementId` **last**. **The orchestrator wrongly claimed `G3-P3`'s new tie-break control discharges G3-P0 item 8.** It does not: that control pins the 0015 trigger's `ORDER BY (recorded_at, record_id)` **binding-diagnostic pick**, which is a different ordering answering a different question, and the two **disagree inside the control's own fixture** — its movements differ in `sourceId` (`...-tie-first` / `...-tie-later`), so the frozen tuple ranks the first-inserted movement while the control asserts the trigger names the later-inserted one. Not a breach of the one-closed-rule requirement — the trigger's pick is diagnostic naming, and the base-unit invariant is order-independent because all of an item's movements provably share one unit — but **not item-8 evidence either**, and the control must never be cited as such. **Two real gaps to close, both currently green against broken code:** deleting the `movementId` comparison at `inventory-posting-service.ts:991` and `:1431` leaves the whole matrix green, so the tuple's final branch is unobserved; and outright deleting the `.toSorted(compareMovementOrderEntries)` at `:1381` is also undetectable, because persisted rows always precede planned ones and every present fixture's verdict is order-insensitive. **Owned by `G3-P4`**, which extends ordering to transfers and so must build the order-decisive fixture anyway: a same-instant persisted mix whose verdict flips when the sort is removed, plus a pair distinguished only by `movementId`. |

So the active release pointer names `northstar.application` while the admitted capability belongs to `northstar.inventory`, and no bridge between them exists. **A migration recording the admission was proposed and is REJECTED**: if the release does not declare the capability, a side table recording it is a *second desired-state authority* with no source — precisely the dual-lineage disease row 1c exists to prevent. **The correct fix is compiler emission**: the capability declaration becomes part of the release artifact, which requires inventory mounted in the active release (`G3-P6a`) plus a projection change. So `G3-P6a` waits for `G3-P4a` to land, then relaunches on a refreshed worktree with a narrow additional grant: remove the obsolete manual composition from `inventory-posting.test.ts` and consume the now-authoritative composed definition, **changing no posting behaviour and weakening no control**. That grant is conditional on the lane proving the 32 controls still observe what they observed before. **PULLED FORWARD 2026-07-29 by user ruling, to get a visible inventory UI a day sooner.** Splits `G3-P6`: this half mounts inventory in the composed application and ships read-only list and record views over real posted movements; **`G3-P6b`** keeps the fixed-five-tool agent journeys and the anatomy burn-down for after `G3-P4`/`G3-P5`. **HARD PREREQUISITE, ADDED 2026-07-30 BY `G3-P4b`'s ROUND-2 REVIEW: row `5g3-term` MUST LAND BEFORE THIS ROW INTEGRATES.** The review's finding was that the prerequisite existed only as prose in a packet record while this queue row — the one that actually gates integration — still permitted the mount, and that `current-plan.md:184` holds that prose without an owning row provides no executing gate. It is corrected rather than deleted, because the stale claim is exactly what made the missing gate hard to see.)* **Three known costs, accepted:** `apps/web/test/browser/composed-application.spec.ts:54` asserts an exact navigation list and goes red until updated; inventory's **71** surface violations become visible in the running app (≈14 per entity across five families, comparable to Catalog's 13, so it reads as *incomplete* rather than broken now that G2-P5d-b unified dispatch and hoisted record-level diagnostics); and it consumes one serialized matrix and review slot that `G3-P5` would otherwise get — roughly an hour of critical path. **The real trade:** deferring `G3-P4` means the serializer's deadlock-ordering rule stays proven only by `G3-P2b-2`'s controls rather than by a real transfer posting. | 5g3-seedrole | **Browser posting seed must encode the runtime role in its connection URL** | Mechanical | **Found 2026-07-31 by the full matrix at `c90904f`; the production privilege guard was correct and the browser harness was wrong.** The fixture constructed a pool with both `connectionString: databaseUrl` and `user: 'north_star_runtime'`, but pg 8.22.0 reparses the connection string and overwrites the explicit field (`lib/connection-parameters.js:57-60`), so the URL's `postgres` superuser reached `assertRuntimeLogin` and was correctly refused with `INVENTORY_POSTING_STORAGE_INVALID`. **The fix belongs only to the harness:** derive a role-specific URL exactly as the product runtime does, prove the resulting login is `north_star_runtime` and non-superuser, and retain a negative control showing the unmodified superuser URL is refused without partial state. `assertRuntimeLogin` is unchanged. |

Controls cover the previously unexecuted operand-plus-list combination, byte-equivalent behaviour for a query declaring no parameters, and refusal of an undeclared root key. |

The production refusal at `packages/runtime/src/semantic-query-gateway.ts:401-405` was correct but appeared nowhere in tests; the only execution was an accidental stale call that failed after Inventory adopted the operand. An ordinary declared row parameter would therefore be ignored rather than bound and type-checked. | 5g3-txtitle | **Inventory transaction detail titles resolve to the actor, not the transaction number** | Behavioral | **Disclosed 2026-07-31 by `G3-P6a`'s writer rather than found by a gate, and correctly left unfixed.** The compiled resolve authority for `inventory_transaction` points at `inventory_transaction_actor_id`, so a detail page titles itself with a principal UUID instead of `ADJ-BROWSER-001`.
