Changing a query's resolve authority was outside `G3-P6a`'s charter and would have been scope expansion to satisfy a cosmetic finding. **DEFERRED 2026-08-01 and explicitly off the critical path.** The attempted fix exposed the platform gap at `5g3-resolveidx`; neither row is worth a lane or ADR while they gate only a human-readable title. **The six deliberate choices are frozen here and must not be re-derived:** `legal_entity` resolves by **code**, its concise stable business key; `inventory_transaction` by **transaction number**, the operator-facing document identity; `inventory_transaction_line` by **line number**, because identity is parent-relative and the item reference is a non-unique UUID; `inventory_period_lock` by **closed-through period**, the authored value that states what it controls; `stock_count` by **count number**, its business and operator identity; and `stock_count_line` by **line number**, for the same parent-relative reason. Field order must never be changed to simulate these choices, and UUID fields must not be made searchable to hide the transition gap. |
| 5g3-resolveidx | **Resolve-derived storage transitions and retirement policy** | Critical | **Created 2026-08-01 by `5g3-txtitle`'s correct stop; deferred and explicitly off the critical path. This is a PLATFORM gap, not an Inventory exception.** Mechanics map: (1) a text resolve-only field derives a stored C-collated fold plus a `foldedAccess` index; (2) a text field that is also searchable derives the same objects independently, so moving resolve authority does not retire them; (3) a text business key derives a stored fold plus its case-insensitive unique index, which a resolve query reuses; (4) a non-text resolve field derives no fold and instead gets a scoped raw-column `resolveAccess` index; and (5) a newly created entity gets its complete table and indexes in the existing fresh-table path. Storage lowering builds this from `resolveMatchFieldIds` at `packages/compiler/src/storage.ts:679`. Existing transition lowering at `storage.ts:1970` covers only a newly added fold and its dependent index; it does not express a new non-text resolve index on an existing column or removal of former resolve-only objects. Thus removing a text resolve-only authority removes its fold and folded index, removing a non-text authority removes its `resolveAccess` index, and relocation is the union of the applicable addition and removal. `inventory_transaction` actor-id → number is removal-only because number already has its business-key fold/index, producing a moved storage root with `elements: []`. **CORRECTED RULING:** retaining former additive objects does not weaken catalog drift; the union-live-root verifier makes them attributable and this is how accepted ADR-0024:60 preserves reversible pointer rollback. Retirement in PR-6c's current phase is unsafe: `module-storage-materializer.ts:543` executes locking DDL while the source release is still active, `:586` verifies the source/target union, and only `composed-application-runtime.ts:417` swaps the pointer. A source resolve query still directly references its compiled fold, so pre-swap removal breaks the active release. **OWED AND UNRULED, not authorized by this row:** whether a future post-swap, crash-recoverable retirement phase should exist, with its own step receipts and an `IRREVERSIBLE / FORWARD_RECOVERY_ONLY` edge classification. That would be a new mechanism and decision; do not infer it from the desire for catalog tidiness. Until then, a resolve-authority transition must preserve attributable additive storage, must not loosen `CATALOG_DRIFT`, and must not make UUID fields searchable as a workaround. |
| 5g3-derivcheck | **Verification admission must independently validate derivation eligibility** | Critical | **Created 2026-07-31 by G3-P6a's Critical review; recorded, not fixed in G3-P6a.** The provider derives scenario exclusions from real operation and storage contracts, but compiler admission validates only a derivation's closed shape, entity/fingerprint binding, and exact partition. A future producer could therefore mislabel a constructible scenario with a well-formed reason and self-certify the skipped execution. G3-P6a adds an independent PostgreSQL control over the real composed release that recomputes every scenario's constructibility and rejects a deliberately mislabelled derivation; the runtime verifier itself still does not recompute eligibility. A dedicated Critical packet must harden admission without crossing compiler/provider ownership or creating a second constructibility authority. |
| 5g3-term | **Terminal-state operation preconditions — `G3-P6a`'s hard blocker** | Critical | **Created 2026-07-30 by the orchestrator, and every claim below was verified by reading before the row was written.** `G3-P4b` authors `stock_count.state` as an ordinary `enumeration` field over `draft \| counting \| reviewed \| posted` (`packages/domain/src/inventory/definition.ts:418`), and `stock_count`/`stock_count_line` sit in `standardEntities`, so `definition.ts:694` routes them through the generic `operations()` — which emits **create, update, archive and restore**. `posted` is terminal: a posted count session is the ADR-0017 evidence justifying already-posted immutable movements. **So the generic press admits an UPDATE that rewrites the evidence behind an immutable fact, including flipping `state` from `posted` back to `draft`.** **Not reachable today only because inventory is unmounted** — which is precisely why this blocks `G3-P6a`, whose whole content is mounting it. **THE EXPOSURE IS AT THE OPERATIONS/API LAYER, NOT THE UI — and the orchestrator got this wrong in BOTH directions before a lane settled it against the compiled release.** First recording: operations-layer only. Then `G3-P4b`'s round-2 review argued it was worse — a rendered Edit button — reasoning from source (`surfaces()` passes `readOnly` as `local === 'inventory_period_lock'`, so both count entities get `readOnly: false` and emit form surfaces with `create`/`update` permissions). The orchestrator amended the row to say so **without compiling the release to check**. `G3-P6a`'s lane then mounted Inventory for real and refuted the amendment with the artifact in hand: **record surfaces emit no `commandBar`, so there is no Edit button; form surfaces carry an unregistered activity slot and no mutation-authorizing sections slot, so the single registry authority treats them as non-writable; and a forged form, archive or restore submission is refused `422 OPERATION_UNSUPPORTED` at `apps/web/src/surface-runtime.ts:169` under the registry rule at `component-registry.ts:148`.** So the original framing was right and the amendment was wrong. **The lesson is the one this program keeps paying for: `readOnly: false` in the source is not a rendered write path, and a claim about what a release DOES must be checked against a compiled release, not inferred from the definition that feeds it.** **The defect itself is untouched by all of this and remains real:** direct authorized invocation of the emitted operation id passes the literal-`true` fence at `packages/runtime/src/semantic-operation-gateway.ts:513`; `prepareMutation` has no terminal-state or parent-session check (`module-runtime-interpreter.ts:306`); and archive writes `archived_at`, making posted evidence vanish from every read path requiring `archived_at IS NULL`. `G3-P6a`'s inert UI prevents the *claimed user journey* but is not a generic terminal-state invariant, and it is a property of that packet's incomplete surfaces rather than a guarantee — it would evaporate the moment those surfaces are finished. **Three escapes checked and closed.** (i) *Declare the entities `appendOnly`* — `conformance.ts:1291` requires an `appendOnly` fact entity to declare **zero** operation effects, but a count session is edited across `draft` and `counting` before it posts, so append-only is the wrong shape for the pre-posted phase. (ii) *Use the existing state-machine concept* — `stateMachines` is **compiled but not enforced**: `storage.ts:751` turns a machine into a `derivedStateFields` column carrying a `stateMachineId`, and `packages/runtime/` contains **zero** references to it, so no transition or precondition is checked at runtime. ***ORCHESTRATOR ERROR, CORRECTED 2026-07-30 by the design lane and re-verified before amending.** This row originally claimed `module-storage-materializer.ts:4098-4107` "explicitly discards `derivedStateFields` in the compatible-merge path (`void _derivedStateFields`) — the same silent-omission shape already found for `abiFunctionChecks`." **That is wrong.** `void _derivedStateFields` excludes them from the base-shape **conflict comparison** only, identically to columns, indexes, uniqueKeys, checkConstraints and foldedColumns; `mergeCompatibleEntity` then merges them into its return value (`:4255-4259`), and the materializer actively consumes them (`:1309` DDL emission, `:3111`). The merge line was visible in the orchestrator's own grep output and was misread. The defect is unchanged and if anything cleaner: **the storage layer materializes derived state faithfully and the runtime simply never enforces it.*** (iii) *Special-case stock count in the interpreter* — module-specific logic in a generic component, banned by ADR-0011. **The mechanism owed is generic operation preconditions keyed on a declared terminal state, enforced below the API rather than in the UI**, with the ratchet that an update or archive of a terminal record is refused and a negative control proving the refusal is not vacuous. **DESIGN RULED 2026-07-30 (Fable, read-only design lane; every load-bearing claim re-verified by the orchestrator before adoption). Option A, and the mechanism already half-exists.** The cheaper Option B — route the count lifecycle through the posting capability as `inventory_period_lock` is routed — **was refuted on its own premise**: `periodLockOperations` is *not* capability routing. Those operations carry ordinary `updateRecordEffect` and execute through the **same generic press**, differing only in ids, permissions and confirmation; no entity's lifecycle is executed by a capability through any transport today, and ADR-0029 explicitly withholds a transport route from the posting capability. So B needs new invocation plumbing plus a bespoke draft lifecycle plus a third conformance carve-out, and is strictly larger than A while growing per module. **What A actually needs is a fence opened, not a mechanism built** — verified independently at all three points: `operationDefinition.precondition` is already a typed v3 predicate in the released grammar (`schemas.ts`, `precondition: v3PredicateExpressionSchema`); its falsity semantics are already ruled (`predicate-kernel.ts:182`, `operationPrecondition: profile('operationPrecondition', 'reject-operation')`); the compiler already transports it to the runtime (`projections.ts:400` → `operationCatalogPayload` → `RegisteredOperationDefinition.precondition`); and the **only** missing piece is evaluation — `semantic-operation-gateway.ts:513-536` calls `inspectPredicateForExecution` in **one-argument mode**, admitting literal `true` and refusing everything else as `SEMANTIC_OPERATION_PRECONDITION_UNSUPPORTED`. An authored precondition today therefore **fails closed, never open**. **No canonical language event and no migration** — grammar and normalization are untouched; this is Q1-P1's precedent of widening a v0 execution fence to the evaluation mode the kernel already ships. **Deliverable:** declare a not-posted precondition on `stock_count`'s four generic operations; make the interpreter evaluate preconditions at `prepareMutation` against **every image the effect consumes or produces** (candidate on create, prior *and projected* on update, prior on archive/restore — which closes mutation, un-posting, forgery via `reviewed → posted`, and archival with one predicate); add a generic **parent-aggregate rule** so a mutating operation on the source of an active `parentScopedChild` relation must also satisfy the parent's precondition, which closes `stock_count_line` with zero line-level declarations while deliberately exempting `reference` ownership so a correction may still supersede a posted prior. Compiler conformance pins the guard as a ratchet, not a second authority. **ADR-0034.** Ten controls, each with a named victim; C2, C7 and C10 are the non-vacuity half — proving the refusal is state-keyed, ownership-scoped, and does not leak into the capability's direct-SQL `#post` path. **The capability's own `reviewed → posted` transition is untouched** because it is direct SQL inside `#post`, not a press operation. **Completeness ruling, also routed here and now answered:** draft authoring **remains the lifecycle-restricted generic press**; no capability-owned authoring path is required before `5g3-ui` integrates. **Sequencing: after `G3-P5` (which owns `module-runtime-interpreter.ts`), before `G3-P6a`.** **Sequencing: `G3-P6a` MUST NOT INTEGRATE until this lands.** `G3-P6a` may rebase and be reviewed in the meantime. |
| gate-perf | **The compile-budget gate measures the machine, not the compiler — and main is intermittently red** | Critical | **Created 2026-07-30 after four wrong diagnoses, and it is the most consequential finding of the evening because it means recent green matrices were partly luck.** `test/compiler/performance-budget.test.ts:36` asserts a **wall-clock** `process.hrtime` measurement of one `compileApplication` call against a fixed 5,000 ms budget, and it runs at **position 103 of a 109-test compiler suite**, i.e. after roughly a hundred other tests have saturated the CPU. **Measured evidence, all on a quiet machine.** On the Q1-P5 integration SHA the full matrix failed it three times at ~17,000 ms; the same file **in isolation** passed at **3,025 ms**. The compiler suite alone reproduced it at **16,607 ms**, and the same suite with `--test-concurrency=1` produced **5,077 ms** — over budget by 1.5 %. **Decisively, the gate fails on `main` with no packet applied**: six consecutive isolated runs on `main` gave 2 passes then 9,203 / 6,539 / 5,833 / 7,871 ms, and a single run after a 45-second pause passed again. The times *climb* across back-to-back runs and recover with rest, which is the signature of sustained-throughput degradation on a WSL VM capped at 8 GB and 8 processors — not of a code regression. **Four hypotheses were tested and refuted in order, and each refutation is worth keeping because each was plausible:** a Q1-P5 integration regression (refuted — fails on `main` without it); load from a concurrently launched review (refuted — fails quiet); the orchestrator's own tool calls (refuted — fails with the orchestrator idle through one blocking call, though that investigation did produce the separate and true finding now recorded in `lanes.md`); and machine degradation over the session (refuted — memory is 6.5 GB free with zero pressure and the isolated test is fast). **What this costs the program:** `G3-P4b` at `f6b7463` and `Q1-P5` at `806a05a` both recorded full-matrix PASS, and this gate is a coin flip, so those passes were partly fortunate. That is the same defect class the orchestrator recorded against itself earlier for `assertBaseUnitBound` — **one green run of a nondeterministic test is not evidence** — and it is now known to apply to the gate set itself. **THE BUDGET MUST NOT BE RAISED.** The bound is meaningful; the *measurement* is broken, and raising it is the exact move the standing prohibition exists to prevent. The repair is to make the gate observe compile cost rather than scheduler luck — the strongest candidate is the minimum of N compiles, since a genuine regression raises the best case too, with a dedicated serial invocation as the alternative (`test:postgres` already sets `--test-concurrency=1`, so the precedent exists in this repository). **This blocks `Q1-P5`'s integration** — not because `Q1-P5` is at fault, which it demonstrably is not, but because integrating onto a red matrix while calling the red "just flaky" is how a real regression eventually walks through. **Deliberately not repaired unilaterally**: a gate change is precisely the class of edit that needs a review arm, and no second arm exists until Codex quota returns on Aug 4. |
| ver-agg | **Aggregate assertions are never verified — the verification plan omits them** | Critical | **Created 2026-07-31 by `G3-P5`'s correct STOP, and it is a pre-existing gap that no packet introduced.** `packages/compiler/src/compiler.ts:867-868` filters `queryType !== 'aggregate'` when building the v2 alias, and `:923` restores aggregates only to the **query catalog** — not to the verification plan. **The evidence is empirical, not inferred:** the lane compiled and materialized a real Party release carrying a scoped aggregate assertion, admitted it, and observed that its verification plan contained **no scenario for that assertion**. `verificationPlanPayload` itself is innocent — `projections.ts:660,680` iterates every query and assertion it is handed — so the omission is in which revision reaches it. **Consequence, and it is wider than the packet that found it:** every release admitted to date has had its aggregate assertions unverified, silently. That is not a regression and nothing is currently wrong-answering, but ADR-0020's whole premise is that admission rests on an executed verification set, and aggregates have been outside it. **Why `G3-P5` could not fix it and was right not to:** the bridge is compiler-owned — the plan must be built from the original canonical query set including aggregate assertions. Recording provider-local evidence, or emitting a reduced plan, would create a second verification authority and violate the compiled-plan digest authority, which is precisely the dual-lineage disease. The lane did not touch compiler paths and stopped instead. **Blast radius to weigh before implementing:** making aggregates verifiable means every existing release's plan gains scenarios, so releases that verified clean may now probe paths that fail — that is the gate doing its job, but it must be sequenced deliberately rather than discovered during an unrelated packet's matrix. **This row blocks `G3-P5`'s release-verification probe condition, which `Q1-P5` originally assigned to `G3-P5`.** That condition is now owed by this row instead, with the reason verified rather than asserted. `G3-P5` may otherwise proceed. |
| lang-adopt | **Whole-application canonical version adoption — one module cannot move alone** | Critical | **Created 2026-07-31 by `G3-P5`, and discovered by trying it rather than by reasoning about it.** Inventory adopted canonical language **v4** for the `onHand` aggregate operand. The composed application fixture then failed with `CANON_SCHEMA_INVALID`, because a v4 module merged into a v3 application mixes node versions and `Q1-P5`'s node-version purity refuses it. **Setting the composed package to v4 was tried and reverted:** it then rejects the v3 nodes of Party, Catalog and Location, turning one violation into sixteen. **The rule this exposes: a composed application can be v4 only when EVERY module in it is v4.** Node-version purity is uniform by design — that is exactly what `Q1-P5` built and what makes `CANON_VERSION_MIXED` meaningful — so version adoption is an application-wide event, not a per-module one. **This was flagged independently by the `G3-P6a` lane** (*"existing composed-Inventory fixtures merge into a v3 application; G3-P6a owns the whole-application v4 adoption in `app/builder.ts`, its current branch is still v3"*), which is corroboration from a second direction rather than a single reading. **What it costs right now:** two architecture controls (`compiled navigation stays flat within budget…` and `compiled navigation reachability reds…`) cannot pass in `G3-P5` without moving Party, Catalog and Location to v4, which is outside its lease and outside its charter. **RULED 2026-07-31 by the orchestrator, on direct precedent rather than on preference: adoption is its own artifact event, absorbed exactly once, across all product modules.** The precedent is `4c` — *"v3-c — packages migrate to v3, the artifact event"* — whose ledger row states the whole `4a → 4b → 4c` decomposition existed to reach exactly this shape: **"definition was free, only adoption cost."** `Q1-P5` is this cycle's definition step and was correctly cheap; `G3-P5` then attempted adoption as a side effect of a feature packet, which is precisely what that decomposition exists to prevent. Mixed-version composition is **REJECTED**: it would weaken the node-version purity `Q1-P5` built, and `4c` shows the sanctioned shape is uniform-within-a-revision with **history keeping its old versions** — after `4c` the application lineage was "mixed historical-v2 / current-v3", so old releases are untouched and only the current revision moves. **Consequence for `G3-P5`:** its `onHand` feature depends on adoption, so its last two architecture controls cannot pass until the adoption packet lands. Either `G3-P5` is split — feature waits, adoption goes first — or it absorbs adoption and grows to touch every module definition, `app/builder.ts`, and every release golden. **Split is preferred, again on `4c`'s precedent**, because an artifact event mixed into a feature packet cannot be reviewed as either one. **Sequencing:** this blocks `G3-P5`'s final two controls and is a hard prerequisite of `G3-P6a`, which mounts Inventory into the composed application and therefore hits the same wall the moment it rebases onto a v4 Inventory. |
| 5g3-priv | **`G3-P5`'s migration 0019 widens a role privilege AND disables the control that would catch it** | Critical | **Found 2026-07-31 by the full matrix, not by review, and it is the worst shape this program tracks: a change that both moves a boundary and blinds its detector.** `db/migrations/0019_semantic_aggregate_anchors.sql:155` executes `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA platform TO north_star_module_runtime`. **Verified new:** `main` contains **zero** such grants across every migration, and `test/postgres/module-storage-transition.test.ts` passes 14/14 there. **The privilege change is real.** `north_star_module_runtime` is the least-privileged role — the one the generic press runs as — and `platform` is the schema holding releases, verification evidence, executor authority events and tenant-scoped gateway data. Granting it schema-level `USAGE` is a boundary move, not an implementation detail, and it is needed only because `0019` put the aggregate anchor and discrepancy tables in `platform` at all. **The control it disabled is the sharp part.** `assertKernelSnapshotDrift` proves the checked-in schema snapshot detects kernel-level drift, and its probe sequence uses **that exact grant** as one canary: grant it, assert the snapshot rejects, revoke it. With `0019` making the grant permanent, re-granting is a no-op, no drift appears, and the assertion fails `Missing expected rejection`. So the packet did not merely widen a privilege — it consumed the canary that existed to notice such widening, and every future grant of that shape would now pass unobserved. **The honest fix is NOT to edit the control**, which would convert a detector into a rubber stamp. Two candidates, and the packet must choose and argue: put the anchor tables where the module runtime already has access so no new schema grant is required, or keep them in `platform` and give the drift control a different, still-absent canary — recording plainly that the runtime's reach grew and why that is acceptable. **RULED 2026-07-31: take (a), relocate to `north_star_internal`.** The choice is settled by a fact rather than a preference — `north_star_module_runtime` **already holds `USAGE` on `north_star_internal`** (granted in `0007`), so the anchors can live there with **no new schema grant at all**, and the canary stays absent and therefore still fires. `north_star_internal` is also where this class already lives: `module_storage_generations`, `module_storage_elements`, `module_storage_element_applications` and `module_fold_function_ddl_witnesses` — internal machinery, tenant-scoped, not business or trust records. A derived aggregate cache and its discrepancy log are exactly that. Option (b) is rejected: it asks the program to accept a permanently wider runtime role and a replacement canary, to obtain nothing the relocation does not give for free. **What the change costs, so the lane can size it:** the `0019` DDL and its grants, the interpreter's anchor read/write queries, the schema snapshot, the tenant-completeness manifest and its two count ratchets, and the `0019` row in the five add-only migration inventories. **Blocks `G3-P5`.** |
| 5g3-mig19 | **Two open packets both claim migration `0019`** | Mechanical | **Found 2026-07-31 by the orchestrator before either landed, rather than at integration.** `packet/g3-p5-v2` carries `0019_semantic_aggregate_anchors.sql` and `packet/g3-p6a-v2` carries `0019_inventory_release_provenance_and_partition_null_safety.sql`. `main` is at `0018`. This is **not** a defect in either packet and needs no ruling — [lanes.md](lanes.md) already governs it: the migration inventory in `test/architecture/release-persistence-boundary.test.ts` is an exact `deepEqual` over filenames and is **add-only shared, not a lease**, so *"on rebase conflict: discard your side, take main's, and re-derive your entry."* **Whichever packet integrates second renumbers to `0020` and re-registers**, because `migrations.ts:248` enforces a contiguous stream and a duplicated number breaks it. `G3-P6a` is expected to land first, so `G3-P5` is the one that renumbers; renaming is safe because neither migration's content depends on its number. Recorded only so this is a two-line rebase rather than a surprise stop. |
| 5g3-stale | ~~**`G3-P5`'s aggregate cache can serve a balance the ledger disagrees with**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-20** | Critical | **DONE. The defect is fixed and this row was stale.** Found by `stock-on-hand-browsable` while pricing a browsable balance, and verified by the orchestrator: `module-runtime-interpreter.ts` now takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(aggregateGenerationLockKey(tenantId, environmentId))` and then `loadAggregateMovementGeneration`, so the cache key carries a **posting-linked movement generation** rather than a self-consistency digest alone. The exact reaching sequence this row specified — anchor `5`, append a `+2` correction with `effectiveAt <= E` and `recordedAt <= H`, repeat the identical request — is a committed control at `test/postgres/inventory-onhand.test.ts` and returns **7**. **A grouped balance implementation MUST reuse this generation guard; a separate cache path reopens the defect**, and that constraint carries to whatever packet builds one. ORIGINAL TEXT FOLLOWS. **Found 2026-07-31 by `G3-P5`'s Critical review, and it is the precise defect that packet's cache proof exists to prevent.** `module-runtime-interpreter.ts:925` validates a cache hit against a **self-consistency digest only** — the request identity plus the cached result. It carries no ledger generation or watermark, and **posting never invalidates an anchor**. An internally intact anchor therefore bypasses recomputation at `:940`. **Reaching sequence, concrete:** query `onHand` at effective horizon `E` and accepted future recorded horizon `H`, anchoring result `5`; append a correction `+2` whose `effectiveAt <= E` and `recordedAt <= H`; repeat the identical query. The cache key and self-digest both still match, so the runtime returns **5** while the authoritative sum is **7** — and **no discrepancy is recorded**, so nothing anywhere observes the disagreement. **Why the proof missed it.** The independent SQL oracle at `inventory-onhand.test.ts:1684` IS structurally independent, exactly as G3-P0 item 10 and AGENTS.md §6 require, but no control drives this sequence: every existing case either changes a horizon, which mints a new cache key, or corrupts the anchor digest, which forces recomputation. **A verifier that never runs on the failing input is not a verifier**, and this is the shape the program keeps rediscovering — a control that is real, independent, and pointed slightly away from the defect. **This is the one-way-doors concern made concrete:** `G3-P2b-2` deferred the major-correctness-domain review until read reconstruction composed, and its seed question was whether a cache can serve a balance that never existed at any instant. The answer, today, is yes. **What is owed:** an atomic posting-linked generation or watermark that an anchor is keyed on, so appending a movement invalidates every anchor whose horizons could include it, plus a same-key append-after-anchor negative control that fails before the fix and passes after. **Do not close this by widening the cache key** to include a timestamp or a nonce — that disables the cache rather than making it correct, and hides whether invalidation works. **Blocks `G3-P5`.** |
| 5g3-mount | **Mounting Inventory proves its entities are UNCONSTRUCTIBLE through the generic press** | Critical | **Diagnosed 2026-07-31 by the orchestrator, by instrumenting the swallowed error rather than guessing.** `G3-P6a` merged onto a main carrying `G3-Pterm`, `lang-adopt` and `G3-P7a`, and two `composed-application` controls failed as `MODULE_PROVIDER_FAILURE`, cause discarded. Temporary instrumentation at the translation site produced the real error and it was removed immediately: **`23502 null value in column "legal_entity_id" violates not-null constraint`, entity `northstar.app:entity.inventory_transaction`.** **This is structural, not a bug.** `legal_entity_id` is compiler-derived storage metadata that lives **outside** the entity's canonical `columns` (`storage.ts:938`) — the same fact that forced `Q1-P5` to exist, because a predicate could not address it either. A generic create therefore has no authored field through which to supply it, so the entity is **emitted and non-constructible**: exactly `G3-P6a`'s own earlier finding that *emitted does not imply arrangeable*, now reaching a real database instead of a survey. **The mechanism to record it already exists and is already on main.** `G3-P7a` widened release verification to an exact partition of executed results and per-scenario impact-analysis derivations, and its packet record states plainly that `G3-P6a` **owns `release-verification-service.ts`, already emits the exact `VERIFICATION_OPERATION_INPUT_UNCONSTRUCTABLE` finding shape, and maps its constructibility findings onto that contract once `G3-P7a` integrates**. It has. **So the work is the mapping, not a fix:** an unconstructible scenario becomes a recorded derivation carrying entity, operation and required-storage-column, never an executed result and never a skip — `ADR-0020:156` forbids admitting an unexecuted scenario absent a recorded derivation. **Explicitly forbidden:** making the generic press invent a legal-entity value, adding an inventory branch to the interpreter (ADR-0011), or authoring an ordinary movement field to carry it, which would create a second physical column `G3-P3`'s posting service does not populate. **THE FULL PATH, mapped by the orchestrator and each step verified against the code before recording — three of four are one-liners and the fourth is the real work.** (1) `release-verification-service.ts` already computes `verificationConstructibilityFindings(compiledRelease)` and already adds `entity.legalEntity.column` to its required set, so **the correct finding is already produced and merely unused**. (2) `executeVerificationPlan(plan, meta, executor)` is called without `G3-P7a`'s fourth `derive` argument; the mapping is a lookup, since scenarios carry `entityId` and findings carry `entityId`, and returning `null` means *execute for real* so an entity with no finding is untouched. (3) Passing `derive` yields a **v2** result set, which trips `type ResultSetVersion = '…/v1'` — exactly the residue `G3-P7a`'s review flagged and assigned here. Widening that alias and `insertEvidence`'s parameter to the exported union `ExecutedVerificationResultSet` clears everything except the last step. (4) **The remaining work is the durable READER.** `readDurableVerificationEvidence` does not select `impact_analysis_derivation` at all — the column appears only in the INSERT — so v2 evidence reads back without its derivations and fails the union. The reader must select that column, parse the closed `northstar.verification-impact-analysis/v1` document, and expose `derivations` for v2 rows while leaving v1 rows a fully executed set. That is the shape `G3-P7a` described as *"after the concurrent service reader consumes v2, that validated evidence can report 7 executed + 32 derived"*. **The orchestrator applied steps 1–3, confirmed only step 4 remained, and reverted rather than leave a half-migrated durable-evidence type in a service that persists trust records.** **CORRECTION 2026-07-31, by reading the writer instead of trusting the map above:** step 4 is **not** the reader alone, and the four-step map understated the work in two ways. (a) **There is also a WRITER.** The evidence `INSERT` at `:1889` hardcodes `'FULL','[]'::jsonb,NULL` for `execution_scope`, `skipped_scenario_ids` and `impact_analysis_derivation`, so it can never emit v2 — and migration `0018`'s `release_verification_evidence_exact_partition` CHECK requires v2 ⇒ `EXACT_PARTITION` **and** a closed non-empty document, so a v2 result set reaching that INSERT unchanged is **rejected by the database**. Steps 1–3 therefore do not merely "trip a type alias"; applied alone they would fail at persistence. (b) **`derive` has two live call sites, not one.** `:178` persists and `:276` (`executeSemanticCandidateWithExecutor`) does not, while `:264` is the empty-plan path and correctly stays v1. Both live sites must derive from the *same* binding findings, or one release yields two different result sets. **The governing pin is [ADR-0033](../decisions/ADR-0033-exact-partition-verification-derivations.md)**, which was not cited in the map above: it rules the v1/v2 split, requires `skipped_scenario_ids` to stay `[]` for both ("derived scenarios are evidence, not skips"), keeps `result_count` counting only genuine executions, forbids a second partition authority, and requires federation to preserve the identical document. **One property makes this safe to build:** derivations are inside the result-set digest, and the reader's reconstruction is re-checked by the same `validateExecutedVerificationPlan` before admission — so a lossy or wrong round-trip fails loudly at `VERIFICATION_EVIDENCE_CONFORMANCE_FAILED` rather than admitting silently. The mount-path reader (`composed-application-runtime.ts:1232`) needs no change: it selects only version and digest, and `0004`'s CHECK requires `verification_evidence_version` to be non-empty, not v1. |
| 5g3-prov | **`contract_release_root` is provenance sitting in an immutability set** | Behavioral | **Found 2026-07-31 by the BUILD lane while mounting Inventory; ruled the same day by the orchestrator after reading migration `0015_inventory_storage_foundation.sql`.** `platform.provision_inventory_scope` is **provision-or-assert-identical**: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, then assert the stored row equals every requested value (`:456-475`), with **no `UPDATE` path**. That is correct for business-**policy** fields — negative stock, backdate days, reason requirements, approval thresholds — where a change must be a deliberate act rather than a side effect of shipping. But `contract_release_root` is **provenance**: it records which compiled contract the scope runs against and naturally advances when the product advances. Because provisioning re-runs on every start with the *current* root, an already-provisioned tenant raises `INVENTORY_POSTING_CONFIGURATION_CONFLICT` the moment the product advances a release — before the kernel's own checks run. **The lane's first fix was rejected:** it read the stored root and passed it back, which makes the parameter *unfalsifiable* — the equality it feeds can never disagree, so the guard goes vacuous for exactly the case it exists to cover. **Ruled fix:** remove `contract_release_root` from the assert-identical set and `UPDATE` it to the requested value, so the stored value stays truthful by **advancing** rather than by being echoed back; every policy field keeps its exact assertion and the same errcode/DETAIL. Controls must prove the stored root actually changed (assert the row, not merely the absence of a throw) and that genuine policy drift still conflicts. |
| 5g3-rls | **The materializer can seed only the first tenant** | Critical | **Found 2026-07-31 by the BUILD lane, confirmed by the orchestrator by reading `module-storage-materializer.ts`.** `provisionDefaultLegalEntity` (`:1372`) and `provisionPeriodLockRows` (`:1374`) run **before** `ENABLE`/`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` (`:1378-1381`) in the same function. RLS is **table-level**, so the very first tenant's materialization slips through while the table is still unprotected, and every **later** tenant fails `42501`: the only INSERT policy is created `FOR north_star_module_runtime` (`:1407`), and the materializer receives a **SELECT-only** policy (`ensureMaterializerSelectPolicy`, `:1412`). The first tenant therefore passes by accident of ordering, which is why this survived a green matrix — nothing mounted a second tenant against these tables until Inventory did. **Ruled fix:** mirror the existing narrowly-scoped precedent and add a seed INSERT policy for `north_star_module_materializer` carrying the **same** tenant/environment predicate the runtime policies use, granted **only** on `legalEntityMaster` and `periodLock` tables — never blanket INSERT, and never by reordering RLS enablement, which would merely re-bless the tenant-1-only accident. Controls must prove a **second** tenant materializes, that the materializer cannot insert into an ordinary business table, and that it cannot insert across tenants. |
| 5g3-nullchk | **Migration 0018's v2 CHECK passes on a NULL derivation document** | Mechanical | **Found 2026-07-31 by the BUILD lane; confirmed by the orchestrator from the constraint text.** `release_verification_evidence_exact_partition` rejects v2 + `FULL`, but **not** v2 + `EXACT_PARTITION` + `NULL`: `jsonb_typeof(NULL)` is `NULL`, so the v2 arm evaluates to `NULL`, `FALSE OR NULL` is `NULL`, and a SQL `CHECK` **passes on NULL**. The service's writer cannot reach that state because it derives `execution_scope` *from* the document — but the CHECK exists precisely to catch a writer that does the wrong thing, so a documented hole in the last line of defence is not a closed finding. **Ruled:** make the v2 arm NULL-safe in a migration and replace the explanatory comment with a real negative control. Leave the v1 arm's meaning untouched. |
| 5g3-sm | **`stateMachines` — enforce it or retire it** | Behavioral | **Created 2026-07-30, routed out of `5g3-term`'s design lane, which explicitly refused to absorb the decision silently — correctly, because deciding the fate of a language concept as a side effect of an inventory packet is how a second authority gets created by accident.** The canonical language declares `stateMachines`; the compiler consumes it (`storage.ts:750-773` turns a machine into a `derivedStateFields` column carrying a `stateMachineId`); the materializer materializes it (`module-storage-materializer.ts:1309`, `:3111`) and merges it across compatible revisions (`:4255-4259`). **And nothing enforces a transition.** `packages/runtime/` contains **zero** references to `derivedStateFields` or `stateMachine`, verified per-file. Every first-party module declares `stateMachines: []` — the concept has **zero users** and has never executed. **This is a proven member of this program's worst defect class — compiled but unenforced — which is exactly the class that produced the runtime payload-family break (`5g3-rt`), the eight version-from-artifact violations, and the terminal-state hole itself.** `5g3-term` deliberately does NOT adopt it: its `stateField` is derived by normalization from the machineId (`normalize.ts:299-302`, stripped from authored form at `:427-428`), so binding it to `G3-P4b`'s authored `state` field would require changing normalization rules — precisely ADR-0029's stated line for a canonical-language event — and would mean either a second state column or reworking landed storage, digest evidence and contract. **The decision owed:** either give the concept an executing enforcement path with a negative control, or retire the spelling per ADR-0021's rule-the-semantics-decline-the-spelling pattern. Retiring is not free either — removing a released spelling is its own language event — so this row must weigh both, not assume. **Not urgent and not a blocker of anything**, since a concept with zero declarations cannot misbehave; it is scheduled work, not a defect to chase. |
| 5g3-post | **G3-P3 — the first posting vertical slice** | Critical | **SCOPED 2026-07-29, and it needs an ADR nobody has written.** The decisive finding: **posting cannot be an O0 operation.** `conformance.ts:1001` (added by G3-P2b-1) requires an `appendOnly` fact entity to declare **zero** operation effects, so the generic press structurally refuses to write a movement — `declaredEffects: ['read']` on the movement capability requirement confirms it. Posting is therefore mediated by the declared capability `northstar.inventory:capability.posting` v1, frozen by G3-P1a. **That capability's implementation is currently UNAUTHORIZED.** ADR-0023 admits a platform-persisted capability only through its own accepted ADR and names saved filters as the sole one admitted; **ADR-0025:56 explicitly disclaims the posting path** ("does not ... add a posting path, or authorize reservations"). So G3-P3 must carry **its own ADR** ratifying the posting capability, or it will either stall or smuggle an unauthorized capability past ADR-0011's ban on module-specific O0 handlers. The honest argument for admitting it is strong and should be recorded rather than assumed: the capability is not an *alternate* path for a Tier-A entity, because the press declares there is no generic path at all. **What it must carry, from G3-P0's obligations:** prove base-unit update rejection after a real post (item 3); source trusted `recordedAt` and enforce the period lock **inside** the posting transaction with no update path (item 4); prove persisted movements, read-back, audit and outbox contain **no monetary amount** (item 6); enforce the typed dials and the one global same-instant tie-break transactionally (item 8); use the natural effect key `(source_type, source_id, source_line, revision, posting_role)` for idempotency (item 10). **`negativeStock` = `reject`, evaluated INSIDE the posting transaction against serialized state — never a preflight**, per the one-way-doors verdict. Reads and writes must stay inside the pinned 30-entry dependency set. **Scope is adjustment only**; transfer and stock-count correction are G3-P4. **`G3-P2b-2` IS ON MAIN — every prerequisite is met.** **BINDING CONSTRAINT FROM ITS REVIEW:** rolling back to a **pre-existing caller savepoint releases advisory locks acquired afterward**, so the posting service must acquire the stock lock **immediately after `BEGIN`**, before any savepoint a caller could roll back to. G3-P2b-2 recorded an executed red observing the lock vanish after `ROLLBACK TO` with the outer transaction ID unchanged. Honour that contract or the negative-stock invariant is silently unprotected. |
| 5g3-ser | **G3-P2b-2 — the per-stock-identity serializer** | Critical | **MECHANISM RULED 2026-07-29 by the orchestrator, so this packet implements and proves rather than decides.** G3-P0 offered a lock point per stock identity or serializable execution with bounded retry. **Ruled: an explicit lock point**, acquired inside the posting transaction. Reasons: SSI takes predicate locks across the aggregate's read set, so on an append-only ledger with hot stock identities it yields broad false-conflict aborts rather than precision; blocking is predictable where a bounded retry budget can be exhausted by a hot identity, the classic ledger hot-account failure, and PR-6c already showed this program's retry budgets trip far earlier than configured; the one-way-doors verdict requires `negativeStock` evaluated **inside** the posting transaction against serialized state, which a lock makes exact and SSI makes probabilistic-with-abort; and it composes with the natural effect key rather than depending on it, where under SSI every retry re-executes and correctness rests entirely on idempotency. **Three binding constraints:** two-key advisory locks with a **reserved namespace integer**, never `hashtext()` into the shared space — `migrations.ts:113` and materializer `:3440`/`:3467` already share one key space and PR-6c proved sharing it makes one tenant's work fail every other tenant with `MIGRATION_LOCK_TIMEOUT`; a **deterministic total order** when a posting touches multiple stock identities, since transfers touch two and unordered acquisition deadlocks; and **transaction-scoped** locks only, never session-scoped, never `pg_try_*` plus preflight, which G3-P0 rules inadmissible. Prove with concurrent negative-stock and transfer controls. |
| 5g2 | **G3-P1b — legal-entity family map and key participation** | Critical | The remainder of G3-P1, consuming `1d`'s composed unique emitter. Freezes the no-default family map, the derived `legalEntityId` column, and `sameEntity`/`crossEntityAllowed` relation semantics. **SCOPED 2026-07-28, and the answer reverses the orchestrator's own first reading.** I initially judged this blocked on `4c`, because the verdict requires *"undeclared family = compilation failure, no default"* and **no canonical spelling for `entityOwned`/`tenantShared` exists** — `schemas.ts` has none, **4b did not reserve one** despite defining every v3 family, and 4c is not adding one. Under that reading the map would need a canonical per-entity field, every existing entity (party, catalog, location) would have to declare it, their canonical bytes would move, and it would cost a **second artifact event** — exactly what decomposing row 4 into 4a/4b/4c existed to prevent. **That reading was wrong, and G3-P1a had already shown why.** `conformance.ts` carries **298 lines** of inventory rules and twelve `INVENTORY_*` diagnostics enforcing pinned domain contracts with **zero canonical spelling** — `INVENTORY_POSTING_DEPENDENCY_UNDECLARED` (`:1084-1089`) is literally the "declared set, else fail" shape this rule needs. **Ruling: express all three pieces as pinned contract plus conformance rule, not canonical fields.** (1) The family map becomes a constant in `packages/domain/src/inventory/contracts.ts` with a typed undeclared-family diagnostic; existing modules declare **nothing**, so no canonical bytes move and there is no artifact event. | lang-singleton | **The coverage ledger drops single-valued axes, so narrowing an axis to one value launders its defects** | Critical | **FOUND 2026-08-03 by `5g3-langnarrow`'s Codex Critical review, on the gate's first real language change — and the gate did not catch it.** `language-conformance-ledger.ts:710` admits an axis only when `candidate.forced || candidate.values.size > 1`. **An axis narrowed to exactly one value disappears from the ledger, and every obligation and defect classification attached to it disappears silently.** langnarrow refused `joinEligibility: 'none'`, which left `'query'` as the sole value — so the axis became a singleton and dropped out. Its partition went from `94 = 0 + 89 + 5` to `87 = 0 + 87 + 0`: **the defect inventory reached ZERO and it read as a clean sweep.** `joinEligibility: 'query'` is still accepted and still has **zero consumers** — verified: 0 files in `packages/compiler/src`, `packages/runtime/src`, `packages/postgres-provider/src` and `apps/web/src` read it, only `canonical-model` declares it. It was hidden, not closed. **This is the laundering the orchestrator was told the gate could not prevent, arriving mechanically rather than deliberately, which is strictly worse — nobody intended it and nobody would have been looking.** The human review chain caught it; the mechanical gate is what was supposed to. **Two consequences:** the `1,899` obligation count **systematically under-reports** the language by excluding every single-valued axis; and **every future narrowing** carries the same trapdoor, since narrowing to one value is the normal end state of refusing shapes. **`5g3-langnarrow` then measured the blind spot exactly, which turns this row from a hypothesis into a fix:** the ledger digest is `b4cf5f06…` **byte-identical before and after removing the member** — the gate returns the same digest for *"narrowed to an unhonoured singleton"* and *"removed from the language,"* two facts with opposite meanings. **The asymmetry is mechanical and named:** the **lowered** derivation passes `includeSingletons: true` and keeps its singletons; the **authored** derivation passes `false`. **~~The fix is the `forced` flag the code already has and does not use here~~ — THAT PREDICTION WAS WRONG, and `lang-singleton` proved it.** The root cause is **classification, not the flag**: `z.literal('manyToOne')` is a bare `StringLiteral`, not a one-member union, so narrowing an axis to its last value moved it off every branch that raised `forced` — the flag could not fire for the exact case it existed to protect. Because `:672` was the only unforced add site, forcing it and flipping `includeSingletons` were **the same fix**, which collapses the filter to `size > 0`; both the flag and the parameter were therefore removed rather than flipped, and the rule is now structural. Recorded against the orchestrator's guess, which named a mechanism instead of finding one. **The original prescription follows, retained for the record** — an axis that ever carried a defect entry, or that is reachable in the authored surface, must stay represented at size 1. **The negative control is this exact scenario, replayed:** narrow an axis to a singleton whose remaining value is unhonored, and require the gate to keep reporting it. Ships before any further language-narrowing packet, because that is the class of packet that triggers it. |
| arch-race | **`test:architecture` races itself — leak-guard's guard trips over leak-guard's own fixture** | Critical | **FOUND 2026-08-04 by `G3-R3`, which reproduced it at unmodified `main` (`cc2d17f`) in a scratch worktree with none of its own code present.** `test:architecture` carries **no `--test-concurrency` pin** while `test:postgres` carries `--test-concurrency=1`, so its files run concurrently. `repository-hygiene.test.ts:464` shells out to a **nested `scripts/run-matrix.sh FOREIGN-CONTROL`**, which inherits `leak-guard`'s **post-lock** container guard (`run-matrix.sh:42`); a sibling file plants a `north-star-leak-guard-victim-*` container as its own fixture; the nested guard sees it and refuses with `POSTGRES_CONTAINER_CONTAMINATION … age_seconds=3`. **Corrected 2026-08-04 by the online review** — this row originally named `:438` (`LOCK-CONTROL`) and the **pre-lock** guard, and both were wrong. `:438` fails earlier at `flock` with exit 75 and never reaches a container guard at all; the pre-lock phase deliberately **leaves recent containers for their owning lane** (`guard-ephemeral-postgres.mjs:29`). Naming the wrong control flow in the row that governs a gate's execution model is the same defect the row exists to fix. **A second correction from the same review:** the guard does **not** refuse on any live `north-star-*` container. Recent ones are left alone before locking and refused after; containers past `NORTH_STAR_CONTAINER_STALE_SECONDS` (default 3600) are **force-removed** (`:69`). So the residual host coupling is worse than a red build — **a sufficiently old container belonging to another lane or a developer's own session is deleted**. **The guard is behaving exactly as designed** — the defect is that two architecture files that must not overlap are permitted to. **Intermittent and worsening: 116/116 at `6646f5d` earlier the same day, then 0 of 3.** **This blocks every packet, not just `G3-R3`**, and it means `main` is intermittently red. **Do not close it by exempting the victim container from the guard** — `lanes.md` forbids adding to an allowlist without a bridge, and the guard's whole purpose is to notice exactly that container name. The likely fix is the `test:postgres` precedent (pin the suite serial) or a targeted mutual exclusion via the existing `run-with-test-lock.mjs` between the nested-matrix control and the victim fixture. **Whichever is chosen, measure and report the suite's wall time before and after** — cycle time is the program's binding constraint and a serial pin is not free. Note the tension with row `cycle-pg`, which asks whether `test:postgres`'s serial pin is still needed now that container lifecycle is fixed: these two rows should be taken together, since one may remove a pin and the other add one. |
| cycle-artifact | **The release generator emits unformatted JSON, so every regeneration costs a failed matrix run** | Mechanical | **CREATED 2026-08-03 from `5g3-searchcap`'s recorded friction.** Its first full matrix invocation died on `[warn] apps/web/release/shell.compiled.json — Code style issues found`, `FULL_MATRIX_FAILED rc=1`, because `apps/web/scripts/compile-app-release.ts` writes JSON that Prettier then reformats. The generated bytes are semantically identical — the packet recorded the raw intermediate digest `bf3385dd…` against the formatted final — so **a run was spent on a whitespace difference**. Every artifact-regenerating packet pays this, and the failure arrives *after* the performance gate rather than immediately. **Fix at the source: the generator emits repository-formatted output**, so `--check` and the committed bytes agree by construction. The control is the one this row exists for: regenerate, run the format check, and observe it green **without a manual `--write`** — and a negative control that a deliberately unformatted emission still fails, so the fix cannot be a widened exemption. Cheap, mechanical, and it removes a recurring 11-18 minute tax. |
| cycle-fixture | **Two tests are 27% of the matrix — share their activation the way `5g3-journeysplit` did** | Behavioral | **CREATED 2026-08-03 from measurement, not suspicion.** Parsing every `duration_ms` in the full matrix at `7791bcfb`: total measured test time is **15.8 min against ~18 min wall**, so the matrix is test-bound and there is almost no tooling overhead to reclaim. Two tests in `test/postgres/composed-application.test.ts` dominate — **`composed product activates through the kernel and persists tenant-scoped gateway data` at 150.2 s** and **`composed product advances an existing deployment to an exact compiled successor` at 136.2 s** — **4.8 minutes, 27% of all test time, in two tests**. Both build a full composed application from scratch. **`5g3-journeysplit` already solved this exact shape** in the browser suite: a worker-scoped fixture owning one container, one application and one seeded posting across four tests, after which **the suite got faster, 119.38 s → 108.64 s**, while gaining tests. **The controls that packet used are the charter for this one:** assertion parity proven by byte-identical normalized matcher multisets rather than counted, and order independence proven by selecting any test alone and still getting a fresh fixture. **Note this is already-optimized ground** — `5g3-activation-cost` cut activation 249.6 s → 134.6 s by caching the pinned artifact set, and `5g3-freshtenant` cut install from unbounded to 17.4 s. That both tests still cost ~2.5 minutes each after those wins is the reason to look again, not a reason to assume nothing is left. |
| cycle-pg | **Re-test the PostgreSQL serial pin — `leak-guard` may have removed its reason** | Behavioral | **CREATED 2026-08-03 after measuring cycle time.** A full matrix is **11-18 minutes** and `test:postgres` — 157 tests — is the long pole. It runs `--test-concurrency=1`, pinned serial because concurrent runs produced a SIGTERM and a phantom `false !== true` that passed in isolation. **That diagnosis pointed at container contention, and `leak-guard` has since made container lifecycle survive abnormal death and added pre-lock and post-lock host guards.** The pin's stated reason may no longer hold. This row does not assume it: it **re-measures**, restoring concurrency behind a flag and running the suite enough times to distinguish a real flake from a clean run. **The negative control is the point** — a single green concurrent run proves nothing, because the original failure was intermittent. If the flake reproduces, the pin stays and the row closes with the evidence recorded, which is worth having written down rather than inherited. Not urgent, but it is the only structural cut to matrix time available, and cycle time is now the program's binding constraint rather than correctness. |

| rollback-release-edge | **Rollback authorizes a release-level operation with revision-level parentage** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-06, ruled as [ADR-0047](../decisions/ADR-0047-the-compiler-semantic-profile-is-the-projection-evolution-axis.md) §6. Not blocking `proj-disc-impl`, which owes only the named refusal.** `authorizeReverseTransitionIfApplicable` (`release-reverse-transition-policy.ts`) decides rollback eligibility from `app_package_revisions.parent_revision_id`. A tenant release is a *compilation*; a package revision is a *source*. For every edge that changed the source those coincide, which is why this was invisible until §4's same-source entries separated them — two profile siblings share one revision, so the SQL asks whether a revision is its own parent and rollback refuses on a correct, adjacent edge. **Do not fix it by keying the revision on the profile:** that would put a compilation fact in the source table and break the `normalizedDefinitionDigest` coupling at `release-repository.ts:704`. The fix is to authorize from release lineage. **This touches the release safety valve's authorization, so it gets its own packet and its own review** — the reason `proj-disc-impl` was forbidden from absorbing it. **BINDING CRITERION added 2026-08-06: the post-authorization fallback misnames every cause, not just profile edges.** After the index check at `composed-application-runtime.ts:367` passes, any `null` from `authorizeReverseTransitionIfApplicable` falls through to `ROLLBACK_TARGET_NOT_IMMEDIATE_PREDECESSOR` — and the target **is** the immediate predecessor, since the index check already established it. `proj-disc-impl` carved one accurate code out of that fallback (`ROLLBACK_ACROSS_PROFILE_ONLY_EDGE`) but the residue still lies about itself, and the honest condition is closer to *"the immediate predecessor has no reverse revision edge, and the profiles are equal."* **This is why the refusal failed review three rounds running** — rounds 1, 2 and 3 of `proj-disc-impl` each found a defect in it, which under `review-tiers`' two-REVISE cap means the charter was mis-scoped rather than the code wrong. The class moves here wholesale. **Give every post-index-check refusal a cause it can defend, or reject a same-source/same-profile duplicate lineage earlier under an accurate lineage-integrity code** — ADR-0046 and [ADR-0047](../decisions/ADR-0047-the-compiler-semantic-profile-is-the-projection-evolution-axis.md) §6 both forbid calling an established immediate predecessor "not immediate". **A third instance, found 2026-08-06 by the lane checking a line number the orchestrator and the reviewer both relayed without verifying.** `test/postgres/composed-application.test.ts:804` is a *pre-existing* test from `d9ddfbc fix(api): refuse empty rollback selectors`, and it asserts the same `ROLLBACK_TARGET_NOT_IMMEDIATE_PREDECESSOR` for an **empty rollback selector** — which is not "the target is not the immediate predecessor" either; there is no target at all. So the fallback now has three known callers whose stated cause is false: the empty selector, the profile-only edge (carved out by `proj-disc-impl`), and the same-revision/same-profile edge. **Enumerate every path that reaches it before renaming any of them** — the count keeps growing because each round found one rather than looking for all. **BINDING CRITERION added 2026-08-06 — the refusal has no observable execution site, and that is why ADR-0047 §6's negative direction could not be proven in four rounds.** A test cannot distinguish *"the classifier was reached and correctly declined the profile-only name"* from *"the request was rejected earlier by the index guard"*, because both throw the same `ReleaseReverseTransitionRefusal` class and the early guard's code merely differs from the profile-only one. Pinning the old exact code did not help either: production emits it from **both** the index guard and the post-authorization fallback, so it was restrictive without being probative. **The fix is production observability, not a better assertion** — either an execution counter invoked once after reverse authorization returns `null` and before the profile comparison, carrying source and target release ids, revision ids and profile versions; or an accurate structured `origin`/`phase` field on the refusal identifying the execution site rather than restating the semantic code. Matching a message or a stack frame stays a proxy and is not acceptable. **`proj-disc-impl` therefore ships §6's positive direction proven and its negative direction declared-unproven** — the condition is correct in source (`targetProfileVersion !== activeProfileVersion`, verified by the orchestrator) and fires with a recorded red when the branch is stripped; what is unobservable is which refusal site executed. Closing that is this row's, alongside the three false-name callers already listed.
| U9 | **Visual-conformance gate program** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-06. Filed rather than forcing U2 to a third REVISE round** — the two-REVISE cap says a third round on one class means the charter was mis-scoped, and proving the visual system holds is a larger job than extracting the token layer ever was. U2's confirm review graded four gates RENAMED, not closed, and each needs a different instrument. **Token provenance:** computed-value comparison cannot distinguish a literal equal to the token from consuming the token — the fix is to *mutate* `--status-<role>-ink` at runtime to a novel value and assert the rendered chip follows, which a hardcoded literal cannot. **Painted perceivability:** the current gate reads DOM `textContent` and `::before` `content`, so `font-size:0` and a transparent dot both pass; ADR §5 calls colour + dot + word non-negotiable and this needs pixel-level or effective-paint observation. **Dark direction:** the predicate passes when *either* channel changes, admitting a white ground with dark ink in dark mode, which inverts ADR §10. **Parser grammar:** `parseComputedColor` extracts the first three numbers from any string, fabricating RGB from `oklch()` or `color(display-p3 …)`; refuse unrecognised grammars. **Also unproven and unowned:** ADR §§2–4 palette identity (gates enforce contrast and distance from the *current* brand, never the approved values), §6's two-weight limit, §9's motion contract beyond the shimmer's name and duration, and §10's non-status dark roles. §7 and §8 have no gate at all. **Two more members of the class, from `U2-fix-b`'s review 2026-08-06 — this is why the class moved here wholesale rather than taking a fourth correction in the U2 chain.** **(a) Computed outline is not painted outline.** The gate reads `getComputedStyle().outlineColor/Style/Width` after focusing, which is a real improvement — it catches `outline-width:0` — but those are *resolved* values. `.record-link{opacity:0!important}` leaves all three unchanged, keeps the layout box, keeps the element focusable, and keeps `renderedColor === declaredRing`, while the link and its ring are invisible. Ancestor opacity, clipping, masking and occlusion separate the same way. The code calls this *painted*; it has moved from declared style to computed style, which is a better proxy and still a proxy. **(b) A reachable hidden state is skipped and only logged.** The four subjects with no layout box are one subject — the `Clear selection` button (`component-registry.ts:384`), `.bulk-ready`, matched by two selectors across two viewports. It is `display:none` at `surface-runtime.ts:1140` and `display:inline-grid` at `:1142` the moment a record checkbox is checked, so it is a real focus target one interaction away. `subjectsNotRendered` is `console.log`'d at `composed-application.spec.ts:224` and never asserted — not its count, not its identity, not any eventual rendered state. So `.bulk-ready{outline-width:0!important}` is invisible: the button is skipped while hidden, the generic `.secondary-action` and `button` selectors stay 'covered' by other visible buttons, and `selectorsWithoutSubject` does not move. **Drive the state and measure it; counting a skipped subject is not observing it.** **BINDING ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, added 2026-08-06: every `FOCUS_RING_*` branch ships an executed red.** Four branches still have none — `FOCUS_RING_UNFOCUSABLE`, `FOCUS_RING_GROUND_UNRESOLVED`, `FOCUS_RING_NON_OPAQUE`, and the opacity refusal inside the contrast path — found by the lane while verifying `U2-fix-b`'s cascade finding and **reported rather than absorbed**, which was the correct call. **This is a routing correction the orchestrator owes:** `FOCUS_RING_CASCADE` lacking a control was ruled a *new* defect and fixed inside `U2-fix-b`, so these four are the same shape and cannot consistently be filed as the observation-proxy class. They are named here rather than left to a fifth correction in the U2 chain, and named individually rather than carried as prose, because the review that found the first one warned that an unlisted obligation is lost in a handoff. A branch never seen to fail is not evidence — that is the rule this entire chain has been about, and U9 does not close until all five have an executed red, the fifth being the one `ea6f3cb` already shipped. **PERCEPTIBILITY RESIDUE added 2026-08-06 from `U6a`'s final review, with the list the reviewer supplied — and the point is that `U6a`'s declared limits are OVERBROAD, not that they are absent.** `message-catalog.spec.ts:88-99` says every remaining case needs pixel sampling or an accessibility-tree read. Several do not: **`content-visibility:hidden`, a transparent computed text colour and a simple `filter: opacity(0)` are directly inspectable through computed style; viewport displacement is the element rectangle intersected with the viewport; ancestor clipping is bounded by intersecting rectangles for clipping ancestors; occlusion is sampleable by hit testing** (though proving every glyph unobscured is materially harder). **Only text painted on an indistinguishable ground, and complete `clip-path`/`filter` effects, genuinely need a stronger visual or accessibility observation.** Also here: the ancestor walk uses `parentElement`, so a node inside a shadow root never reaches its host — a shadow-aware census continues through `getRootNode().host`. Not a current defect, since the renderer emits ordinary HTML. And refusing on any `visibility:hidden` ancestor is conservative — a descendant may restore `visibility:visible` — which can cause a false red, never a vacuous green, so it is safe as shipped.

| U5b | **Compiled disclosure tier** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-06 — the one part of M1 with an executable gate today, ~1–1.5 lane-days.** Compiler emits `always` · `progressive` · `onDemand` per field and section (proposal §3.2), with the hard rule that anything required, anything resolving to `blocked` or `attention`, and anything the user must act on is `always`. **Run it against an unadopted `v2`, exactly as `U5-design` measured** — cut `v2`, gate the field on it, leave adoption on `v1`; `check:app-release` stays green and no lineage entry is minted. Adoption is a separate, schedulable event once fields have accumulated. **Three things the design pass could not settle and this packet must:** the carrier granularity (§3.2 wants per-field-and-section; the model has neither), whether the `blocked`/`attention` half of the hard rule is expressible at all (as written it asks the compiler to predict a runtime value, which it cannot), and what the narrowed rule should be instead. **Gate tolerance is 0 px if geometry is ever compared** — `--space-1` is 4 px and the hairline 1 px, so any band wide enough to matter is blind to the smallest defect, and 0 is achievable because both renders come from one definition through one runtime into one engine. Note `grid-column: span 12` is near-universal, so comparing x and width is vacuous and needs its own recorded red. **RULING 2026-08-06, and it reverses the orchestrator's earlier one.** `U5b` takes **option (1)**: revert the `always` declaration on `party_detail`, leave all four `disclosureTier` obligations **exempt** on the existing unused-shape rationale (*"no first-party instance… conditional on remaining unused"*), regenerate the coverage decision document with 4 exempt rather than 2, and freeze with the compiler half intact and the lineage undisturbed. **Why the earlier ruling was wrong:** it preferred observation to exemption on the reasoning that `always` is the default the hard rule leans on — sound in general, but it assumed the declaration would carry a compiled consequence. The lane measured that it does not: `disclosureTier` appears in **zero artifacts** of either lineage entry because `v2` is unadopted, so the eight differing artifacts are digest churn. The entry buys three ledger observations for a fact invisible in every artifact, and costs a rewrite of fresh-tenant install evidence inside `rollback-release-edge`'s subject area. **The obligation moves, it is not dropped:** the `v2` adoption packet declares `always` on a real slot, where the same one line carries a compiled difference and both affected assertions are corrected once against a delta that means something. Recorded as [ADR-0047](../decisions/ADR-0047-the-compiler-semantic-profile-is-the-projection-evolution-axis.md) §4a.
| ladder-trigger | **The skeleton deferral's trigger has no executing gate** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-06, raised by `U5-design` after its own lane closed.** [ADR-0032](../decisions/ADR-0032-feedback-ladder-and-loading-states.md) §2a defers skeleton geometry until *"the recorded counter reports a registered read in the 1 s – 3 s band"*. **Nothing observes that.** `U1`'s own `runtime-slos.md` row says so: the counter is in-process, `apps/api` composes the surface server alone, and one test reads it. `op-latency` does not cover it either — that row instruments the *operation* gateway, it does not expose the reads. **So the deferral currently has no way to end**, which is a declared rule with no executing gate — the shape AGENTS.md §6 exists to close and the same shape as the origin-sync rule that lapsed for 637 commits before anything noticed. **Cheapest honest fix: something that fails when a recorded read lands above the Doherty band**, so the trigger fires by going red rather than by someone remembering to look. Small. It does not reopen the deferral — it makes it reversible rather than permanent.
| shimmer-trajectory | **Observe the shimmer's sweep, not just its timing** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-06, re-routed from `U5` by [ADR-0032](../decisions/ADR-0032-feedback-ladder-and-loading-states.md) §2a — the orchestrator's original routing was wrong and kept this unproven across two packets.** `U2`'s gate reads `animationName`, `animationDuration` and `animationTimingFunction`, so a `translateX(-20%) → translateX(20%)` keyframe passes while ADR-0035 §9 says the sheen begins fully left and travels past the right edge. **Its subject is the stylesheet, which already ships** — no skeleton needs to render. `U5-design` named the mechanism without executing it: pause through `getAnimations()` and read the `::after` transform at t=0 and t=duration. Rides `U9` or takes its own slot; it does not wait on skeletons.
| U6 | ~~Compiled message catalog (M3)~~ — **DESIGNED, ruled as [ADR-0048](../decisions/ADR-0048-the-message-catalog-is-platform-vocabulary-held-in-code.md); split into `U6a`/`U6b`** | Behavioral | **Design pass done 2026-08-06.** Its headline finding corrected this row's own premise: the user-facing text of a failure is **not** the machine code — two hand-written copy tables already exist and **12 of 27 codes are registered**, so U6 finishes a half-built mechanism rather than building one. **The catalog is held in code, not compiled**, which makes U6 independent of `U5-design`'s `v2` measurement under either outcome. Four rounds, every finding the observation-versus-proxy class. **Unblocks `U7`** on its own — U7 needs a registered sentence, not a placement vocabulary.
| U6b | **Placement, empty states and success weighting** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-06, after `U6a`.** Implements [ADR-0048](../decisions/ADR-0048-the-message-catalog-is-platform-vocabulary-held-in-code.md) §4. Registers `page` · `slot`; **refuses `toast` and `modal` by name with a diagnostic**, each recording what would admit it; wires slot placement to U4's existing `slotResolutionState()` rather than deriving a parallel fact; replaces the single global `emptyDataPanel()` sentence with per-code empty states; weights success confirmations per ADR-0032 §5 in place of the global `feedbackHtml()` template. **Split from `U6a` deliberately** — `U6a` is a mechanical substitution whose gate is string equality, `U6b` changes where and whether things render, and bundling would put a high-review-value behaviour change behind a 27-file substitution diff. **CHARTER CORRECTION 2026-08-06 — `U6b` is NOT covered by `U6a`'s gate 'by construction', and both the lane and the orchestrator asserted otherwise before a reviewer checked.** `messageAttributes()` has exactly one call site; `feedbackHtml()` and `emptyDataPanel()` are separate literal helpers that never call it, and it always emits `data-diagnostic-code` while deriving only `attention`/`blocked` — so it is not a neutral success/empty API today. **The cheapest escaping tree is `U6b`'s most likely implementation:** register the success and empty copy, render it through the existing helpers, leave those treatments unmarked, and account for reachability through the specs that already drive them. `U6a`'s synthetic census can synthesize any catalog code through `renderApplicationDiagnostic()` and stay green while the real production treatment never enters the gate. **So `U6b` owes real-path observations of the actual success and empty renderers**, plus validation that a success treatment carries `role="status"` — `U6a`'s observer records `role` and deliberately does not assert it, which proves selector neutrality and nothing about the real treatments.
| msg-code-accuracy | **Six gateway mappings and one code name the wrong thing** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-06, found by `U6a` and reported rather than folded in — correctly, since fixing them moves `data-diagnostic-code` values that existing browser assertions pin.** (1) **`INVALID_SURFACE_BINDING` is reachable from no path under its own name**: `readCompiledSurfaceDataBinding` throws it and `surface-runtime.ts:113` catches and renders `QUERY_UNSUPPORTED`. A code-accuracy defect, not a copy one. (2) **Six gateway-error mappings are inaccurate**, each marked `INACCURATE` in place with the code it should carry — clearest is `UnsupportedSemanticAggregateQueryError` → `QUERY_UNAVAILABLE`, where the class name says *unsupported*; also `MalformedPinnedQueryCatalogError`, `SharedListContractError`, `InvalidResolveByNameContractError`, `MalformedPinnedOperationCatalogError`, `NoSuchRegisteredCapabilityError`. **`U6a` preserved behaviour exactly so it stayed a substitution**, which is why this is a separate packet with its own gate rather than a rider on `U6b`. Also here: `app-server.ts` sets no `Allow` header on its 405, a distinction that belongs in the header rather than in duplicated copy.
| lock-owner | **`run-with-test-lock.mjs` logs the waiter, never the owner** | Critical | **RE-TIERED 2026-08-08 from Mechanical.** Both rows were forecast on a one-line scope -- *write the holder's pid into the lock file so the next starvation names its cause.* The packet that implemented them, `lock-obs`, shipped a 175-line lock wrapper, a 365-line registry, a new suite runner, changed acquisition modes on every test entry point, and a **new invariant**: an inherited claim authorizes work only if path, pid, start ticks, liveness, holding state and mode all validate. `review-tiers` puts new or changed invariants at Critical wherever they live, and tier follows the final diff. Five review arms; four found real defects and three would have defeated the packet's purpose. The tier was right in practice and wrong on the row. **OPEN 2026-08-06, and it has already cost a diagnosis.** `U6a`'s first matrix aborted at `test:compiler` with `TEST_GATE_LOCK_BUSY` after 300 s waiting for *shared* access — which blocks only on an exclusive holder, and nothing in the matrix takes one before that point. So an external exclusive holder existed and **is now permanently unidentifiable**: the process was gone, `ps` and `fuser` were clean, and the wrapper records the waiter and not the owner. Small fix — write the holder's pid and label into the lock file so the next starvation names its cause. **Do not widen `NORTH_STAR_TEST_LOCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`**; `lanes.md` forbids it, and the lane correctly re-ran quiet instead of enlarging what starvation is permitted to look like.
| ux-picker | **Every form field is a bare text box — there are no pickers, so a relation is typed as a UUID** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, from a user-level audit against a $25M distributor's needs.** `component-registry.ts:721` renders every field identically as `<label><span>{label}</span><input name="value:{fieldId}" autocomplete="off"></label>` — **no `select`, no `datalist`, no `type`, no `required`, no validation.** A supplier reference, a date, a quantity and a status enum are the same control. **No reference or relation renderer exists at all**, so linking a purchase order to a supplier means typing a raw UUID. **Design constraint that decides the shape:** ADR-0036 authorises exactly four client behaviours and a scripted combobox is none of them — **`<datalist>` gives native typeahead with zero JavaScript** and is the obvious carrier. Owed with it: an **add-new affordance inside the picker** (today `New` exists only on list headers, so you can create a customer from the customer list but not while writing an order), and field **types** derived from the canonical field kind rather than free text everywhere. |
| ux-list-usability | **Lists show UUIDs, render no search box, and paginate forward only** | Behavioral | **CLOSED 2026-08-20 by accepted `inventory-surface-legibility`, merge `f2096b7`.** The rendered GET form carries the existing validated `q`, archive and legal-entity scope; Previous uses the runtime's opaque cursor format; row links and selection labels use the compiled display field with UUID only as an absent-display fallback.
