Distributor Item moved `1–100 of 119` → `101–119` → back, and search found the page-two record as `1–1 of 1`. Three separate deletion observations red search, Previous and business identity. Full matrix green at `58e5ac1`. |
| ux-clutter | **Fifteen eyebrow labels, and the worst leak implementation vocabulary to the operator** | Behavioral | **CLOSED 2026-08-20 by accepted `inventory-surface-legibility`, merge `f2096b7`.** The Task renderer emits only authored title and compiled statuses; the final heading assertion reads its complete normalized text, so a bare direct text node is in the census. The prescribed Task-only bare `runtime release` mutation moved the focused journey 1/1 pass → 0/1 pass and restoration returned 1/1 pass. Authored labels remain separate from canonical query/record identity across List, Form, Record, breadcrumb, compatibility List, relation picker and relation freeze. Full matrix green at `58e5ac1`. |
| required-relation-uncreatable | **A module with a required relation cannot be created from any web surface** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-09, measured by `ux-picker` while pricing `ux-reference-picker`. This blocks `PUR-1` directly.** `operationInput` (`surface-runtime.ts:658`) builds `{recordId, values}` and **never posts relations**, while the provider refuses a non-nullable relation with `MODULE_REQUIRED_RELATION_MISSING` (`module-runtime-interpreter.ts:815`). **A purchase order needs a supplier**, so `PUR-1` cannot create one from the web at all if that relation is required. **It has never fired only because no first-party module declares a required relation yet.** **This refutes the `ux-picker` row's framing twice:** it is not that a user types a UUID — **there is no wire for a relation at all** — and a working server-side record picker already ships for legal entity at `surface-runtime.ts:555-634`, which locates a list surface by `sourceEntityId`, re-invokes the query gateway inside the same request, derives labels from `displayFieldId`, and renders a `<select>`. **The gap is the wire, not the renderer**, and the only missing input is the target entity id. **PREMISE CORRECTED 2026-08-12 by `ux-reference-picker`, and it makes this worse rather than better.** This row said the defect *"has never fired only because no first-party module declares a required relation yet."* **That is false.** The shipped `apps/web/release/app.compiled.json` already carries five required relations — `party_role_party`, `inventory_transaction_line_transaction`, `stock_count_transaction`, `stock_count_line_session`, `stock_count_line_transaction_line` — and **four of their entities carry a form surface** (`party_role_form`, `inventory_transaction_line_form`, `stock_count_form`, `stock_count_line_form`). `party_role` is an ordinary first-party entity mounted since G2 whose create form **has never been able to succeed on `main`**. This is a live defect in shipped surfaces, not a latent one awaiting a first declarer. **Consequence for evidence:** no fixture was authored, because **two independent specimens already existed** — first-party `party_role`, and `master_role_parent` in `test/fixtures/g2/module-conformance/definitions.ts`, which declared `required: true` before this row was written. An author-chosen specimen would have been strictly weaker. **PREMISE CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-08-13 by the orchestrator at `3509ce8`, and this row's central measurement is now STALE: `operationInput` DOES post relations.** `relation-contract-integrity` (`87c667a`) landed `RELATION_SUBMISSION_PREFIX = 'relation:'` and a `relationInput` reader, and `operationInput`'s create branch now returns `{recordId, relations: relationInput(submission), values}` (`apps/web/src/surface-runtime.ts`, re-locate by symbol). **So the wire exists.** What does not exist is anything that fills it: `grep -n relationInputs apps/web/src/*.ts` returns hits in `surface-contract.ts` **only** — `surface-runtime.ts` and `component-registry.ts` never read the relation authority, `renderFormFields` iterates `surface.fieldIds` alone, and no control is rendered whose name starts with `relation:`. **`EntityRelationAuthority` therefore has ZERO consumers**, which means packet 1's ergonomics remain untested by use exactly as its own row warned. **And the renderer cannot be written yet** — see the profile-v2 finding in the TRIAGE section above. **The remaining defect is unchanged in effect and different in location: it is now a missing renderer plus an unadopted profile, not a missing wire.** |
| relation-update-fork | **Relations are create-only, all the way down** | Behavioral | **VISIBILITY HALF CLOSED 2026-08-20 by accepted `inventory-surface-legibility`, merge `f2096b7`; the create-only operation rule is unchanged.** Update forms show a `role="note"` headed `Locked after creation`, name each relation in business vocabulary, and state it is chosen during creation and cannot be changed later. The whole-disclosure deletion and divergent-identity relation-freeze reversion both red. Full matrix green at `58e5ac1`. |
| adoption-reidentifies-evidence | **A language adoption re-identifies 69 of 163 verification scenarios while changing none of them** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-09, measured by `LANG-ADOPT-v5` and the orchestrator owes this row.** Adopting `v5` **re-identified 69 of 163 verification scenarios and changed the behaviour of none**; the profile-only edge re-identifies zero. **So durable evidence keyed by scenario id only partially survives an adoption.** `PUR-2` and `SAL-2` will both key posting evidence that way — receipts, change documents and trust records — so a later adoption silently orphans a fraction of it while every count and every behaviour holds. **This is the same shape as the coverage ledger's own lesson from the same packet: a count that holds is not evidence that nothing moved.** Owed before `PUR-2` freezes an evidence key: rule whether scenario identity must be adoption-stable, or whether evidence must carry the language version it was recorded under so a re-identification is detectable rather than silent. |
| adopt-constant-illusion | **Moving `ADOPTED_LANGUAGE_VERSION` alone leaves every gate green and adopts nothing** | Critical | **CLOSED 2026-08-10 — the owed control exists and the orchestrator verified it rather than taking the lane's word.** `canonical-contracts-purity.test.ts:391-403` asserts `normalizeApplicationPackage(composedApplicationDefinition()).languageVersion === ADOPTED_LANGUAGE_VERSION`, with a **second arm for `platformModuleDefinition()`** because platform is a first-party module outside the composed package and the first assertion cannot see it — and node-version purity is precisely what forces all five modules together, so a guard covering four of them does not observe the rule. **It compares the definition's own normalized bytes against the constant, so moving either side alone produces an observed disagreement.** Both directions measured. **The row's refinement, from the closing lane and accepted:** the misleading green was never a missing control — it is `check:app-release`, which is *correct* and simply irrelevant to this fact, because `compileNormalizedDefinition` reads the version from the definition's bytes. **So the row was about where the fact is observable, not whether it is enforced**, and the answer is that it is observable in the architecture suite and invisible in the release check. The original framing follows. **OPEN 2026-08-08, measured by `LANG-ADOPT-v5` and it refutes this queue's own charter for that row.** The charter said adoption *"moves every release root and mints a lineage entry"* and implied the constant is the event. **Both halves are wrong.** Moving the constant alone leaves `check:app-release` green with **byte-identical artifacts** — verified with `--check` and with the writer — because `compileNormalizedDefinition` takes the version from the **definition's own bytes**, not from the constant. **A lane that moves only the constant ships an adoption that looks done and is not.** And *"every release root moves"* is false on two of three: the bootstrap and shell roots **held**. **The real event is the five module definitions plus `app/builder.ts` moving together**, which is what actually re-serialises the authored bytes. **Owed: a control that reds when the constant and the definitions disagree**, so the half-done state cannot be green. **This is the third instance of the class `5g3-sm-impl` named** — a fact stated in one place and enforced from another — and it is the most dangerous form yet, because the failing state is *silent green* rather than a misnamed refusal. |
| test-inventory-third-copy | **Three inventories pin one test-file list, and the one that runs last is the one nothing checks** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, measured by `ux-picker` at the cost of a full 20-minute matrix.** Registering one compiler test requires it in **three** places: `package.json`'s script, `repository-hygiene`'s reviewed inventory, and `test/helpers/reachability-producers.ts`. The lane updated two. `repository-hygiene` compares `package.json` against the filesystem and **passed while `reachability-producers.ts` disagreed with both** — and `check:language-coverage`, which reads the third, runs **last, after `test:postgres`**. So a one-line omission costs twenty minutes to discover. Integration was unaffected only because it is registered there as a **glob**, which is why exactly one file broke and the failure looked specific rather than structural. **This is the same class as `adopt-constant-illusion` and `5g3-sm-impl`'s five version omissions — a fact stated in one place and enforced from another — with the added cost that the enforcing gate is the slowest one in the matrix.** Owed, and cheap: **derive one inventory from another, or deep-equal them against each other in the architecture suite**, which runs in the first five minutes. Do not fix by adding a fourth place to remember. |
| matrix-must-pin-its-tree | **A matrix runner that does not pin the tree it measures can measure another lane's uncommitted work** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, and `ux-picker` spent two matrix windows proving it.** Lanes share `/home/rvham/2rain-greenfield`; every other lane has a detached worktree and `ux-picker` did not. Its gate poller waited nineteen minutes for a genuinely quiet machine, took the window at 07:41:18 — and by then another lane had switched the shared checkout to `packet/ux-reference-picker`. **The matrix ran `f378114` with a foreign lane's uncommitted work in it** and died in five seconds at `check:app-release` with `COMPILER_HISTORICAL_REPRODUCTION_MISMATCH` on lineage entry 0. **The runner printed the SHA it was testing into its own log header and ran anyway** — the lane's words, and they are the exact AGENTS.md §6 shape: *it recorded the evidence of its own vacuity and did not act on it. A gate that observes without refusing is not a gate.* **Fix is two `rev-parse` calls, one before the run and one after, with a mismatch as a refusal rather than a note.** **Both independently-written pollers on this machine gate on the lock and the machine but not on the tree**, so this is a property of the convention, not of one lane. **Second finding, same incident and worth its own attention:** two lanes polled the same gate with no visibility of each other, having written near-identical scripts in the same hour, both in scratchpads and neither in the tree. `lock-obs` leases *suites*; **nothing leases intent**, so a lane cannot see that another is about to take the window. Both behaved correctly and one still wasted ten minutes. **A committed poller with a declared queue would cost little**, and it is the concrete form of what `matrix-preflight-blind` calls unenforceable sequencing. |
| lockout-reads-as-silence | **A lock-out produces no test output, and a grep for failures over nothing looks exactly like a pass** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, caught by `LANG-ADOPT-v5` one step before it banked the silence as a measurement.** A targeted run under `run-with-test-lock.mjs` while another lane holds the lock exits `TEST_GATE_LOCK_BUSY` **with no test output at all**, and the lane's `grep -E '^not ok'` over that returned empty — **indistinguishable from a clean run.** It was caught only because *"no output looked wrong"*, which is judgement, not a gate. **This is the same shape as `matrix-evidence-truncated` and as the orchestrator's own `echo "pushed $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)"`: a reporting harness whose failure mode is a false green.** The lane's fix is the right minimum — widen the filter so `LOCK_BUSY` cannot masquerade as silence — **and the first thing that widened filter caught was a deliberate `LOCK_BUSY` from `test-lock-observability`'s own provoked controls**, which is the useful discrimination: the *count* carries signal, the alerts are a prompt to go look. **Owed generally: any harness that greps for failures must first prove the payload ran.** An empty result set is not evidence until non-emptiness of the input is established — the same lesson as `scan-subject-discovered-by-glob`, one layer up. |
| dev-environment | **Nobody can open the application and look at it — every human view of a data surface goes through a test harness** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, found by the orchestrator trying to answer "can I open the app right now" and getting it wrong first.** `apps/web` ships `pnpm dev` (`demo-server.ts`), which is hardcoded to `apps/web/release/shell.compiled.json` and serves **four shell surfaces** — home, setup, unsupported, error. **No inventory, catalog, party or location surface is reachable from it**, so no form control, no list, no picker and no write path has ever been looked at by a human outside a Playwright run. Pointing it at `app.compiled.json` does not work and the reason is structural, not a config gap: the shell artifact carries `projections`/`releaseRoot`, which is the shape `readDemoCompiledFixture` consumes, while `app.compiled.json` carries `applications`/`bootstrap` — eight lineage entries whose shape is `artifacts`/`nodeContracts`/`releaseManifest`. **There is no projection in the application artifact for the demo reader to consume.** **The mechanism to fix it already exists four times over and is test-only:** `test/fixtures/g2/{catalog,location,party,saved-filter}/runtime-harness.ts` each import `createSurfaceRuntimeServer` from `apps/web/src/app-server.js` and stand the full Postgres stack up behind it. **They are per-module, unseeded, and spawned only by a Playwright spec.** **Why this is Critical rather than convenience:** `PUR-1` hands purchase orders to an office worker, and the standing UX rulings — searchable pickers above five options, no clutter, add-new affordances — are judgements about *appearance and feel* that no assertion settles. **A project that cannot be looked at will discover its usability defects from its users.** It is also how the orchestrator came to assert, wrongly and in the user's presence, that a merged packet's controls were visible when they were not. **Three hazards the fix must not create, each already an open row.** (1) **A long-lived dev Postgres is a container-bearing path**, so it must not trip the stray-container guard, must not contend for the matrix lock, and must not leak volumes — `leak-guard-orphan` records a container outliving its dead owner by an hour, and `matrix-machine-decay` prices what leaked volumes cost. **A dev container that outlives its session is the same defect, not a new one.** (2) **A dev environment must never become a second authority for how the application is composed.** The compiled release is the authority; a harness that hand-assembles a variant reintroduces exactly the per-module platform code the factory thesis exists to delete. (3) **Seed data must be authored where the modules are**, not in a fixture that drifts. **AND THE NAMING IS PART OF THE DEFECT, not a cosmetic afterthought.** What `pnpm dev` serves is the human-visible arm of a **compiler fixture**: `shell.authored.json` is a real authored application package containing only the shell, compiled through the same `compileApplication` path as everything else, and it exists to prove the shell is compiled rather than hand-written, to be gated against drift by `check:demo-release`, and to give the renderer a database-free fixture (`apps/web/test/helpers.ts`). **All three are legitimate. None of them is 'let a person try the application.'** But `dev` and `demo` both promise exactly that, and the orchestrator read those names and told the user a freshly merged packet's form controls would be visible in it. **A name that claims more than the thing does is the same failure shape this queue keeps filing in gates** — it simply lives in a script name here. **So the packet owes a rename as well as a build: the fixture server should be named for the fixture it serves, and `dev` should mean the application or not exist.** **NOTE: this packet fires `lease-derivation`'s stated trigger** — *the first packet that adds a new test entry point, a new container-bearing path, or a third registry producer* — so that row's design question arrives with it, and `test-inventory-third-copy` applies to any new test entry point it registers. **MEASURED 2026-08-11 by the `dev-environment` packet, and this row's central claim is REFUTED.** **A composed-application dev server already exists and is not a test harness.** `apps/api` — a workspace package with its own `dev` script — has since `G2-P5e` read `apps/web/release/app.compiled.json`, installed it through `createComposedApplicationRuntime` (`packages/postgres-provider/src/composed-application-runtime.ts`, **2,177 lines of provider-owned production code**), seeded through the real `SemanticOperationGateway`, and served it via `createSurfaceRuntimeServer` with the full gateway set on `http://127.0.0.1:4174`. **The ledger already records a human using it:** `G2-P5e`'s row says the orchestrator "created a Party through the browser form (POST 200, 'Create complete.'), read it back through the list, and confirmed the row directly in PostgreSQL", and four packet records — `G2-P5d-a`, `G2-P5d-a1`, `G2-P5d-b`, `G2-P5d-nav` — carry `pnpm --filter @north-star/api dev` in their "Test it yourself" steps. **Everything this row says about `apps/web`'s `pnpm dev` is correct;** the error was generalising from one package's script to the repository, without examining the other. **The four named harnesses would have been the WRONG mechanism:** they compile *per-module fixtures* (`compileCatalogFixture()` and siblings), so deriving from them would have hand-assembled an application variant out of module fixtures — this row's own constraint (2). **They are also not four instruments:** `catalog` and `location` differ in **46 of 706 lines** after module-name normalisation and `party` is the same machinery again, while `saved-filter` is an unrelated admission-refusal observer — see `harness-triplication`. **What was genuinely broken is filed separately and was fixed:** the dev container was named `north-star-composed-app` (matrix exit 76, or force-removed at one hour with no liveness check), it was never stopped on exit, and the seed rebuilt module identifiers by string concatenation at 12 records. **The rename this row asks for is done:** `apps/web`'s `dev` is now `serve:shell-fixture` and root `dev` runs the application. **Remaining open:** `dev-container-unguarded` and `dev-path-outside-container-census`. **THE TRIGGER NOTE ABOVE IS WITHDRAWN 2026-08-11, and it is withdrawn explicitly because it still reads as live guidance.** This row's closing NOTE said the packet fires `lease-derivation`'s trigger. **It does not.** The packet added no new test entry point (no suite script), no registry producer, and **no new container-bearing path** — `apps/api/src/main.ts` has created a Docker container since `G2-P5e`, so the path pre-dated the packet that was chartered to add one. That is a direct consequence of the refutation above. **`test-inventory-third-copy` DOES apply and was honoured:** the packet registered one new test file, `test/unit/dev-environment.test.ts`, in all three inventories — `package.json`'s `test:unit`, `repository-hygiene`'s reviewed list, and `test/helpers/reachability-producers.ts`. **Round 1 review (REVISE) corrected two claims this row's amendment had overstated; see `docs/execution/packets/dev-environment.md` §4, §5 and §7 for what was wrong and what now has controls.** |
| harness-triplication | **Three of the "four" runtime harnesses are one harness copied three times** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-11, measured by `dev-environment` while looking for a mechanism to derive from.** `test/fixtures/g2/{catalog,location}/runtime-harness.ts` are **706 lines each and differ in 46 lines** after normalising the module name away — 93% identical — and `party` (602 lines) is the same 21 functions in the same order. `saved-filter` (349 lines) is **not a fourth copy**: it observes admission refusal, stands up no server, and exports no `withReal*Runtime`. So the repository has *one* per-module runtime assembly written three times, plus one unrelated file. The duplicated machinery is substantial and identical: `persistSequence`, `admitCandidate`, `federateCandidate`, `revisionCommand`, `releaseCommand`, `setPointer`, `grantExecutorAuthority`, `prepare`, `runtimeEntry`, `issuedView`, `humanActorIssuer`, `trustedContexts`, `migrateAndSeed` and an `AllowPolicy` class. **Not repaired by the finding packet, deliberately:** these files gate `test:postgres` and `test:browser`, so the refactor's risk profile is unrelated to that packet's goal, and a fourth caller was not added — the dev path uses `createComposedApplicationRuntime` instead. **The cost is a per-module edit tax:** a change to the release-install sequence must be made three times, and nothing detects the third being missed. **Note the shape:** `dev-environment` was chartered to derive from these four and would have produced a fifth copy of the wrong abstraction had the production installer not been found first. |
| dev-container-unguarded | **The dev database container has no detached guardian, so `kill -9` leaks it** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-11, filed by `dev-environment` as a stated residual rather than a closed hazard.** `apps/api/src/main.ts` now stops its container on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`, which closes the ordinary case that `leak-guard-orphan` describes. It does **not** survive `kill -9` of the dev process: nothing detached is watching. The existing mechanism, `test/helpers/postgres-container-guardian.mjs`, is process-identity bound (`/proc/<pid>/stat` field 19) and would fit, but **line 17 hard-requires a `north-star-` prefix** — and the dev container is deliberately outside that namespace, because `guard-ephemeral-postgres.mjs` would otherwise make the matrix exit 76. Reusing it therefore means editing the guardian every PostgreSQL test depends on, which the finding packet judged out of proportion to its charter. **The residual is bounded and that is why it was accepted:** ONE named container on ONE fixed port with ONE reused named volume, invisible to the matrix guard, removed by `pnpm --filter @north-star/api dev:stop`. This is categorically unlike the test-container leak `matrix-machine-decay` priced, where every leak is a *new* container and a *new* volume and 481 accumulated to 12.96GB. **The real question for whoever takes this:** whether the guardian's prefix check should become a small allowlist so one guardian serves both callers, or whether a long-lived dev container wants a different instrument entirely. **WIDENED 2026-08-11 by round 1 review — this row understated the leak and the understatement was the defect.** It claimed the ordinary lifecycle was closed and only `kill -9` remained. In fact the signal handlers were installed **after** `docker run` and after a ~60s install, so **Ctrl-C during migration or the 176-record seed, an occupied port 4174, or any database failure all leaked the container** — none of which is `kill -9`. Worse, `application.close()` rejecting **skipped `docker stop` entirely**, and a failed `docker stop` was swallowed by a boolean helper and exited 0, reporting success while leaking. **Fixed and observed:** handlers install before the container starts; the existence flag flips when `docker run` returns rather than when the endpoint is healthy; startup is wrapped and `startComposedApplication` closes its runtime if seeding or `listen` throws; shutdown runs each step independently, reports failures and **exits 1**. Measured: SIGTERM mid-startup → `Exited (137)`; port occupied → `EADDRINUSE`, exit 1, `Exited (0)`; database failure → exit 1, `Exited (0)`. **The remaining residual is genuinely `kill -9` only, and it has no committed control** — the lifecycle observations are by hand and not reproducible from the tree, which is this row's real open work. **CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-08-11 by round 2 review — "genuinely `kill -9` only" was still wrong.** A signal arriving while `docker run --detach` was **in flight** also leaked: ownership was recorded AFTER the awaited command, so shutdown found nothing recorded, skipped cleanup and exited, and the Docker child then finished creating an orphan. Reachable on an image pull, a slow daemon, or any SIGTERM sent to the Node PID rather than the process group — an ordinary startup case, not `kill -9`. **Recording ownership before the command is necessary but NOT sufficient**, which the review said explicitly: the stop would then race ahead of the creation it undoes. **Fixed by waiting the pending create/start out before stopping**, with an absent container treated as success and every other stop failure reaching the exit code. **Now carries committed controls**, which is what this row was really missing: a spawned-process test with a stub `docker` that blocks inside `run`, signals the Node process mid-flight, and asserts no container survives — **red against both the pre-fix shape and against removal of the settle step** — plus direct controls for the rejecting-close and failing-stop branches. **Remaining residual, and it is now genuinely narrow:** `kill -9` leaks, still uncontrolled, and the stub is not the real daemon. |
| dev-path-outside-container-census | **A container-bearing path has been outside the container census since `G2-P5e`** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-11, supplied by `dev-environment` as a concrete specimen for `lease-derivation`.** `apps/api/src/main.ts` runs `execFileAsync('docker', ['run', …])` and has stood up a PostgreSQL container since `G2-P5e`. The census that claims to derive container-bearing entry points, `test/architecture/test-lock-observability.test.ts:39`, matches `containerCallPattern = /\bwithEphemeralPostgres\s*\(/u` — **a test-helper call site** — and its own comment records that "workspace manifests are not scanned here at all". So this path is invisible on **two independent counts**: it is a workspace-manifest script, and it reaches Docker directly rather than through the helper. **This is `lease-derivation`'s abstract claim with a pre-existing instance attached** — *a gate that asserts a necessary condition where its name claims the sufficient one* — and it is exactly what round 3 of that row predicted: "`execFile`, `fork` and any literal non-fixture target are invisible". **The prediction was correct and the instance was already in the tree when it was written.** **Not a regression from the finding packet:** it renamed the container and gave it an ending, but did not create the path. **The design question stays with `lease-derivation`:** the authority for "container-bearing" must be a real process edge, not a helper-call marker, and the census must read workspace manifests or say that it does not. |
| dev-invocation-docs-are-hazardous | **Four packet records tell the user to create a container that breaks every lane's matrix** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-11, found by `dev-environment` while renaming the dev container.** `docs/execution/packets/G2-P5d-a.md:218`, `G2-P5d-a1.md:115`, `G2-P5d-b.md:171` and `G2-P5d-nav.md:161` each carry a "Test it yourself" line of the form `NORTH_STAR_DATABASE_CONTAINER=north-star-g2-…-check … pnpm --filter @north-star/api dev`. **Any user following one of those instructions creates a `north-star-*` container**, and `scripts/run-matrix.sh:88` then refuses with `POSTGRES_CONTAINER_CONTAMINATION` and exit 76 — *after* acquiring the exclusive lock, so the slot is burned for whichever lane was queued. Left running past an hour it is instead `docker rm --force`d by the age sweep, which never checks whether an owner is alive. **Mitigated, not closed:** `main.ts` now refuses any container name beginning `north-star-`, so those commands fail loudly at startup instead of poisoning the matrix, and the env var was renamed to `NORTH_STAR_DEV_DATABASE_CONTAINER` so the old lines set a variable nothing reads. **Owed:** the four records still print instructions that no longer work. Correct them, or accept that packet records are historical and say so once, somewhere, rather than four times implicitly. **CORRECTED 2026-08-11 by round 1 review — the mitigation claimed here was FALSE as first written.** This row said `main.ts` "now refuses any container name beginning `north-star-`, so those commands fail loudly at startup". They did not: the four records set `NORTH_STAR_DATABASE_CONTAINER`, and the packet had **renamed the variable it reads** to `NORTH_STAR_DEV_DATABASE_CONTAINER`, so the old commands were **silently ignored** and ran against the default container — the prefix refusal never saw their value. **Now true, and observed:** `apps/api/src/dev-container.ts` refuses the retired variable outright with a migration diagnostic, and running the recorded `NORTH_STAR_DATABASE_CONTAINER=north-star-g2-p5da-check …` form exits before any container is created (measured: zero containers created). Both refusals carry negative controls. **Still owed, unchanged:** the four records print instructions that no longer work. |
| domain-package-is-not-esm | **`packages/domain` was the only workspace package that was not an ES module, and it made the seed constraint unsatisfiable** | Behavioral | **OPEN-AND-FIXED 2026-08-11 by `dev-environment`; filed because the fix is a bridge into a shared manifest, not because it is unresolved.** `packages/domain/package.json` declared no `"type"`, while `postgres-provider`, `compiler`, `runtime` and `canonical-model` all declare `"type": "module"`. **Nothing surfaced it until seed data was authored beside the modules** — the row's own constraint (3). That makes `apps/api/src/composition-root.ts` import `packages/domain/src/app/seed.ts`, and `apps/web/test/browser/composed-application.spec.ts` imports `composition-root.ts`, so **Playwright pulled `packages/domain` into its transform for the first time**, treated it as CJS, and failed with `Cannot find module './builder.js'` — a `.js` specifier resolved as a literal filename. **Every file in the package uses `.js` specifiers**, so specifier rewriting was not a fix: dropping the extension moved the same failure one level down into `builder.ts`. **The fix is 9 lines** — `"type": "module"` plus `.js` on five re-exports that only resolved because the package was CJS, since `node16` resolution rejects extensionless relative specifiers under ESM. **Two things worth carrying.** **(1)** The defect was latent for the entire life of the package and was reachable only by a *new consumer under a different transform* — `tsx` had been papering over it everywhere, including in the dev server, which ran fine before and after. A gate that runs one loader cannot see this class. **(2)** It changed an observable contract: before the fix a dynamic `import()` of a domain module returned `{ default: … }` rather than its named exports, measured both ways. **Lease note:** `packages/domain/src/index.ts` is held by `packet/pur-1`, which is adding a purchasing export on the adjacent lines; the merge resolution is to keep both. |
| application-ids-is-a-second-spelling | **`APPLICATION_IDS` restates identifiers the modules build privately, and nothing makes them agree** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-11, found by round 1 review of `dev-environment`, which had claimed the opposite.** Each module builds its identifiers through a private `ids(namespace)` factory inside its own `definition.ts`; `packages/domain/src/app/builder.ts` then **re-spells the same strings by hand** in `APPLICATION_IDS`. The module definitions do not consume it. So renaming `field.location_code` in `location/definition.ts` leaves `APPLICATION_IDS.location.fieldIds.code` type-correct, and every consumer of that table — the demo seed, and the dev server's printed surface URLs — keeps compiling against a name the application no longer has. **The packet's claim that the seed was "bound to the module declarations" and would "break at the type level" was false**, and is corrected in its record. **Partially mitigated, not closed:** `test/unit/dev-environment.test.ts` now checks every seeded operation, field and enum value, plus the three printed surface IDs, against the **compiled release** — the authority neither table can move — with negative controls per vector. That makes a drift *detected*; it does not make it *unrepresentable*. **The real fix is to derive `APPLICATION_IDS` from the module `ids()` factories**, which means exporting them and rewriting `builder.ts`. **Not taken because `builder.ts` is held by `packet/pur-1`**, which is adding a purchasing module to that same table — and which will therefore add a fifth hand-spelled entry unless this lands first. **Worth noticing:** the enum *option* IDs are a third copy — the Location module generates them from `['warehouse','Warehouse']` while the seed built `:option.warehouse` by concatenation. |
| main-is-format-and-lint-red | ~~**`main` cannot pass its own matrix**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-13** | Critical | **DONE. Re-measured at `dea42f7` on 2026-08-13: `pnpm format` GREEN, `pnpm lint` GREEN, `prettier` pinned exactly at `3.9.5` in `package.json`.** Closed by `matrix-unblock`. **The residue is real and moves to `unrun-quality-gates`:** the pin is correct and ungated, so reverting `3.9.5` to a range leaves every gate green until the next lockfile refresh silently re-arms the exact trap that cost several days. `matrix-unblock`'s lane offered a `fmt-pin-gate` charter for that, **it was never filed, and it is not being filed as a 135th row** — it belongs to `unrun-quality-gates`, which already owns the "nothing runs or guards these" question. That lane also reported failing to build the gate three times, which is evidence the guard is harder than it looks and worth its predecessors' notes. ORIGINAL TEXT FOLLOWS. **OPEN 2026-08-11, measured by `dev-environment` at the cost of one matrix slot, and it blocks every lane.** `scripts/run-matrix.sh:186-188` runs `format && lint && typecheck && build && …`, so `pnpm format` sits second — after `test:performance` and before everything else. At `08d6465` it failed on **16 files**; after LANG-ADOPT-v5 merged at `33bcdb6`, **14 remain**, and `pnpm lint` (the next gate) carries **7 errors**. **Attribution is proven, not assumed:** the 14 files are byte-identical between `origin/main` and the measuring lane's branch (`git diff --stat origin/main HEAD -- <files>` is empty), and that lane's own 8 files pass `prettier --check`. **The consequence is total: no packet branching from `main` can reach a green matrix**, so AGENTS.md §6's *green at the integrated SHA* is unreachable for every lane until this lands — and a lane discovers it only by spending a slot, since the failure is 90 seconds in and looks like its own. **`packet/matrix-unblock` holds most of the named files** (`apps/web/src/surface-contract.ts`, `packages/canonical-model/src/predicate-kernel.ts`, `packages/compiler/src/{projections,storage}.ts`, `packages/runtime/src/semantic-operation-gateway.ts`, `test/postgres/module-runtime.test.ts` and the surface-data-binding tests), so the repair belongs to it and not to whoever trips over it next. **Do not fix by running `prettier --write` from another lane** — it manufactures conflicts across four active branches. **Worth noticing:** `format` and `lint` are cheap and run early, yet nothing runs them outside the matrix, so a lane that never takes a slot never learns `main` is red. A pre-push or pre-integration hook would have caught this before it cost anyone a window. |
| foreign-detector-misses-cpu-heavy-gates | **The matrix's foreign-process detector cannot see `typecheck`, `build`, `lint` or `format`, and they are what starve its performance gate** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-11, and it cost `matrix-unblock` two matrix windows the same day it was found.** `scripts/run-matrix.sh`'s detector matches `[c]orepack pnpm (test|check:boundaries|check:schema)|[p]laywright test|[r]un-security-scans\.sh`. **`typecheck`, `build`, `lint` and `format` match none of them**, take no test lock, and are CPU-heavy — `tsc` was measured at **235% CPU**. The performance gate requires **90% idle** and reports `COMPILE_BUDGET_INDETERMINATE` below it. **Measured consequence:** the `dev-environment` lane ran `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` from `/home/rvham/2rain-greenfield-devenv` while `matrix-unblock` held the window; idle fell to 73-79% and its performance gate went INDETERMINATE **twice**, then a third time at 57%. Neither lane was misbehaving: one was running the gates its reviewer had demanded, the other was correctly waiting, and **nothing in the protocol let either see the other**. `matrix-contention` says the wrapper is bypassable; this is the sharper form — **the commands that starve the performance gate are precisely the ones no lock and no detector covers**, because they are not suites. **Two candidate fixes, and they differ in kind:** widen the detector pattern to the CPU-heavy scripts (cheap, but another hand-maintained enumeration of the shape `lease-derivation` keeps filing), or have the performance gate name the processes consuming its margin so the next INDETERMINATE is self-diagnosing rather than costing a window to attribute. **The second is the better buy** — the gate already measures idle; it does not say who took it. **WIDENED 2026-08-11 the same evening, and the widening changes which fix is worth buying.** `dev-environment` took its own post-rebase window and hit the identical `COMPILE_BUDGET_INDETERMINATE` at **59.8% idle** — with **no repository command running at all**. The consumers, measured by `ps --sort=-pcpu`, were **six `ccd-cli` agent sessions at `--effort xhigh`**, together ~75% of CPU: the orchestration tooling itself, other lanes *thinking*. **No detector pattern over repo scripts can ever see that**, because it is not a repo process. **So the first candidate fix — widening the pattern to `typecheck`/`build`/`lint`/`format` — is now known to be insufficient**, and the second is the only one that generalises: **the performance gate must name the processes consuming its margin.** It already measures idle and refuses below 90%; printing the top consumers costs one `ps` call and converts every future INDETERMINATE from a lost window into a self-diagnosing one. **The deeper consequence is a scheduling fact this queue has not recorded:** with several agent lanes active on one 8-core WSL cap, **a 90%-idle gate may be unsatisfiable while the lanes that need it are running** — the gate is contending with the very sessions queued behind it. That is not a defect in the gate's threshold; it is the cost of parallel lanes on one machine, and it should be decided deliberately rather than rediscovered per packet. |
| review-prompt-clause-contradicts-scoping | ~~**Two binding skills give opposite instructions about review scope**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-13** | Behavioral | **DONE 2026-08-13. This row's own proposed resolution was adopted verbatim and it was the right one.** `mission-cadence`'s clause no longer asserts that nothing bounds scope; it now says **the LANE has fenced nothing**, that any scope stated is the orchestrator's, and that it stands as a claim under test. `review-tiers` §charter now cites `mission-cadence` and vice versa, which is the cheap-and-owed cross-citation this row asked for. **Worth recording about the orchestrator, not the skills:** this question was ruled once on 2026-08-13 as *"keep it verbatim, no edit"* by an orchestrator who had not read this row, hours before finding it during an audit — a filed row that nobody reads is indistinguishable from an unfiled one, which is the same class as the queue reaching 134. ORIGINAL TEXT FOLLOWS. **OPEN 2026-08-12, surfaced by `dev-environment`'s round-5 confirm arm.** `mission-cadence` requires **every** lane-written review prompt to carry, *verbatim*, the clause *"Nothing in this prompt bounds your scope... read anything you judge relevant."* `review-tiers` says the opposite: a review needs **specific decisive questions**, and *"find any way this could fail" is never a valid framing*; tier sets intensity, the charter sets scope. **A lane obeying `mission-cadence` therefore emits a prompt that `review-tiers` calls invalid**, and the round-5 reviewer said so directly — *"The prompt's statement that 'nothing bounds your scope' is steering... Binding review-tiers doctrine says the opposite."* It then reviewed to the narrower scope anyway. **Both rules have a real purpose and the conflict is not cosmetic:** the clause exists because a reviewed party choosing what may not be examined is the failure `mission-cadence` guards against; the scoping rule exists because unbounded review is how packets spiral, which is what the new convergence criteria were written to stop. **The collision is specifically in WHO may narrow.** The plausible resolution is that the *orchestrator* states the decisive questions and the *lane* may not — in which case the clause should say the lane has not fenced anything and the orchestrator's scope ruling stands as a claim under test, rather than asserting nothing bounds scope at all. **Do not fix by deleting either rule**; the drift they each prevent is real. **Cheap and owed:** one of the two skills must cite the other, since `AGENTS.md` §1's authority order does not settle a conflict between two skills at the same level. |
| gate-reads-a-different-thing-than-its-name | **Three gates this week read a different tree, format, or unit than their name implies, and each failure is indistinguishable from the real thing** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-13, three instances in one day, two of them found by `ux-picker` and one by the orchestrator on itself.** (a) **`check-review-record.sh` reads `docs/execution/review-log.md` from the WORKING TREE while walking commits on the branch.** The same gate, at the same instant, reported OK from a tree checked out at `origin/main` and `FAIL: af3462c ... no recorded SHA contains it` from a packet worktree whose log predated the record. **A lane cannot verify trunk's gate from its own checkout, and the failure looks exactly like a missing review.** (b) **The same script reads `grep -oE '[0-9a-f]{40}'` at line 116 — full SHAs only.** The orchestrator recorded a 7-character SHA; the row was silently ignored and the output was byte-identical to having written no row at all. **A record that looks recorded and is not read is worse than an absent one.** (c) **Containment is not packet-scoped** — see `merge-direction-hides-packet-work`; the gate cannot tell whose arm covered a commit. **The class: an instrument whose name claims one subject while it reads another**, which is `scan-subject-discovered-by-glob` and `double-must-honour-refusal` one layer up, now in the *tooling* rather than in packet controls. **Owed, and cheap for the first two: read the log from the branch being walked, and refuse a short SHA loudly instead of ignoring it.** A gate that silently ignores malformed input is a gate that cannot fail. |
| double-must-honour-refusal | **A test double that turns production's refusal into a success inverts the evidence it exists to provide** | Critical | **PARTIALLY CLOSED by accepted packet `form-wire-semantics`.** `BrowserFixtureExecutor` now has no ignore-refusal mode: it persists only the patch returned by the real `parseMutationInput`, and the parser's throw escapes. The two `KNOWN_FALSE_GREEN` assertions were converted rather than deleted and now observe success only beside an accepted provider verdict. **Prompt premise corrected:** at the packet base those two creates were refused because the fixture rendered only `master_name` while its create contract also required `master_number`, not because of boolean or blank-value encoding. The fixture now renders and submits both required values. **THE WIDER SWEEP REMAINS OPEN** for other in-memory executors and query stand-ins; it was explicitly outside this packet. |
| scan-subject-discovered-by-glob | **A control whose subject is found by a glob is silenced by editing the glob, and every synthetic control stays green** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, routed out of `LANG-ADOPT-v5`'s round-2 review and verified by the orchestrator at `315c158`.** `canonical-contracts-purity.test.ts:191-203` builds `productionFiles` from `globSync(['packages/*/src/**/*.ts','apps/*/src/**/*.ts'])`, flat-maps the scan over it, and asserts `deepEqual(selections, [])`. **Nothing asserts the glob matched anything.** Point it at an unmatched path and `selections` is `[]`, the live assertion passes, and all seven synthetic controls stay green because they call `latestVersionSelections('synthetic.ts', …)` directly. **This is the THIRD erasure mode found on one control in three rounds**, and the lane named the pattern itself: a strip that removed the subject, a rename that changed the subject's name, and now a glob that removes the subject's file — *"a scan over text has that failure mode structurally; only resolution removes it."* **The instance is closed inside `LANG-ADOPT-v5` because it is one assertion and the matrix was owed regardless; the CLASS is this row.** The general question: when a gate discovers its own inputs, what proves the discovery found the thing the gate exists to guard? A nonempty check is not enough — a glob matching the wrong tree is nonempty. **The answer is probably a named subject pinned per gate**, and every input-discovering gate in the repository needs the same treatment, not just this one. |
| form-write-untyped-wire | **The web write path was string-typed end to end, while `booleanFieldType` demands a native boolean** | Critical | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-15 in `form-wire-semantics` at `88f66b0`; ADR-0053 rules the form wire.** The raw URL-encoded carrier remains `Readonly<Record<string, string>>`; typed JSON on that carrier is unrepresentable. The selected operation's already-pinned input contract reaches the web binding, and exact `"true"`/`"false"` spellings become native booleans at one seam before semantic invocation. Other boolean spellings are detected and refused as untrusted bytes. The adopted profile-v1 control observes bare text controls, present operation input fields, raw `"true"`, an optional blank date, accepted real-parser verdict, stored native `true`, and absent date key. **Count correction:** the current vocabulary has ten field kinds — boolean plus nine string-validated kinds — rather than the nine total stated in the packet prompt. No compiler field or profile adoption is involved. Fresh Codex review passed and the full matrix is green at the identical SHA. |
| merge-direction-hides-packet-work | **Merging `main` INTO a packet and fast-forwarding hides the packet's commits from the review-record gate** | Critical | **WIDENED 2026-08-13 — this is the workflow's DEFAULT OUTCOME, not a slip, and the gate cannot catch its worst form.** **Two lanes produced the shape independently, by different routes, neither aware of the other:** `relation-contract-integrity`'s `0d990d2` (`merge: main into relation-contract-integrity`) and `matrix-unblock`'s `f0178ed` (`merge: main into matrix-unblock ahead of the acceptance matrix`, first parent `c4700a9` the packet, second parent `33bcdb6` main). **Merging trunk into your branch before an acceptance matrix is the natural, correct-seeming thing to do, and nothing warns you it hides your work from the gate.** **The consequence measured on trunk 2026-08-13, and it is worse than a missing record:** `ux-picker`'s implementation commits `3dd8f10`, `7f42380` and `0c5fa8d` were all ancestors of `main` — arriving through `matrix-unblock`'s merge, never through any `ux-picker` push — while `d9c9196`, carrying the **Critical write-path closure control**, was not. `params.getAll('operationId')` appeared in **zero files on `main`**, so **trunk ran one packet's renderer without the control that stops a duplicate `operationId` making an update form address create**, un-recorded, for as long as that merge stood. **And `check-review-record.sh` reported OK the whole time**, because it walks `--first-parent` and reads a merge through its second parent: `ux-picker`'s content was *covered by `matrix-unblock`'s record*. **That is not a gap in the record — it is a gap in what containment can mean.** A gate that asks *is this commit contained by some reviewed SHA* cannot distinguish content reviewed under its own packet's arm from content that merely rode along inside another lane's merge. **Owed: rule whether integration may ever be a fast-forward, and whether containment must be scoped to the packet whose row claims it.** Found by `ux-picker`, which refused to accept the orchestrator's word that trunk was clean and verified from its own tree. **OPEN 2026-08-12, caused and then measured by `relation-contract-integrity`.** `scripts/check-review-record.sh` covers a merge commit when its **second** parent — the packet tip — is an ancestor of, or is, a recorded SHA, because `git-workflow` expects a packet to reach `main` as `git merge --no-ff <packet>` **into** `main`. **This lane did it backwards:** merged `origin/main` into the packet branch, then fast-forwarded `main` onto the result. The merge `0d990d2` therefore has `parents = aa6cc91 (packet) f8c583e (main)` — the packet's work sits on the **first**-parent chain and main's own already-recorded tip is the second parent. **The gate cannot see the packet's commits at all**, so it would have failed identically even if the review HAD been run and recorded. **Why the shape is tempting:** merging main into the packet is exactly what `lanes.md` tells a lane to do when main moves under it (*"every other lane then merges current `main` into its branch"*). That instruction is correct for making the lane's own tree current. **What is wrong is finishing the integration by fast-forwarding `main` onto that merge** rather than performing a second, opposite-direction `--no-ff` merge into `main`. Nothing says so, and the FF looks attractive because it makes the integrated SHA identical to the matrix SHA. **Owed:** state the integration direction explicitly in `git-workflow` — a packet lands by `--no-ff` merge INTO `main`, and a fast-forward is admissible only when the packet branch has not itself absorbed `main` — and consider whether `check-review-record.sh` should also walk first-parent history so the coverage model does not depend on merge direction. **A gate whose correctness depends on an unwritten convention is one rename away from silent.** |
| record-truncation-ungated | **An append-only shared record can be silently truncated by a stale-tree commit, and no gate observes it** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-12. Measured twice on one packet's record, three days apart.** `docs/execution/ledger.md`, `docs/execution/review-log.md` and `docs/execution/current-plan.md` are append-only shared files by `lanes.md`'s own rule, and `docs/decisions/**` is orchestrator-owned. **Nothing enforces any of it.** **The second instance is the instructive one:** `8cab25a`, whose stated subject is the convergence-criteria skills change and which made that change correctly, has `090f1cc` as its direct parent and **also deleted that commit's entire content** — `ADR-0052` (242 lines), four `current-plan` rows, one ledger row and two review-log rows. The message mentions none of it. It reads as a commit authored against a tree that predated its parent, not as a deliberate revert. **Why no gate caught it:** every check in the matrix reads code. `check:reachability` counts test files, `repository-hygiene` pins the test inventory, `check:language-coverage` pins obligations — **nothing pins the ledger, the review-log or the ADR set**, so removing an accepted packet's entire record is invisible to a green matrix. Restoration was manual and depended on the branch still existing. **Owed:** a ratchet over the append-only records — an ADR count/id set that may only grow, and ledger/review-log row counts that may only grow — with a named diagnostic and a negative control that reds when a row is removed. **Note the failure is a superset of documentation:** the same shape would silently drop an accepted packet's integrated SHA, which is the evidence link `git-workflow` forbids breaking. |
| ephemeral-pg-endpoint-race | **The ephemeral PostgreSQL container reports ready internally while its published host port refuses, failing a matrix at a SHA that passes on retry** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-12, measured by `relation-contract-integrity`.** A full matrix reded with `not ok 97 - migrations 0006-0021 … converge with the checked-in snapshot`, error `ephemeral PostgreSQL is ready inside its container but its published endpoint is unavailable: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:55622`. The container log shows PostgreSQL through bootstrap and *listening on Unix socket*; the **published port** refused. The harness's own readiness tests — *retries a refused published endpoint after internal readiness* and *reports a persistently refused endpoint only at its deadline* — both passed, so the retry worked and hit its deadline. **The same SHA passed on the next run with nothing changed**, clean tree, and the packet's diff touches no migration, schema, `db/` or provider file. **So this is environmental, and it costs a full matrix run (~20 min) at random.** Distinct from `matrix-contention`, which is about lock serialisation across lanes; this is a Docker port-publishing race on WSL2 inside a single run. **Owed:** decide whether the readiness deadline is too tight for this machine, whether readiness should poll the published endpoint rather than the container's internal state first, or whether the run should retry the container once before failing the suite. **A verdict that flips between runs is the failure `lanes.md` already names** — a loaded run does not fail honestly — and it makes any single matrix result weaker evidence than its greenness suggests. |
| relation-scoped-enumeration | **The record picker that actually makes a required relation fillable — and the scoped-list problem that defeated the first attempt** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-12. Packet 2 of 3 from `relation-picker-rechartered`; packet 1 `relation-contract-integrity` accepted at `0d990d2`.** This is the packet that closes `required-relation-uncreatable`. Until it lands, **a purchase order still cannot be given a supplier from any web surface**, which is what blocks `PUR-1`. **THE BLOCKER, measured against the compiled release rather than predicted:** every first-party inventory target list declares `legalEntityScope` — `inventory_transaction`, `inventory_transaction_line`, `stock_count`, `stock_count_line` are all SCOPED; only `party`, `party_role` and `legal_entity` are unscoped. The blocked attempt's `pickerListSurfaceFor` rejected exactly the scoped ones, so three of the four form surfaces ADR-0052 cites remained uncreatable and **only `party_role` was ever fixed**. **ADR-0052 §7's justification for that exclusion is FALSE and must be amended, not inherited:** it claims the caller cannot supply the scope, but `renderSurfaceRuntimeWithData` already computes `legalEntitySelection` from the URL and hands it to the workspace context bar. It was simply never passed to the picker. **Already ruled — do not relitigate.** `hasMore` is the completeness boundary: a picker that cannot enumerate its target completely REFUSES rather than showing a prefix, because silently dropping valid foreign-key targets is a write-path correctness defect, not list usability. `truncatedByMaximum` must NOT independently refuse — it means the requested page size was capped, not that records were omitted. A targetless OPTIONAL relation renders nothing, never a `<select>` whose only choice is `None`. ADR-0036 §2 admits no scripted control, so this stays a server-rendered `<select>`; if option count makes that unusable, that is `ux-list-usability`, not a licence. **What packet 1 built for this packet to consume:** `CompiledSurfaceDataBinding.relationInputs` is now `EntityRelationAuthority` — `{status:'known', relationInputs}` or `{status:'unavailable'}`. **`unavailable` must never be read as `none`**; it means no create contract declared any authority, which is a legitimate compiled state. This packet is its FIRST consumer, so its ergonomics are untested by use. **Owed by this packet:** carry the selected legal-entity value into the target list query's declared operand; **rule same-scope semantics** (may a child only reference parents in the same legal entity, and what happens when the operand is unselected); decide the bounded search/paging path for targets exceeding the list maximum; and close `relation-refusal-unnamed` if a dedicated diagnostic code is minted. **Its review must also re-examine packet 1's final delta (`28e7ce3`)**, which shipped without an independent arm by user ruling — see the `relation-contract-integrity` ledger row. **PREREQUISITE CLEARED 2026-08-15 — `profile-v2-adoption` is accepted at `a73bd36` and this packet is UNBLOCKED.** Every relation input in the shipped artifact now carries its `targetEntityId` (0-of-6 → 6-of-6), so a picker finally has a list to resolve. **Read the profile-v2-adoption ledger row before starting:** adoption also made per-field kinds reach the artifact, so form surfaces now render typed controls, and `form-wire-semantics` landed its renderer rewrite in the same tree. The original prerequisite follows. No relation input in the shipped artifact carries a `targetEntityId` under the adopted v1 profile, and a picker with no target has no list to resolve. |
| profile-v2-adoption | ~~Adopt compiler-semantic profile v2~~ — **ACCEPTED 2026-08-15, integrated `a73bd36`, matrix-green `949a9dc`; see the [ledger](ledger.md)** | Critical | **DONE. `relation-scoped-enumeration` is unblocked: relation inputs carrying a target went 0-of-6 to 6-of-6 in the shipped artifact.** Original framing follows. **OPEN 2026-08-13, filed by the orchestrator at `3509ce8` while sweeping to write the Tier 1 prompt. The absence of this row is the finding:** two packets (`ux-reference-picker`, `relation-scoped-enumeration`) were chartered to build a record picker that **cannot render against the shipped application**, and neither charter named the reason. `ADOPTED_COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_VERSION = COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_V1_VERSION` (`packages/compiler/src/compiler.ts`, re-locate by symbol) and `relationTargets` in `packages/compiler/src/projections.ts` requires `compilerSemanticProfileVersion === COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_V2_VERSION`, so `targetEntityId` is emitted by no recorded lineage entry. ADR-0052 §5 states this outright — *"under the adopted profile no relation carries a target, so that is the normal case until v2 adoption"* — and it was read as a note rather than as a scheduling fact by everyone who read it, including the orchestrator writing the handoff that called `relation-scoped-enumeration` the only blocker. **What the accumulation window holds, and why adoption is now worth its cost:** `targetEntityId` (ADR-0052 §1) and `disclosureTier` (`U5b`). `U5-design` measured that a profile freezes at ADOPTION, not at the cut, precisely so fields could accumulate and adoption could be one schedulable event minting exactly one lineage entry no matter how many fields waited. **Two fields have accumulated and one of them blocks the inventory path, so the window's purpose is served and it should close.** **Carried obligations, both already written down:** `U5b` deferred its `disclosureTier` declaration to this packet explicitly — *"the `v2` adoption packet declares `always` on a real slot, where the same one line carries a compiled difference and both affected assertions are corrected once against a delta that means something"* — and ADR-0047 §4a records that ruling. **The first thing this packet must MEASURE rather than assume:** whether the adoption edge is a profile-only edge or an ordinary source edge, because `rollback-release-edge` (open, Critical) says two profile siblings sharing one revision make rollback refuse on a correct adjacent edge, and `proj-disc-impl` already carved `ROLLBACK_ACROSS_PROFILE_ONLY_EDGE` out of the fallback for exactly that case. If `U5b`'s `always` declaration edits a domain definition then the revision changes and the edge is ordinary; if adoption is otherwise source-identical then it is not. **Report which, with the measurement — do not fix `rollback-release-edge` here**, it has its own row and its own review for a reason. **Scope note:** ADR-0047 §4 makes moving the constant a lineage-minting event that moves every release root and retires the serving head to history, so `check:app-release` and `test:postgres` are both required gates and the packet is Critical regardless of how few lines it changes. `LANG-ADOPT-v5` is the closest fresh precedent for how small an adoption diff can be while still being Critical. **EVIDENCE-READY 2026-08-14. Lineage 9 -> 10; entry 8's root preserved byte-identically; all nine prior entries reproduce; `check:app-release` and `check:demo-release` both exit 0.** **§3 ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT, and the answer is ORDINARY SOURCE EDGE.** Both arms were run. Adoption ALONE is source-identical -- constant moved, nothing else -- and mints an entry whose `normalizedDefinitionBytesBase64` is byte-identical to entry 8's, i.e. a genuine profile-only sibling that would share one package revision and make rollback refuse. But this packet does not ship adoption alone: `U5b`'s deferred `disclosureTier` declaration edits `packages/domain/src/party/definition.ts`, the normalized definition digest moves `57e52370..` -> `7061077d..`, and the two entries therefore hold DIFFERENT package revisions. So `parent_revision_id` lineage holds, `ROLLBACK_ACROSS_PROFILE_ONLY_EDGE` does not fire, and an operator rolling back across this edge sees ordinary behaviour. **`rollback-release-edge` was NOT touched and its defect was NOT reproduced on this edge** -- the §4a obligation is what keeps this edge ordinary, which is a second, unstated reason ADR-0047 was right to bundle them. **THE ACCUMULATION WINDOW HELD THREE SHAPES, NOT TWO — this row said two.** `targetEntityId` and `disclosureTier` were named; `fields` (per-field kinds, landed by `e29935d`) was not, and it is the largest of the three by artifact delta and the only one that moves a compatibility floor. Adoption ships all three. **THE BLOCKER IS MEASURABLY LIFTED:** relation inputs carrying a target went **0 of 6 -> 6 of 6** in the recorded artifact bytes, and module input contracts now name v3/v4. `relation-scoped-enumeration` is unblocked. **THE DISCLOSURE TIER IS IN ARTIFACTS, REFUTING `U5b`'s STATE:** 0 artifacts -> 1 artifact carrying it, 106 slots. **But the authored `always` is indistinguishable from the 105 defaulted ones** in any compiled artifact, because `?? DEFAULT_DISCLOSURE_TIER` resolves absence to the same value -- the survivor `U5b` itself named. The declaration is observable in the NORMALIZED definition and in the conformance ledger (427 -> 429 observations), never in the artifact. |
| relation-argument-set-unbound | **A compiled operation's CLOSED ARGUMENT SET is not bound to its effect, so an accepted argument can be silently ignored** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-12, found by `relation-contract-integrity`'s round-2 review and routed rather than fixed, because the layer that could enforce it is architecturally denied the vocabulary.** Only the `relations` member is bound to the effect. The rest of `closedArgumentKeys` is validated as *an array of strings* and nothing checks it against what the effect actually consumes. **The defect:** a create contract whose keys additionally name a mutation argument it never reads is admitted by every validator; the writer's create branch trusts the compiled array, reads its own keys, and **silently ignores the extra** — ADR-0041's accepted-and-ignored state, reachable by a one-property mutation. **The dual failure is worse:** dropping a key the writer requires declares an operation no caller can satisfy. Field and writable-field declarations are likewise unbound to the effects that could consume them. **Why it was not fixed in place, and this is the interesting part:** the natural home is the operation gateway, and **two independent fences forbid it**. `owned gateway sources expose one authority each` (`test/integration/semantic-gateways.test.ts`) refuses the gateway source containing update-argument vocabulary at all — the check reds on the literal. And `workspaceDependencyAllowed` (`packages/dev-tooling/src/architecture-boundaries.ts:616`) permits a `runtime` package to depend only on `contracts`, so the gateway may not import the layer that emits the sets. **The lane declined to smuggle the vocabulary in past a fence built to keep it out.** **Owed: rule where the effect/argument-set binding lives** — a contracts-layer vocabulary both sides may import, enforcement in the writer that already switches on effect kind, or a conformance gate over emitted contracts. Each has a different threat model: only a runtime check defends a forged artifact, which is the model the relation work already assumes. |
| relation-picker-rechartered | **`ux-reference-picker` returned BLOCK at round 2 and RE-CHARTERS into three packets** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-12. Reviewed candidate preserved at tag `ux-reference-picker-reviewed-r2` (`e9c9cd5`), pushed; branch head with the full record at `085c0f7`. NOT merged and not intended to be — `main` has since replaced the intent-addressed write path with an operation-addressed one (ADR-0051), so the browser half would need rewriting regardless.** The online arm returned BLOCK with an explicit instruction not to run a third revision, because **three of round 1's accepted classes recurred**: split parser authority, heuristic source authority, and non-discriminating controls. `mission-cadence`'s stop-convergence rule and `review-tiers`' two-REVISE cap both say that is the charter failing, not the code. **The lane verified all five findings against source before accepting the route.** **(1) BLOCKER, and it contradicts the packet's own ADR.** `pickerListSurfaceFor` rejected any target list declaring `legalEntityScope`, and **every first-party inventory target list is scoped** — verified against the compiled release: `inventory_transaction`, `inventory_transaction_line`, `stock_count`, `stock_count_line` all SCOPED; only `party`, `party_role`, `legal_entity` unscoped. So three of the four form surfaces **ADR-0052's own Context cites as the live defect** still refused at profile v2, and **only `party_role` was ever fixed**. ADR-0052 §7's justification — that the caller cannot supply the scope — **is false**: `renderSurfaceRuntimeWithData` already computes `legalEntitySelection` and hands it to the workspace context bar; it was simply never passed to the picker. **(2) `create-only` is not an artifact invariant.** The catalog parser binds only `systemInput` to `createRecordEffect`; nothing forbids non-empty `relationInputs` or a v3/v4 contract on update/archive/restore/transition. The provider hardcodes `relations: {}` on update and validates it against the declared relations, so a required relation on an update contract makes **every update unsatisfiable** — ADR-0041's accepted-and-ignored state, reachable by a one-property mutation. **(3) The shared parser was a subset, and its shared call was deletion-dead.** `parsePinnedRelationInputs` validated versions, target-key pairing, array shape and id string-ness; the gateway's surviving loop *additionally* validated closed relation keys, `archiveBehavior` and canonical ids. The browser delegated only to the shared function and so **accepted five single-property forgeries the gateway refuses**. Every condition the shared call checked was independently checked around it, so deleting it removed no gateway check — the appearance of one authority without the forcing. **(4) Entity relation authority was a cardinality heuristic** — `relationInputs.length > relationInputs.length`, evaluated before lifecycle filtering — so a retired create declaring more relations outranked the active one, and equal-length disagreement resolved by payload order. Masked only by a compiler coincidence the readers do not enforce. **(5) The `hasMore` control could not reach its branch.** The specimen set `hasMore: true` with `totalCount = pageOffset + returnedCount`, which `list-behavior/index.ts:251` refuses as internally inconsistent; `recordPickerOptions` caught that and returned `null`, so the form refused for the wrong reason and **deleting the production line left the control green**. **Confirmed sound and reusable:** the carrier exhaustion over all five pinned projections; the 2x2 contract binding the KEY rather than list presence; `hasMore` as the right completeness boundary with `truncatedByMaximum` correctly excluded; the replaced literal adopted-profile control. **PACKET 1 ACCEPTED 2026-08-12 at `0d990d2`** — the contract now has one complete authority both consumers run, create-only enforced as an artifact invariant, unique identities, and explicit absent authority. **Packet 2 is next and is where the blocker is actually fixed.** **The three packets:** **(a) `relation-contract-integrity`** *(DONE)* — one genuinely shared complete parser, create-only enforced as a catalog-parser invariant, and real entity-level relation authority. **(b) `relation-scoped-enumeration`** — carry the selected legal-entity value into the target list's declared operand, rule same-scope semantics, and decide the bounded search/paging path for oversized targets. **(c) evidence**, folded into both: an internally valid `hasMore` specimen and the **scoped** first-party inventory forms exercised, not only the tenant-shared fixture. |
| relation-refusal-unnamed | **A create form that cannot offer a required relation refuses as `QUERY_UNSUPPORTED`, which does not name what is missing** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-12, routed by `ux-reference-picker` rather than fixed there.** An unsatisfiable create form must refuse rather than render controls the provider will certainly reject, and `QUERY_UNSUPPORTED` is the one existing slot-placed code that is honestly true — the missing thing really is a semantic data capability. **But its sentence is `Capability unavailable`, which tells an operator nothing about which relation is unfillable or what an administrator must author.** Minting a code was declined at the time because `apps/web/src/message-catalog.ts` was held unintegrated by `ux-picker`. **Owed: a dedicated code carrying the relation as its `subject`.** May fold into `msg-code-accuracy`. |
| web-form-lease-contention | **Three unintegrated branches held the same three `apps/web/src` files; two have since merged** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-12, measured by `ux-reference-picker` via `lanes.md`'s empirical disjointness check rather than predicted from the partition table. Largely RESOLVED by integration and kept for the lesson.** `packet/ux-picker`, `packet/pur1-intent-limit` and `packet/ux-reference-picker` all diffed `apps/web/src/{component-registry,surface-contract,surface-runtime}.ts` against `main` simultaneously. `pur1-intent-limit` has since merged and `ux-reference-picker` is blocked and will not merge, leaving **`ux-picker` still unintegrated on those paths**. No lane was actively editing, so this was an integration-ordering cost rather than a corruption risk. **The durable point:** the partition table's `apps/web/**` row still reads *frozen while lanes run*, which was false three times over, and the empirical check is the only thing that found it. |
| form-empty-means-nothing | **A blank rendered control formerly collapsed set-empty, clear and leave-alone into `""`** | Critical | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-15 in `form-wire-semantics` at `88f66b0`; ADR-0053 rules this here, not in U7.** A native companion select carries the closed `nothing | clear | emptyText` vocabulary. Before object construction a discriminated `set | clear | nothing` union makes the three meanings unrepresentable as one another; provider input uses a missing key, `null`, or a real string/boolean. Rounds 1–5 closed unavailable controls, one-line text sanitisation, valid empty replacement, renderer-only companion applicability and whole-subject erasure. Round-6 review found no remaining production bypass, but found the central `OR` guard's two operands were not independently load-bearing: existing validation already owned both one-sided omissions. Round 7 narrows that guard to the `AND` case it alone owns—complete primary-and-companion erasure before the generic early return. The unchanged profile-v1/profile-v2 presence matrix observes 422, no provider verdict and unchanged revision plus values for companion-only deletion, primary-only deletion and whole-subject erasure. ADR-0053 separates optional and required-text defaults. Required non-text keeps hidden preservation and required create keeps no companion. Fresh Codex review passed and the full matrix is green at the identical SHA. U7 remains parked UX doctrine. |
| form-required-field-not-rendered | **A form surface can omit a field its create contract requires, leaving the create operation impossible to satisfy** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-14, found while converting `form-wire-semantics`' two false-green assertions; filed and left.** At the packet base, `ordinaryModuleV1`'s `master_get` selected `master_name` only while `master_number` was required by create, so both supposedly wire-related false greens actually failed with `MODULE_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING`. The browser fixture now explicitly selects and fills both required values so this packet can measure its own subject, but production still has no general compile/bind rule proving every required create field is renderable. Rule honour-or-refuse for that mismatch in its own packet; do not infer a production fix from the repaired fixture. |
| matrix-evidence-truncated | **Piping a matrix through `tail` destroys the only record of why it failed** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-10, self-reported by `ux-picker` and it cost a 27-minute re-run.** The run was piped through `tail -60`, so when `test:postgres` came back `cancelled 1 / fail 0` the failing detail had already scrolled past — and the reachability artifact records `path` and `realResultCount` only, **not per-test outcomes**, so nothing else held it. A second cost landed in the same report: **the completion notification carried the tail pipeline's exit code, not the run's**, so a red matrix first read as a pass. **Both are the same shape as the orchestrator's own `echo "pushed $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)"` error — a reporting harness that cannot fail.** Owed, and cheap: capture full matrix output to a file and read the file; never let a truncating filter or a pipeline sit between the run and its exit status. Belongs in `git-workflow` beside the freeze rules. **RECURRENCE 2026-08-14 from `form-wire-semantics`:** the packet charter and `review-tiers` still instruct lanes to read `MATRIX_EXIT` from inside the log, but current `scripts/run-matrix.sh` emits no `MATRIX_EXIT` token. It appends retries for one SHA, so one log can contain an indeterminate `FULL_MATRIX_FAILED` followed by `FULL_MATRIX_PASS_SHA`; the final marker is readable, but the promised in-log process exit is absent. Align the runner and doctrine rather than making each lane infer which terminal marker is authoritative. |
| container-pressure-forges-outcomes | **Docker volume pressure produces a WRONG release-activation outcome, not a timeout** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-13, measured by `ux-picker` with a three-run discriminator.** `composed product activates through the kernel` failed with `composed release activation did not verify: NO_SWAP_TERMINAL` **on `ux-picker`'s merge AND on `main` alone at `b289911`** in a worktree carrying none of the packet — so it was pre-existing and not the merge. **Then it passed 16/16 on the same merged tree after reclaiming 27 leaked anonymous volumes and one orphaned `north-star-release-activation` container whose owner pid was dead.** Same code, same test, different Docker state. **This is a worse manifestation than the one `matrix-machine-decay` records.** That row's symptom is slowness and a 30-second container-readiness timeout — loud, and obviously environmental. This one produces a **definite, plausible-looking outcome code**: the kernel reports the release did not swap, which reads as a release-kernel defect rather than as machine state. **A lane hitting it will debug the activation path.** **Owed:** find why activation returns `NO_SWAP_TERMINAL` under container pressure rather than failing loudly, and whether any other gate can be pushed into a wrong-but-plausible verdict the same way. **`main` was red on this at `b289911` and nothing recorded it**, which is how it reached a second lane. Pairs with `leak-guard-orphan` — the orphaned container here had a dead owner, which is that row's exact claim. **THIRD RECURRENCE 2026-08-25, in `PUR-2a` round 6, and this one is the READINESS manifestation rather than the wrong-outcome one.** `release verification leaves an optional self-reference unset and terminates` failed in `test/postgres/module-runtime.test.ts` with `ephemeral PostgreSQL is ready inside its container but its published endpoint is unavailable: ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:57556` — the container reports ready INSIDE itself while its published port refuses. Re-run in isolation under the same lock: **1/1 pass, same code, same tree.** It appeared as the 51st test of a whole-suite run and passed as the first of a scoped one, which is the same position-dependence `postgres-mutation-entries-need-an-isolable-subject` records. **Worth noting for whoever owns this row: the readiness form is the benign one** — it names the transport plainly instead of forging a plausible domain verdict — so the two manifestations differ in cost by a lot, and a fix that only addresses readiness would leave the expensive half open. **RECURRENCE 2026-08-14 in `form-wire-semantics` round 6:** the first browser blast-radius run returned the same `NO_SWAP_TERMINAL` immediately after another lane's PostgreSQL suite released the exclusive lock, while all 28 form-wire tests passed. Post-failure inspection found zero active containers, eight local volumes totalling 904.5 MB, 937 GB disk free and 6.5 GiB memory available; one exclusive retry from that quiet state passed 89/89. This is corroboration of the wrong-outcome class, not proof that volume count alone caused this recurrence. |
