| matrix-machine-decay | **The suite is ~2x slower than this morning on an equally quiet machine** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, measured by `pur1-intent-limit` across two GREEN runs, both on gated-quiet machines.** Sum of shared measured tests **697s → 1191s**. `composed product advances` 98.8s → 208.3s; `Catalog tenant SKU/resolver/lifecycle` 6.2s → 14.3s; `migration 0010 narrower-key duplicates` 4.0s → 10.8s. **Not contention** — the second run was gated at `RUNNABLE=0`, 97.7% idle, zero foreign worktree processes — **and not the tree**, since pure-compute tests slow equally and the production delta was a behaviour-preserving extraction. **Most likely cause: Docker carrying 454 local volumes (12.5GB, 94% reclaimable) plus 6.1GB of build cache** — itself downstream of `leak-guard-orphan`, since orphaned containers leave their volumes behind. **The concrete consequence:** `composed product activates through the kernel` landed at **257.0s against a 300s bound — a 1.17x margin**, where its own header documents 210.9s (1.42x) as the matrix figure. **It will red for the next packet on any bad day, and the prescribed fix is splitting the `-source-edge` tenant, never raising the bound.** First action is cheap and measurable: reclaim the volumes and re-measure the same tests. **PARTIALLY RECOVERED 2026-08-09 — the prune ran and this row stays open.** 481 volumes → 7, 13.8GB → 831MB, **12.96GB reclaimed**, plus 1.57GB of build cache; the four in-use volumes untouched. Re-measured on the same suite: shared tests **1191s → 831s**, `composed product activates` **257.0s → 191.8s**, `advances` 208.3s → 151.3s, and **test 8's margin recovered from 1.17x to 1.56x**. But the pre-decay baseline was **620s**, so the suite is still **1.34x slower**: **the volumes were a large part of this and not all of it.** Remaining candidates named by the measuring lane — 4.5GB of build cache still present, three old `2rain-*` containers belonging to another project, or host-side WSL state invisible from inside. **RE-OPENED WIDER 2026-08-10 — eight `test:postgres` readings on one machine in one day, and the recent trend is upward again.** 926 → 1617 → 903 → 907 → 921 → 1202 → 1004 → **1310s**. The 1617s outlier is attributed and settled: it was launched at a 1-minute load of 2.60 by a preflight that saw no test processes, and it carried the one cancelled test. **But the three most recent readings — 1202, 1004, 1310 — all cleared the same gate that produced the clean ~910s cluster**, so the earlier explanation of *one bad launch* no longer covers them. **The cancellation did not reproduce** across two later runs (196/196, 0 cancelled), so it was environmental and never touched `ux-picker`. **Do not close on the recovery; a third of the regression is unexplained and the recent readings are moving the wrong way.** **THIRD DATA POINT 2026-08-10, and it establishes a plateau rather than a trend.** `pur1-intent-limit` re-measured the same 34 shared tests by the same method across three SHAs: `b86cb09` pre-decay **634s (1.00x)**, `be86909` post-prune **851s (1.34x)**, `4277f1f` now **820s (1.29x)**. **The two post-prune runs agree within 4%, so the residual ~30% is persistent and reproducible, not noise** — which is what a single reading could not establish. Test 8 tracks it: 135.8s → 191.8s → **185.6s**, holding a ~1.6x margin against its 300s bound rather than the 1.17x that cost three runs. **Bookkeeping caveat, and it is owed:** this 634/851/820 series is a **34-test sum** and does not reconcile with the 697/1191/831 series recorded above, which was a differently-scoped sum. **Do not blend them.** Whoever measures next should state the test set explicitly and reconcile the two series, or retire the older one. |
| leak-guard-orphan | **`leak-guard`'s guardian did not reap a container whose owner died** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, found by `pur1-intent-limit` when its own stray-container guard refused a launch.** A running PostgreSQL container `north-star-stock-lock-order-75392-a8800338` was up **about an hour**, holding `127.0.0.1:56421`, spawned by `test/postgres/stock-serializer.test.ts` — and **owner pid 75392 was dead.** `leak-guard`'s central claim is a detached, process-identity-bound guardian that survives a `kill -9` of the real owner. **Here the owner died and the container outlived it by an hour**, so either the guardian did not arm on that path or it died with the harness. **This is a defect in an accepted packet's central guarantee**, and it surfaced only because another lane tripped over it — had that lane's own guard not caught it, it would have appeared as an unexplained port collision deep in `test:postgres` and cost a round to attribute. The finding lane **checked the owner was dead before removing another lane's artifact**, which is the right order. |
| matrix-preflight-blind | **A preflight that scans for test runners cannot see the load that matters** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, measured by `pur1-intent-limit` across five matrix attempts.** Its preflight certified the machine at **98.4% idle** and the run still degraded ~2x: `ledger bytes and code-point ordering`, a pure-compute unit test with no containers, went **9.5s → 28.4s (3.01x)**, and every suite moved 1.2–3x. **The cause was another lane's `esbuild`.** tsx and esbuild transpile, `tsc`, and agent-runtime work burn CPU and take **no test lock at all**, so a preflight matching `comm=node` running a test runner is blind to them — and `r=7` runnable on an 8-processor cap does not need a single test suite to be running. **This is the concrete form of `matrix-preflight`'s deeper gap:** `lock-obs` leases test *suites*; nothing leases lane *tooling*. **An exclusive window granted in good faith is unenforceable**, which is why the fix is sequencing lanes rather than a better scan. Three distinct contention failures on one packet — a 300s timeout, `lock-scan-scope`, and `COMPILE_BUDGET_INDETERMINATE` — all trace here. |
| lock-scan-scope | **A machine-wide scan is checked against a per-invocation exemption set** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, diagnosed by `pur1-intent-limit` while attributing an intermittent red it did not own.** `repository-hygiene.test.ts:441` runs `run-matrix.sh` against an **isolated control lock path** (`NORTH_STAR_TEST_LOCK_PATH=/tmp/north-star-matrix-lock-control-<pid>`). `run-matrix.sh` then computes its exemption set from `coordinatedProcesses`, which reads participants **from that control registry** — containing only the test's own spawned holder. **Meanwhile the foreign-process scan reads the real, machine-wide `ps` and matches `[c]orepack pnpm test`.** So the sweep's own `corepack pnpm test:architecture` ancestor, which *would* be coordinated on the real lock, is absent from the control lock's exemption set — and so is every other lane's queued or running invocation. **The scan and the exemption set read different locks.** That is why it passes sometimes and fails others: the verdict depends entirely on what else is on the machine in that window. **Suggested fix, from the diagnosing lane: the scan and the exemption set must read the same lock.** Attribution was established rather than assumed — the test passed at `b86cb09` inside a full matrix and at `5ea1b8b`, and the diagnosing packet touches no lock machinery. **A contended red is indistinguishable from a real one in the output**, which is what makes this worth fixing rather than tolerating. |
| matrix-contention | **Nothing enforces heavy-suite serialisation across lanes, and the matrix permits the race** | Critical | **RE-TIERED 2026-08-08 from Mechanical.** Both rows were forecast on a one-line scope -- *write the holder's pid into the lock file so the next starvation names its cause.* The packet that implemented them, `lock-obs`, shipped a 175-line lock wrapper, a 365-line registry, a new suite runner, changed acquisition modes on every test entry point, and a **new invariant**: an inherited claim authorizes work only if path, pid, start ticks, liveness, holding state and mode all validate. `review-tiers` puts new or changed invariants at Critical wherever they live, and tier follows the final diff. Five review arms; four found real defects and three would have defeated the packet's purpose. The tier was right in practice and wrong on the row. **OPEN 2026-08-06, raised by `U1` after it cost three matrix attempts in one round.** AGENTS.md §5 names the heavy suites serial, but that is a declared rule with no cross-lane enforcement — and `scripts/run-matrix.sh:111-119` **actively downgrades to shared access** *"for the load-tolerant matrix"*, which permits two browser suites to race on ephemeral PostgreSQL container ports. `U1` observed exactly that: `catalog-runtime.spec.ts` hit `ECONNREFUSED` while `U6a` ran `test:browser` concurrently. A second attempt died on `TEST_GATE_LOCK_BUSY` because a lane invoked the aggregate `corepack pnpm test` directly rather than through `run-matrix.sh`, tripping the foreign-process detector — **so the wrapper is bypassable, which is the deeper defect.** Pairs with `lock-owner`: together they are why a contended failure is both likely and undiagnosable. **Do not fix by widening the timeout** — `lanes.md` forbids it. The question is whether the shared downgrade should exclude container-port suites, and whether the aggregate script should refuse to run outside the wrapper.
| main-worktree-install | **The main checkout cannot typecheck, and every new session is pointed at it** | Mechanical | **SUBJECT MOVED 2026-08-10 — re-measure before citing this row.** `/home/rvham/2rain-greenfield` is no longer a checkout of `main`: `git worktree list` puts it on `packet/ux-picker` at `7cf7831`, and that lane ran **nine suites green from it** — `test:unit` 106/106, `test:compiler` 139/139, `test:integration` 122/122, `test:contracts`, `test:architecture` 139/139, `test:browser` 71/71 and both release checks. **So "no unit or PostgreSQL suite can run in that checkout at all" is refuted at that SHA.** What still measures as before: root `node_modules/@north-star/` holds one link of nine and `require.resolve('zod')` throws from the repository root — **which may simply be pnpm workspace isolation rather than breakage**, since `apps/web/node_modules/@north-star/` holds five links and `zod` is `canonical-model`'s dependency, not the root's. **`LANG-ADOPT-v5` cited this row to predict that a matrix running in that directory was doomed; it was `ux-picker`'s own matrix in `ux-picker`'s own worktree, and the prediction was wrong.** A stale row read as a live measurement is the same error class the orchestrator keeps making. **Owed: re-measure what the root typecheck actually needs, and either re-scope this row to `main`-checkout sessions or close it.** The original framing follows. **OPEN 2026-08-08, found incidentally by `PS-1`.** `node_modules/@north-star/` in `/home/rvham/2rain-greenfield` holds **one** symlink of nine — only `canonical-model` — so `@north-star/compiler`, `runtime`, `postgres-provider` and the rest do not resolve. The root typecheck reports **402 errors** there. **Matrix evidence is unaffected**: every lane runs in its own worktree with its own install, and `lock-obs` and `U5b` both froze from theirs. **UPGRADED 2026-08-08 — the hazard is not orientation, it is total.** Re-measured: `node_modules/@north-star/` now holds **zero** links (it held one earlier the same day), **`zod` is absent** although `canonical-model` declares it, and `require.resolve('zod')` throws. **`canonical-model` cannot load, so no unit or PostgreSQL suite can run in that checkout at all.** A second consequence, reported by `PS-2`: differential-typecheck baselines measured against a broken install are noise — that lane reported "692 pre-existing errors", then "402", then zero after `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, and **none of its earlier "zero new against baseline" evidence should be cited.** Repair with `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, never by hardlinking `node_modules` from another worktree — that is how the npm-shaped tree arrived in a pnpm workspace. The original framing follows. `CLAUDE.md` points every new session at that checkout; a session running `pnpm typecheck` there sees 402 errors, and the reachable outcome is not panic but habituation — learning to discount typecheck output, which is how a real error later reads as noise. **Fix: repair the install, and add a check that fails when a workspace package is unlinked, so the next occurrence is reported rather than discovered.** |
| msg-catalog-reach | **Half the message catalog is declared without an executing path** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-08, spotted by `5g3-sm-impl` in a matrix log it had no reason to read.** The run reports `message catalog reachability: 13 of 27 codes driven by a real request path; 14 declared without one`. [ADR-0048](../decisions/ADR-0048-the-message-catalog-is-platform-vocabulary-held-in-code.md) anticipated a shortfall — *"`U6a` must measure this and declare a shortfall if the gate cannot be 27-of-27"* — and named **six** page-level codes as hard to exercise. **Fourteen is not six.** The remaining codes are observed through a synthetic census rather than a real request, so a code registered with correct text but reachable from no path passes. **This is the same declared-but-unexercised family `5g3-sm-impl` spent its packet on, one layer up.** Pairs with `U6b`, which owes real-path observations of the success and empty renderers. |
| surface-command-order | **Command bars render Cancel before Release** | Behavioral | **CLOSED 2026-08-20 by accepted `inventory-surface-legibility`, merge `f2096b7`, under [ADR-0056](../decisions/ADR-0056-current-document-command-order-is-a-bounded-presentation-rule.md).** The renderer applies one closed current-pair precedence—Release first, unknown verbs stable, Cancel last—to a copy of the already-admitted commands. The browser observes `Release, Cancel` and proves each control still dispatches its own operation; the integration binding remains `Cancel, Release`. Removing the presentation sort red the exact order assertion. The distributor release has no two-command surface at this base, so that limitation remains explicit. Full matrix green at `58e5ac1`. |
| verification-short-text-nondeterministic | **A text field shorter than about eight characters makes release verification non-deterministic** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-22, found by `PUR-1` and reproduced by `test:postgres`, not predicted.** `verificationFieldValue` (`packages/postgres-provider/src/release-verification-service.ts`, re-locate by symbol) seeds every text field with `V-` plus a sha256 hex digest **truncated to `maximumLength`**, and the search predicate is a SUBSTRING match (`LIKE '%' || value || '%'`). So a short field's generated value is a PREFIX of every sibling text value on the same record, and the `searchableExclusion` scenario fails whenever a searchable sibling's digest happens to share that prefix -- **about one run in eight at `maximumLength: 3` with two searchable siblings, and every run at length 2.** Observed as `VERIFICATION_SEARCH_EXCLUSION_FAILED: excluded field value was searchable: northstar.app:field.purchase_order_currency`. **It has never fired because the application's shortest text field was 32 characters until `PUR-1` authored a 3-character currency code.** `PUR-1` did not work around it -- `currency` is now searchable for reasons that stand on the field's own nature -- and the underlying generator is untouched. **This is a FLAKY gate, which is worse than a deterministic one:** a packet that authors a short text field will see an intermittent red in `test:postgres` with no obvious cause. Owed: make the generated value distinguishable at any bound -- a suffix rather than a prefix would do it, or a per-field salt placed where truncation cannot remove it. `packages/postgres-provider/**` was out of `PUR-1`'s scope, so this is filed rather than fixed. |
| purchasing-requires-inventory | **A release carrying Purchasing must carry Inventory, and nothing says so** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-22, found by `PUR-1` when `test:postgres` failed an unrelated ABI fixture.** `createManagedTable` calls `requiredLegalEntityMaster` for any `entityOwned` table and refuses with `LEGAL_ENTITY_MASTER_TARGET_INVALID: expected one compiled legal-entity master, received 0` when none is compiled -- and `legal_entity` is declared by the **Inventory** module. `PUR-1` classified both purchasing families `entityOwned` per the charter, so **Purchasing is now coupled to Inventory at the STORAGE layer**, not merely at the file layer where `LEGAL_ENTITY_FAMILY_MAP_V1` happens to live. Two fixtures that build "the composed application without Inventory" -- one in `surface-grammar-conformance.test.ts`, one in `module-storage-transition.test.ts` -- now strip Purchasing to stay valid, and **a third will appear.** Before `PUR-1` the without-Inventory composition had no entity-owned entity at all, so the check was never reached. The refusal is correct and fails closed; what is missing is that the coupling is nowhere declared, so each fixture discovers it as a red. Owed: either state the dependency where a reader will meet it, or move the legal-entity master out of the Inventory module -- the second is the same question the charter fenced when it forbade moving the family map, and it should be answered once rather than per module. |
| PUR-2a | **The posting-family mechanism, proven on stock count** | Critical | **EVIDENCE READY WITH ONE BLOCKED GATE, on `packet/pur-2a`. Record: [packets/pur-2a.md](packets/pur-2a.md); [ADR-0060](../decisions/ADR-0060-a-posting-family-declares-whether-the-kernel-writes-its-companion.md).** **The risk section 7.16 named as the real one did NOT materialise: the companion identities ARE derivable from the stock-count source alone.** `deriveInventoryPostingCompanionId` is a pure UUIDv8-over-SHA-256 of `namespace | capabilityId | familyId | companionFamilyId | sourceRecordId`, exported so a reconciliation reader can recompute it without the posting kernel. **Section 7.16's acceptance control passes**: a reviewed stock count is created with zero `inventory_transaction` and zero `inventory_transaction_line` rows in the tenant, and posting derives and writes both companion IDs and both revisions inside the posting transaction. Posting now dispatches through one compiled family-execution binding keyed by `(capabilityId, familyId)`; the three `switch (postingRole)` statements and the `'stockCount'` literal are gone. **DECLARED LIMIT, and it is deliberately NOT the refuted ADR-0049 claim in new words:** what is frozen in the provider is the ROSTER; what is compiled and release-verified is everything each family is MADE OF. Section 1.1's second half — proving the roster itself is the exact active-release artifact — is still OWED, and moving it into the contract release needs an `InventoryPostingRegistrationV1` change with fourteen construction sites. **TWO STOPS, which is the cap, and BOTH are artifacts pinned outside the lease that an in-lease change necessarily moves — neither is a defect in the packet's production logic.** **Stop 1, corrected after measurement:** the release-lineage refusal reds `check:app-release` AND TWO tests in `test:postgres`'s `composed-application.test.ts`, which both shell out to the same compile script — so it lands on the gate the charter made REQUIRED here, not only on a release artifact. An earlier draft of this row said `check:app-release` alone and was wrong. Relaxing requiredness on an ALREADY-RELEASED relation is a storage transition v1 cannot plan — `sameRelationShape` includes the relation column's nullability, so `COMPILER_STORAGE_RELATION_MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` refuses the composed app's 14-release lineage from advancing. **A fresh compile is unaffected**, which is why the acceptance control is green and `test:compiler` passes 83/83 including the test that pins this very rule. NOT NULL to NULL is a widening DDL that is always safe, so this is a missing planner element, not an unsafe change — routed to `relation-requiredness-relaxation-unplannable`. **`--truncate-invalid-lineage` was NOT used**: it repairs a lineage a compiler change invalidated, and using it here would discard release history to route around a correct diagnostic. **Stop 2:** one stale integer in `test/architecture/module-press-law.test.ts:460` — see `press-law-splice-control-pinned-by-line-number`. **GATES AT `499aab1`:** expected-red 8/8 reproduced and restored, expected-red-controls OK, boundaries OK, unit 155/155, compiler 152/152, integration 149/149, architecture 177 pass 1 fail, postgres 210 pass 2 fail. **Every test this packet added or changed passes, all four stock-count tests included.** |
| relation-requiredness-relaxation-unplannable | **The v1 storage planner cannot relax an already-released relation from required to optional** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-23, found by `PUR-2a` through construction rather than prediction.** `sameRelationShape` (`packages/compiler/src/storage.ts`, re-locate by symbol) includes `relationColumn` — and therefore `nullable` — in the compared physical shape, so a candidate release that widens a relation from required to optional is refused with `COMPILER_STORAGE_RELATION_MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` whenever the previous release already carries that relation. **NOT NULL to NULL is a widening DDL that is always safe**, so the refusal is a missing transition element rather than a guard against an unsafe change, and the accepted alternative the diagnostic offers — *"add a distinct optional relation through the v1 additive path"* — means a second column for the same fact. **Concretely blocking today:** `apps/web/release/app.compiled.json` cannot advance past `PUR-2a`'s two optional companion relations, so `check:app-release` is red on `packet/pur-2a` while every other gate the packet can reach is green. Owed: a requiredness-relaxation transition element in `packages/compiler/src/storage.ts` plus its DDL in `packages/postgres-provider/src/module-storage-materializer.ts`. **Its own charter, not a bridge** — a wrongly-planned DDL against a live tenant is a Band A failure, and both files are Critical. |
| stock-count-companion-released-contract-transition | **`PUR-2a` changes an already-released posting contract and there is no transition** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-24, found by `PUR-2a`'s first review arm and verified by the lane.** The base `InventoryStockCountPostingCommandV1` required a caller-authored `transactionId` and per-line `transactionLineId`, and both companion relations were REQUIRED, so a reviewed stock count necessarily carried caller-selected companions. `PUR-2a` makes a reviewed count's companion columns null and a posted count's derived. Relaxing `NOT NULL` clears no existing value and re-keys no existing row, so against already-released data: an existing reviewed count is refused by the must-be-null fence; an existing posted count whose caller-chosen ids differ from the derivation fails the projection read-back; an old caller cannot resend the old shape because `exactKeys` refuses it; and an existing version-3 receipt recomputes from derived ids and replays as an idempotency conflict. **The last one is a RULE VIOLATION, not a judgement call** — migration `0016` says writers *"retain the v1 default until their own digest input changes under a versioned migration"*, and the digest input changed. **`PUR-2a` argued the exemption on the grounds that the digest SHAPE was unchanged, which is a distinction the rule does not make; that argument is withdrawn.** Owed, and none of it is a lane's to rule: a versioned public command and capability version; a digest version with its `CHECK` migration; dispositions for legacy reviewed rows and their pre-staged drafts, and for legacy posted rows, movements and receipts; and either an explicit proof that no live data exists or a transition that carries it. **This is why §7.16's *"a code change, not a ruling"* is refuted by construction.** **MEASURED COST, taken from a real attempt rather than estimated: `PUR-2a` built migration `0022` plus a version-4 write, and it broke ELEVEN tests across SEVEN files** — `db/schema.snapshot.json`, four independently pinned tail-migration assertions (`migrations.test.ts`, `trust-substrate.test.ts` x2, `inventory-storage.test.ts` x2), two exhaustive migration lists (`release-persistence-boundary.test.ts`, `module-storage-transition.test.ts`), and a migration range encoded in a test's own TITLE. **There is no single registry, so each site surfaces as a separate red.** The work was withdrawn rather than finished in place, because a change with that surface ships without controls of its own when buried in a posting packet. `PUR-2b` starts with this list written in. |
| migration-addition-has-no-registry | **Adding one migration file requires updating at least eight hand-pinned sites, discovered one red at a time** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-24, measured by `PUR-2a` when it added migration `0022`.** Four suites independently pin the tail migration filename, two hold exhaustive ordered lists of every migration, one encodes a migration RANGE in its test title, and `db/schema.snapshot.json` must be regenerated with no write path — `test/helpers/check-schema.ts` only compares. Nothing enumerates these sites, so a packet adding a migration finds them by running the suite and reading reds serially; `PUR-2a` hit them across three separate runs. **This is the `gate-reads-a-different-thing-than-its-name` shape inverted**: the gates are right, but their subject is copied into eight places instead of derived from one. Owed: a single derived source for the migration inventory, or at minimum a documented checklist so the surface is known before the bridge is taken rather than after. |
| companion-writers-not-closed | **Generic writers can still create the companion the posting kernel claims to own** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-24, found by `PUR-2a`'s first review arm.** `PUR-2a` writes both stock-count companion identities inside the posting transaction and its reviewed-must-be-null fence refuses a source that already names one. **That is refusal, not sole-writer enforcement.** Generic O0 operations still admit draft `inventory_transaction` create and update, reviewed stock counts remain generally mutable, and `stock_count_form` still renders the Transaction picker. Something else can therefore create a transaction under the derived identity, or write the source relation, before posting. **ADR-0049 explicitly required user-reachable companion creation, update and listing to be CLOSED and recorded those conditions as owed; they still are.** `PUR-2a` corrected its own ADR and controls to claim only non-adoption. Owed: close or explicitly constrain the generic writers before any packet claims the kernel owns the companion. Subsumes `stock-count-form-offers-a-dead-transaction-picker`, which is the operator-facing half of the same gap. |
| movement-effect-reservation-not-observed | **The only constraint that reserves a natural inventory effect lives in a table the posting service never binds or reads** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-26, found by `PUR-2a`'s ninth review arm and verified by the lane against its own tree.** Every movement insert fires `north_star_internal.reserve_inventory_movement_effect`, which copies the movement's columns into `movement.factStorage.companion` — a table the compiler declares and the materializer creates under `north_star_module`. `inventory-posting-service.ts` reads `factStorage` three times, for `mutability` and `businessPeriod.column`; there is no companion binding and no read-back. **The keys are the finding.** The movement table's own `effectIdentityUnique` contains `record_id`, freshly minted per posting, so it CANNOT collide between two postings — it is not a duplicate-effect guard, and it is shaped that way because a partitioned table's unique constraint must carry its partition key `(tenant_id, business_period)`. The companion's PRIMARY KEY omits both `business_period` and `record_id`, so it is **the only thing in the system that reserves a natural effect**. The foreign key runs companion → movement `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so a movement with no reservation row violates nothing. **Consequence:** the `23505` raced-replay branch in `#post`, whose refusal reads *"a natural effect identity was claimed by a different posting"*, depends entirely on that table — and through nine rounds `PUR-2a` and ADR-0060 described the simultaneous-post case as *"reasoned, not measured"* while **the reasoning named the wrong constraint**. A retry on ONE source collides on the derived companion transaction identity, which is what the ADR argues and is true; two DIFFERENT sources carrying one natural effect tuple collide only on the fact companion's primary key. Owed: bind the companion from the compiled target; read the reservation rows back one-to-one against the persisted movements before any trust document or receipt is written; a control that disables the reservation trigger and proves the posting refuses and commits nothing; expected-red for a wrong reservation writer AND for removing the verifier; and the simultaneous-post measurement itself — two documents, one natural effect tuple, different stock identities, distinct idempotency keys, exactly one accepted. **Root cause, and why `PUR-2a` was re-scoped rather than given a tenth round:** the writer inventory is hand-enumerated while the compiled storage target already declares every writer. Round 7's rule that coverage must not be derived from the compiled binding is right for column VALUES inside a row and wrong for WHICH TABLES a posting writes.  **ADDRESSED 2026-08-26 by `posting-writer-inventory`, and the fix is the DERIVATION rather than the reservation.** The service now derives from the compiled target every physical relation a posting writes — each written entity's table, its declared partitions, its declared `factStorage.companion`, and any projection whose insert trigger the target declares on a table it writes — and REFUSES AT CONSTRUCTION when a derived relation carries no registered read-back ([ADR-0062](../decisions/ADR-0062-the-writer-inventory-is-derived-from-the-compiled-target.md)). The reservation is bound from the target and read back one-to-one. **Two things this row got half-right, corrected by measurement.** First, `effectIdentityUnique` carries `record_id` because it is the companion foreign key's TARGET, not only because of the partition key. Second, the FK covers ALL TEN companion columns, so a wrong-effect reservation is refused by the database with 23503 and is not reachable — what no constraint holds, and what the read-back actually adds, is the reservation's EXISTENCE and its one-to-one pairing. The simultaneous-post case is now MEASURED with two concurrent postings sharing neither lock. |
| module-writer-edges-are-convention-not-declaration | **No artifact declares which relation a trigger writes FROM and TO, so both the materializer and the posting kernel reconstruct the same convention independently** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-26, found by `posting-writer-inventory`'s first review arm (F1) and verified by the lane against its own tree.** The compiled target declares three of the four writer expansions honestly — an entity's table, its `factStorage.partitioning.partitions[]`, and its `factStorage.companion.reservationTriggerName`, which names the trigger and the companion it writes on the SOURCE entity's own fact storage. **It declares nothing at all about the posted-stock balance edge.** The materializer finds the movement and balance entities by entity-id suffix, computes the trigger name from the balance's table name, and installs it on the movement; `inventory-posting-service.ts` reconstructs the same pairing in a hand-written two-edge list. **Two implementations of one convention, and the reviewer's phrasing is the right one: "both are pure functions of the target" does not make the edge target-declared.** Consequence: a sixth materializer-installed writer on a new convention changes no target shape the service reads and appears in no list, which is the omission generator `PUR-2a`'s nine rounds were, one level up. **Owed:** one authoritative artifact carrying source relation, trigger identity or kind, destination relation, and PLANE, consumed by BOTH materialization and posting; then a transitive closure derived from the kernel roots over that artifact. Band-A control: `movement -> balance -> audit` with the audit verifier omitted, proving construction refuses. **Why it is filed rather than fixed:** it requires `packages/compiler/src/storage.ts` and `packages/postgres-provider/src/module-storage-materializer.ts`, both held by `relation-requiredness-relaxation` and both fenced out of `posting-writer-inventory` by charter. **The reviewer's scope ruling stands and is directed at the orchestrator: that fence is not compatible with closing ADR-0062, so this needs a bridge or its own serialized packet.** ADR-0062 now carries the correction in-line and must not be read as claiming edge completeness. |
| posting-writer-coverage-is-declared-not-observed | **The writer-inventory gate proves a verifier EXISTS for each relation, never that it RAN** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-26, found by `posting-writer-inventory`'s first review arm (F2).** The construction check compares a derived relation set against a registered relation set. It never resolves, invokes, or observes the verifier. **The original registrations carried arbitrary strings and one of them named `assertDraftTransaction`, a function that does not exist anywhere in the file** — shipped, and accepted by construction, which is what proved the strings decorative. Registrations now pass the verifier FUNCTION and read the id off the function object, so naming an absent verifier is a compile error; **that closes half of it.** What remains open is the Band-A "proxy satisfied while the fact does not hold" vector: a relation whose registered verifier is real but never executes on the path a given posting takes is still accepted. `AGENTS.md` §6 — *a gate must OBSERVE the fact it asserts, never a proxy; inferring from a declaration is a proxy.* **Owed:** per-posting runtime coverage — each read-back records the relations it actually observed, and before commit the posting compares that set against the relations this transaction actually wrote. **The strong form is measurable rather than declared:** every module-schema relation carrying a row with `xmin = txid_current()` was written by this transaction, so scanning them and requiring each to have been observed would close BOTH this row and the edge row above, without any compiler change. **It is a design change beyond that packet's charter and beyond one lane's judgement — cost per posting is the open question — so it is filed for a ruling rather than taken.** |
| posting-platform-plane-writes-not-row-complete | **A successful posting writes platform-plane rows no read-back observes column-for-column** | Critical | **OPEN 2026-08-25, found by `PUR-2a`'s eighth review arm while closing the module-plane class.** `PUR-2a` now proves every MODULE row a posting writes — companion header, companion lines, movement, authored header, source header, source lines, and the trigger-written posted-stock balance — is compared column-for-column against an executed proof before the posting commits, and refuses on any column no proof covers. **The platform plane is not covered.** A successful post also writes four trust and evidence documents, a semantic-operation receipt (and a second one on natural replay), and advances the semantic aggregate generation through the movement table's other `AFTER INSERT` trigger. None of those rows is read back and compared; each is trusted on its own statement's reported row count, which is the same 'a mutating statement reporting on itself' argument `PUR-2a` rejected for the rows it does own. **Why it is filed rather than fixed:** `persistAcceptedEvidence`, `insertReceipt` and the aggregate-generation trigger belong to the platform contract, not to the inventory posting capability, and none of the owning files is in `PUR-2a`'s leases. The risk is the same class the packet declares Band A for — a wrong-but-valid trust document commits on a successful posting and is discovered, if at all, by something recomputing it later. Owed: decide whether the platform plane carries its own row-complete contract or adopts this one, then hold it with controls of the same shape (`a-verifier-stops-comparing-a-column`, `a-read-back-observes-a-partial-row`). Until then `PUR-2a`'s closure claim is scoped to the module plane in its own record and in its review prompt, and must not be restated as 'every write is read back'. |

| matrix-preflight | **The matrix spends 16 minutes to learn the machine was busy** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-08, raised by `U5b`'s two indeterminate runs.** The compile-budget gate refuses to measure below a 90% CPU-idle floor and returns `COMPILE_BUDGET_INDETERMINATE` — correct, and **the floor must not move**: a measurement taken under load measures the load, and widening it converts a refused measurement into a false green. **The defect is where the check happens, not what it checks.** Idle is observed at the exclusive gate, near the end of the run, so a loaded machine costs a full matrix to discover. Two attempts landed at 65.3% and 87.3% idle. **The deeper gap: `lock-obs` leases TEST SUITES; nothing leases AGENT RUNTIMES.** `lanes.md` records the runtime sitting at ~12% CPU sustained while working, and three or four concurrent sessions on a WSL cap of 8 processors put idle in the mid-to-high 80s — structurally just under the floor. So the exclusive-machine requirement exists only in someone remembering it, which is the AGENTS.md §6 shape. **Cheapest honest fix: a pre-flight at matrix START that reads the same idle figure and refuses immediately with the same diagnostic**, so the cost of a busy machine is one second rather than sixteen minutes. Do not widen the floor, do not retry into it, and do not make the pre-flight advisory — a warning that does not stop the run buys nothing. |

| provider-query-refusal-taxonomy | **Query-side provider refusals with stable identity still render as generic data unavailability** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-20, routed from the independent `web-refusal-taxonomy` confirmation rather than expanded into that packet.** The semantic query gateway rethrows provider errors intact, but `queryMessageCode` maps only the closed gateway error-name table and therefore collapses a `ModuleRuntimeInterpreterError` such as `MODULE_LEGAL_ENTITY_READ_SCOPE_REQUIRED` to `QUERY_UNAVAILABLE` / `Data unavailable`. The web packet deliberately rules only operation refusals and the typed runtime-view boundary. This row owns the query-side truthfulness decision and its complete observable; coordinate with `provider-operation-refusal-contract` rather than re-deriving the frozen 61-code operation census. |
| runtime-integrity-attribution-untwinned | **Two of the five integrity checks that must precede the capability refusal have no behavioural twin** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-14, routed out of `profile-v2-adoption` on its confirm arm rather than worked, under `review-tiers`' rule that a claim outrunning its specimens for two rounds is NARROWED rather than grown.** `request-runtime-view-service.ts` verifies five things on opaque bytes before comparing the capability floor: chunk-link cardinality, artifact presence, `verifyArtifact` (domain/hash), descriptor match (byte length/version/media type/scope), and the semantic digest. **Two committed twins exist** — a deleted chunk link and a corrupted chunk blob — and together they kill any gate placed before artifact verification. **The last two checks have none.** **THE SURVIVING BROKEN TREE, named rather than implied:** a gate placed after `verifyArtifact` but before the descriptor and digest checks keeps all four current controls green while misreporting a descriptor or semantic-digest mismatch as an under-supported reader — which is precisely the misattribution `UNSUPPORTED_RUNTIME_CAPABILITY` was separated from `MALFORMED_*` to prevent. **A PRIOR CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN: the packet said these specimens "cannot be seeded at all" because admission recomputes the semantic digest. That premise is true of NORMAL admission and false as a conclusion** — the suite does not use normal admission for these specimens; it admits a valid release and then disables the immutable-storage rules, which is how both existing twins work. Both missing specimens are constructible the same way: rebuild the projection-manifest artifact with a changed `byteLength` (or a different valid 64-hex `semanticDigest`) on both manifest and reference, rebuild the manifest hash and release root, and repoint the stored links. `rewriteProjectionPayload` already implements most of that plumbing. **They are uncovered because they were not built, not because they are impossible, and the difference is the difference between a limit and an excuse.** **Also owed here:** a genuinely single-fault content-hash specimen — the committed one breaks three invariants (hash, JSON validity, semantic digest) and is valid only because `verifyArtifact` deterministically fires first. |
| runtime-capability-floor-unenforced | **The surface manifest declares a reader capability floor that nothing ever checks** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-14, found by `profile-v2-adoption` while correcting an assertion the adoption moved. Filed, not fixed.** `surfaceManifestPayload` emits `requiredRuntimeCapability.minimumVersion = emitsFieldKinds ? 3 : navigation ? 2 : 1` (`packages/compiler/src/projections.ts`, re-locate by symbol), and profile v2 adoption moved it from **1 to 3 on every manifest**, flat and grouped alike. The field-kind packet allocated 3 deliberately and wrote down why: a reader that drops `fields` renders bare text boxes, and the argument that this degrades safely is a claim about what a BROWSER submits, which two attempts failed to gate. Its comment in `component-registry.ts` states the intended contract plainly -- *"a reader without field support must refuse it rather than fall back"*. **Nothing implements that refusal.** The only comparison of `minimumVersion` anywhere in the tree is `compiler.ts`'s dependency-validity check (safe integer, `>= 1`); `release-repository.ts` persists the key and no consumer compares the manifest's floor against what it supports. **CORRECTED 2026-08-14 BY THE REVIEW ARM, and the correction makes this worse than it was filed.** This said `request-runtime-view-service.ts` *passes it through*. **It does not pass it through -- the floor is DROPPED.** `RuntimeProjection` (`packages/runtime/src/request-runtime-view.ts`) declares exactly six members -- `artifactRoot`, `familyId`, `instanceId`, `payload`, `payloadSchemaVersion`, `semanticDigest` -- and `requiredRuntimeCapability` is not among them. The provider's single use of the field sits inside a `canonicalize` comparison asserting that the MANIFEST and its REFERENCE agree with each other: a self-consistency check, not a support check. Verified independently: `minimumVersion` appears nowhere in `packages/postgres-provider/src`, `packages/runtime/src` or `apps/web/src` except one comment in `component-registry.ts`. So the floor is erased from the runtime representation BEFORE any consumer could enforce it, and `readCompiledSurfaceManifest` never gets the chance. ADR-0041's accepted-and-ignored state, reached by omission rather than coercion. **The cheapest decisive counterexample, from the review:** an otherwise valid manifest/reference pair whose `minimumVersion` is `999`, hashes regenerated consistently and payload schema unchanged -- the provider's self-consistency check accepts it, the runtime projection drops the requirement, and the web reader serves the payload with no refusal anywhere. So the number is a declaration, not a gate, and the safety its authors documented does not exist. **Second consequence, cheap to miss:** all three arms of `surface-grammar-conformance.test.ts` now assert `3`, where they previously discriminated flat (1) from grouped (2), so that assertion no longer distinguishes the two and a regression that stopped bumping for `navigation` would keep them green. The grouping behaviour is still gated by `payloadSchemaVersion` and `navigationSurfaceIds`, so nothing is left unguarded, but the weakening is real and is recorded at the call site. **Owed:** either a consumer that refuses a manifest above its supported floor by name, or an explicit ruling that the floor is advisory -- ADR-0041's honour-or-refuse says it cannot stay both. |
| demo-shell-profile-untracked | **"The demo shell stays on v0-experimental" is true of the language axis only** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-14, found by `profile-v2-adoption` when `check:demo-release` went red on a packet that never intended to touch the shell.** `lanes.md`, the packet prompt and the standing orchestrator instruction all say the demo shell pair stays on `v0-experimental` and must not be moved. That protects the LANGUAGE axis and gives no protection at all on the profile axis: `apps/web/scripts/compile-demo-release.ts` spreads `DEFAULT_COMPILER_PROFILE` and overrides `languageVersion` and `normalizationProfileVersion` from the fixture, but **not `compilerSemanticProfileVersion`**, so the shell's semantic profile silently tracks whatever constant is adopted. Regenerating was correct here and ADR-0047's Consequences already prescribed it -- the shell is one string-compared fixture with no lineage to invalidate -- and the regeneration was explicable: `normalizedDefinitionDigest`, `compilerVersion`, `outputProtocolVersion`, `schemaVersion` and four of five projections all HELD, `shell.authored.json` was untouched, and only `projections.surface` and `releaseRoot` moved. **The row exists because the guidance is misleading, not because the outcome was wrong.** A future lane told "do not move the demo shell" will believe the shell is insulated from a profile adoption, and it is not. **Owed:** either derive the shell's semantic profile per artifact as the language axis already is, or correct the standing wording everywhere it appears to say *language* rather than *profile*. **SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME SCRIPT, found the same way:** `compile-demo-release.ts` serializes with `JSON.stringify(payload, null, 2)` and **does not import prettier at all**, while its sibling `compile-app-release.ts` writes through `format(..., {parser:'json'})`. So `build:demo-release` emits a file that `pnpm format` immediately rewrites, and a lane that regenerates and commits without running `format:write` leaves the tree **format-red** -- which is `unrun-quality-gates`' subject, reached from a different direction. The two gates do not contradict each other: `--check` compares `JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(existing))` against the payload, so it is semantic and indifferent to whitespace, and the committed form on `main` is prettier's because someone formatted after generating. **Nothing makes the generator's own output the committed form**, and nobody would learn that from either gate. Cheap fix: import prettier in `compile-demo-release.ts` exactly as `compile-app-release.ts` already does. |
| adoption-selector-seam | **Five syntax repairs to one control produced five escapes; the newest-readable rule needs a seam, not a sixth rule** | Critical | **DOES NOT GATE `PUR-1` — ruled 2026-08-11.** The seam decides whether the default profile is built by *take the adopted version* or *take the newest readable one*. Those two rules **cannot disagree today**: `ADOPTED_LANGUAGE_VERSION` and `LATEST_LANGUAGE_VERSION` are both `v5`, and `PUR-1` authors a module at the adopted version rather than cutting a new one. **This row gates the NEXT version cut, which is the first moment the wrong rule becomes observable in production** — and it is Critical for exactly that reason, not because purchasing waits on it. **OPEN 2026-08-10, routed out of `LANG-ADOPT-v5` at a round-4 BLOCK on the repository's own convergence rule.** `canonical-contracts-purity`'s scan was repaired four times — a strip that deleted its subject, a rename that changed its subject's name, a glob that never reached it, an exemption that swallowed it — and each repair was followed by a CHEAPER escape than the one before. **The fifth needs no obfuscation at all:** `profile.languageVersion === SUPPORTED_LANGUAGE_VERSIONS.at(-1)!` selects the newest readable version using an identifier production already imports. Measured on the real `DEFAULT_COMPILER_PROFILE`: the profile still resolved to v5, every behavioural assertion held, and the scan stayed green. **A sixth token rule buys a sixth escape**, which is why `LANG-ADOPT-v5` narrowed its claim instead of patching again — that control now asserts only what a source-reference scan can prove, and says so in its title. **What this row owes:** a production SELECTOR SEAM that takes the supported profiles and the adopted members as PARAMETERS and returns the complete default profile; a constructed control exercising it on a readable set whose adopted member is deliberately NOT its last, where \"take the adopted one\" and \"take the newest\" disagree and the wrong rule is observable; a binding control proving `DEFAULT_COMPILER_PROFILE` is produced THROUGH that seam; and the `.at(-1)!` mutation as its recorded red — **and a second owed red, escape 6**, which names the constant and launders it through an intermediate binding: `const selected = \`${LATEST_LANGUAGE_VERSION}:selected\`` then `languageVersion: selected.slice(0, -9)`. Measured: `MODULE_COMPILER_PROFILE` resolved to v5 and the scan reported the tree clean. **`LANG-ADOPT-v5` then RETIRED that scan as semantic evidence** rather than adding a seventh destination name — it now asserts only the syntactic fact that such references sit inside literal-bearing templates, and says in its own header what it does not establish. So the newest-readable rule currently has NO semantic gate; this row owns it. `selectAdoptedProfileVersion` is the half that already exists and already carries this reasoning in its own doc comment — the gap is that nothing proves the exported default is built by it. **The transferable lesson, and it is this program's own:** a scan over source text classifies SPELLINGS; only a seam that takes the choice as a parameter can observe the CHOICE. Five rounds of evidence say the first cannot be patched into the second. **WORSENED 2026-08-14 by `profile-v2-adoption`, measured and reported by that lane against itself.** This row said the two rules "cannot disagree today" because `ADOPTED_LANGUAGE_VERSION` and `LATEST_LANGUAGE_VERSION` are both `v5`. That was true of the LANGUAGE axis and **false of the compiler-semantic axis, which was the one place left in the tree where they could disagree**: readable was `[v0, v1, v2]` while adoption sat on `v1`, so substituting `SUPPORTED_COMPILER_SEMANTIC_PROFILE_VERSIONS.at(-1)` for the adopted constant moved `DEFAULT_COMPILER_PROFILE` from v1 to v2 and would have moved every recorded release root -- a loud red, available for free. **Adoption consumed it.** Measured at `f664a8f`: compiler-semantic is `[v0, v1, v2]` with **v2** adopted, language is `[v0-experimental, v1..v5]` with **v5** adopted, so **both axes now have adopted == last-readable and this row's own owed red -- the `.at(-1)!` mutation -- cannot be made to fire against production anywhere in the tree.** The row is unchanged in substance and strictly harder to discharge: it now needs a CONSTRUCTED seam exercise, because no live axis can distinguish the rules. Recorded at the call site in `compiler-semantic-profile.test.ts`. |
| version-predicate-derivation | **Two feature-level predicates still enumerate their versions by hand; both are now OBSERVED, and only the conversion itself remains open** | Behavioral | **OPEN 2026-08-09, routed out of `LANG-ADOPT-v5`'s sixth-omission sweep, and RE-WRITTEN TWICE.** First from the packet's own deletion table, which refuted the original draft's claim that neither site could be observed going stale. Then again at **review round 8, which found this row still routing future work from an absence the packet had already closed** — it told the next packet that one predicate was "not observed at all" and that the other's cumulative behaviour could not be witnessed without a production signature change, and both statements were false in the very tree the row was written from. That is the packet's own named finding one layer out: **a record can outrun its controls exactly the way a hand-written version outruns its cut.** `languageHasV2Features` (`packages/canonical-model/src/normalize.ts:2229`) and `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape` (`packages/compiler/src/compiler.ts:1227`) both ask `featureLevel === 'v2'/'v3'/'v4'/'v5'` — row 1 of `5g3-sm-impl`'s finding table, where that shape silently dropped a released v2 rule from a v5 package. **Every number below was RE-MEASURED on the reviewed tree at `2ed5595`, each mutation applied alone to a clean tree and reverted before the next.** **(a) `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape` now has a discriminating MEMBERSHIP witness — and that is not the same as a behavioural one.** Dropping `'v5'` from its list reds **1 test**, `normalization.test.ts:217`, by name. The same mutation before the packet's control was **green across the ENTIRE 589-assertion matrix, byte-identical to the unmutated run**. So the row's earlier "needs a control before it needs a refactor" is DISCHARGED; the control exists and it is the only thing standing between this predicate and a silent v6 omission. **What survives is narrower and should not be confused with it:** the three things the predicate actually gates — required reporting family, the reporting-entity invariant, and validation of lowered physical mappings — are still unwitnessed by behaviour, because each is a self-check the compiler satisfies by construction and the faults they catch cannot be produced from authored input — but the two halves of that are **not equally strong, and the earlier wording overstated one of them.** `COMPILER_PHYSICAL_NAME_TOO_LONG` is structurally unreachable: physical names are a fixed prefix plus a base32 SHA-256 digest, always under PostgreSQL's 63-byte limit. `COMPILER_PHYSICAL_NAME_COLLISION` is *infeasible*, not impossible — reaching it means finding a SHA-256 collision under the domain-separated naming construction, so the guarantee rests on the project's collision-resistance assumption rather than on arithmetic. A disabled safety net is invisible unless you also introduce the fault it catches. **Widening the sample will not fix that** — `test:integration` and `test:postgres` were still not run against the mutation, but the obstacle is constructibility, not coverage. **(b) `languageHasMaterializedStateFields`'s successor arm is OBSERVABLE, and this row's standing "do not convert" instruction is WITHDRAWN.** That predicate already uses the ordered-list form (`candidate >= introducedAt`) a conversion would copy. Narrowing it back to `candidate === introducedAt` — precisely the defect `5g3-sm-impl` fixed — now reds **1 test**, the successor arm, where before the packet's harness it red **nothing** across `test:unit` and the full `test:compiler`. It is observable because the packet stopped requiring the predicate to accept its list as an argument: a committed test-only harness supplies the exported body's **free bindings** and runs **the real emitted body** against a synthetic supported list `[v4, v5, v6]`. **Disclosed limitation:** the harness reconstructs the export through `toString()`, so it is guarded by a faithfulness loop that re-runs it under the live bindings and compares against the export at every supported version. Deletion table: a behaviourally equivalent rewrite correctly does NOT red (the control measures behaviour, not spelling), and a body that gains a binding the harness does not supply reds loudly (`STATE_FIELD_INTRODUCED_AT is not defined`). **A conversion is therefore now provable, and that is the change from the earlier disposition** — the next packet does not have to alter a production signature first. **(c) The surviving next-cut debt belongs to ONE of the two predicates, not both — an earlier draft of this row said both, and that was measured false.** `languageHasV2Features` is well covered for the ADOPTED version — dropping `'v5'` reds **46 tests** (9 in `test:unit`, 37 in `test:compiler`; the earlier "13" in this row was the same mutation read against a narrower sample, `test:unit` plus the adopted-language fixture alone, and is superseded). But coverage of the ADOPTED version is not coverage of the NEXT cut, and that is where the two part company. **Measured by simulating the cut** — v6 appended to `LANGUAGE_VERSIONS`, `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGE_VERSIONS` and both supported-list ratchets, with BOTH predicate enumerations deliberately left un-updated: `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape`'s omission **reds by name** in `test:unit` (*"v6 must carry the module projection shape"*), because its control does not spot-check the adopted version — it derives the introduction boundary and walks the whole supported list, so an appended successor inherits the assertion. `languageHasV2Features`'s omission **reds nothing** across `test:unit` and the full `test:compiler` (137/137 green). *Two further unit reds in that run are artifacts of a deliberately incomplete simulation — `LATEST_LANGUAGE_VERSION` was left at v5, which degrades `negative-contracts`' derived one-past-newest probe into a mixed-node probe, exactly as that helper's own comment warns. Neither is a v2-features red.* **So: `languageHasV2Features` carries the unobserved next-cut hole — this is how row 1 of `5g3-sm-impl` happened, and nothing yet stops it happening again. `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape` does not; its next cut is already guarded.** **Fix: convert both enumerations to the ordered `candidate >= introducedAt` form** — but the two conversions buy different things. For `languageHasV2Features` it closes a live behavioural hole and is the reason this row stays open. For `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape` it is derivation and maintenance cleanup only; **do not sell it as repairing an unobserved branch, and do not delete the membership witness while converting** — that witness is the only thing observing the predicate at all. **Residue, deliberately not fixed here:** the overstated collision wording above also appears in two source comments (`packages/compiler/src/compiler.ts` above `languageUsesModuleProjectionShape`, and `test/unit/canonical-model/normalization.test.ts` above the membership witness). Correcting them would put an executable-tree edit above the governing matrix SHA for a comment, so it is left to whichever packet next touches those files. |
| unrun-quality-gates | **`pnpm lint` and `pnpm format` are both red on `main`, and no gate runs either** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-09, found by `LANG-ADOPT-v5` when a repo-wide `format:write` rewrote eight files it does not own.** Measured on `main` at `5aa2d2c`: `pnpm lint` reports **7 errors** (`apps/web/src/surface-contract.ts` unused `transitionEffect`; four unused imports in `packages/canonical-model/src/predicate-kernel.ts`; a `no-fallthrough` in `packages/postgres-provider/src/module-runtime-interpreter.ts`; an explicit `any` in `test/postgres/module-runtime.test.ts`) and `prettier --check` reports drift in at least three files. **Neither script is in the `test` matrix command**, so AGENTS.md §6's *full CI matrix* has never observed them and the drift accumulated silently. **Two consequences already paid for:** a lane running `format:write` to format its OWN files rewrites files under other lanes' leases, which happened twice in `LANG-ADOPT-v5` and had to be reverted by hand both times; and a real lint regression is indistinguishable from the standing seven. **Fix: burn the seven down, then add `lint` and `format` to the matrix command** — in that order, because adding them first makes every lane's matrix red for reasons it did not cause. Until then, lanes must format with a file-scoped `npx prettier --write <paths>` and compare `pnpm lint`'s error COUNT against the base rather than expecting zero. |
| suite-inventory-copies | **A suite's test-file list is written by hand in three places, and the control compares only two** | Mechanical | **OPEN 2026-08-09, found by `LANG-ADOPT-v5` when adding one test file failed a full matrix at its second-to-last gate.** The compiler suite's file list lives in `package.json`'s `test:compiler` command, in `test/architecture/repository-hygiene.test.ts`'s reviewed inventory, and in `test/helpers/reachability-producers.ts`'s producer argv. **`repository-hygiene`'s control compares the COMMAND against the discovered glob and never looks at the producer argv**, so updating two of the three is green everywhere until `check:language-coverage` reads the recorded evidence and reports `Observed argv mismatch`. **The cost is the discovery point, not the refusal:** every suite ran green — unit through browser, compile-budget MEASURED at 99.6% idle — and roughly 40 minutes elapsed before a list mismatch surfaced. That is `matrix-preflight`'s complaint in a different gate: a cheap fact learned expensively. **The fix is to make the third copy derived or compared, not to add a fourth reviewer.** `repository-hygiene` already parses the command's tokens; extending the same test to assert `reachabilityProducers` names the identical set is a few lines and moves the failure from minute 40 to minute 1. **Note the shape:** this is the `LANG-ADOPT-v5` sweep's own finding — one value written by hand in more than one place — one layer out from language versions, which is why it is recorded rather than quietly fixed. |

When it arrives it rides `surfaceManifestPayload` at a new payload version gated on `compilerSemanticProfileVersion` — the `navigation` precedent, ruled by ADR-0030 as a compiled surface-manifest concept. **The only U6 piece that touches the projection freeze**, and therefore the only one whose size is conditional on `U5-design`'s `v2` measurement: large if a v1-gated field breaks entry 7's reproduction (a profile cut, a lineage entry, ~7 pinned expectations), medium if it does not.
